A deliberately unparseable example row still ships fabricated text. An opt-in sample adds a feature nobody asked for. Header-only removes the fabricated row where it is made; the column names are the instructions.
- **Tests:** valid rows used as test input now live in test-only helpers: `SAMPLE_ROWS`/`sample_csv` in `packages/sources/tests/imports/conftest.py`, and `TRENDS_CSV`/`CJ_CSV` in `apps/api/tests/test_admin.py`. New regressions check that all four downloads (service and API) are exactly the header, and that an unchanged download writes no observation through the service (all four templates), the real FX and Trends adapters, and the API commit route.
- **Out of scope:** template rows already committed on the collector database (FX `2026-01-01 USD/JPY 150 manual`, Trends "example keyword") were not queried, edited or deleted; removing them is a destructive data operation for the owner. Also out of scope: E-20/E-21, the parser's accepted formats and the import panel copy.

### DEC-116 · CJ provider balance guard and shared-template CSV import
**2026-10-23 · proposed (P1-08d)** · CJ-2; DEC-009/035/095/105/112
- **Points:** retain validated integer `usedToday/remaining/total` and receipt time in existing source JSON, separately from the daily reservation. Before each paid attempt, apply whole-minute total/1440 refill capped at total, using exact integer 1/1440-point units; debit and commit before sending. Drop partial minutes before a debit conservatively and retain uncertain-send debits. Missing/invalid evidence or a prior UTC day is unknown and refuses paid requests; a free auth/category response may establish fresh evidence, with no new bootstrap request. Auth response bodies remain uncaptured. External account spend remains unknowable between responses; existing provider-error blocking and imp-039 are unchanged.
- **CSV:** register the existing CJ template with the shared import service, keeping disabled-source imports and append-only replay. Reuse listing persistence with explicit currency/observation date; group SKU rows by supplied product ID and use namespaced manual identities when no real ID exists. Reuse an existing variant only for one matching listing/SKU. Store aggregate listing ranges, variant unit cost, MOQ and unit-quantity US freight by variant/warehouse; mixed warehouses leave the offer-level country null. The template has no stock column. No migration, dependency, source/seed/flag or scheduler change.
- **Alternatives / why:** assuming a full daily provider balance can overspend; charging only local daily reservations misses starting balances and refill. Waiting/sleeping for points adds orchestration that is unnecessary for this slice; stop and let a later run retry. A separate CSV schema or guessed API IDs would duplicate the shared contract or merge unrelated entities.
- **Split / evidence:** under the task's size allowance, P1-08e retains the account-activity computation and explicit evidence type/tests. No account-wide last-positive-transaction evidence shape is proved by fixtures; no number is shown and token issuance is never transaction evidence. All ten seed mappings remain unresolved and fail closed. Synthetic fixtures verify refusal before send, unknown/invalid balances, capped fractional refill, existing daily limits and CSV dates/variants/freight/replay; public docs only, no data-source API request. PostgreSQL/connected-service behavior remains unverified locally.

### DEC-156 · CJ points evidence across responses and UTC days; CSV variant identity and writer
**2026-10-23 · proposed (P1-48)** · P1-47 C-M1–C-M3; amends DEC-116 and the DEC-120 import caveat; DEC-095/105/112/136
- **Absent versus invalid (C-M1):** a response with no `pointsInfo` key (stock, freight, error bodies, non-JSON pages) leaves the stored evidence as it was, including the local debits already applied. A `pointsInfo` that is present but invalid (null, non-integer, negative, remaining above total) still clears it, so the next paid send is refused.
- **In-run renewal (C-M2), changing DEC-116's "no new bootstrap request":** when a paid send is refused with `cj_points_unknown` (no evidence, or evidence from an earlier UTC day), the transport sends **one** `/product/getCategory` per run with the current token and retries the paid send once. That endpoint is already allowed, is unlisted in CJ's points table, and reserves nothing locally. The retry goes ahead only if that response renews valid current-day evidence; otherwise the refusal stands. Stale evidence is never extrapolated or refilled across days, and no balance is assumed. The renewal counts in `http_requests` and keeps the configured pacing. Its body isn't stored (like auth); a rejected renewal is stored through the existing token-rejection path. A provider error on the renewal is handled like an auth failure, so `1600014` still disables once, and block codes still count.
- **Unchanged:** the local daily cap is checked first, and the provider estimate and per-attempt reservation stay committed before each paid send. Refill stays whole minutes within one UTC day, capped at total. `cj_points_exhausted` and `cj_daily_budget` never trigger a renewal. Retries are not refunded, and provider-error blocking is unchanged.
- **CSV identity (C-M3):** an existing variant is reused only when exactly one variant in the listing has the row's SKU. Otherwise the row gets or creates the unique `csv-sku:<sku>` variant on `(listing_id, external_id)`.
- **CSV writer (DEC-120 caveat):** CJ CSV variant prices use their own `Writer`: captures whose `raw_payloads.request.mode` is `csv`, owning `price_minor`. Change-only compares CSV rows only with earlier CSV rows, so interleaved detail or stock rows no longer make an unchanged price append again. CSV rows are kept out of `VARIANT_WRITERS`, so detail stays the only owner in the default read and a manual price is never current there; read CSV prices explicitly with `writers=(CSV_WRITER,)`. No migration or key change.
- **Alternatives:** extrapolating stale evidence to a full refill (assumes no outside spend overnight); joining CSV to the detail writer (a manual or non-USD price would become the current API price); a renewal on every run (spends a request when evidence is already fresh).
- **Limits / follow-ups:** whether `getCategory` returns `pointsInfo` and is free is unverified live; if it doesn't renew, runs stay refused as before. The renewal only reports the balance: other account clients can still spend after it. CJ listing snapshots now have CSV and API rows on one listing with no writer, so alternating imports and catalog/detail runs append extra (never lost) listing rows, and the latest listing row can come from either; a listing writer split is a follow-up. C-L1 and C-L2 stay with imp-004. Fixtures only, with no CJ request.

### DEC-186 · CJ CSV: an omitted column or blank cell keeps the stored offer value
**2026-10-23 · proposed (P1-52)** · aud-008 imp-070; builds on DEC-116/156 (unchanged) and DEC-074; vault Data/Sources/Source - CJ Dropshipping
- **Cause:** the shared parser drops both an absent column and a blank cell from the row, and the CJ `Row` then defaulted them to `moq=1` and `warehouse_country=None`, which the writer assigned to the existing `SupplierOffer`. A later price-only import overwrote `(5, US)` with `(1, None)`.
- **Decision:** a blank cell means the same as an omitted column: no evidence for that field, so the stored offer value is kept. A stated `moq` updates it. Stated `warehouse_country` values update it when the product's rows name one country, and set it unknown when they name several. Blank rows are ignored in that count, so `{CN, blank}` now stores CN where it used to clear the country. A CSV cannot clear a stored value. Variant prices, freight rows and the DEC-156 writer are unchanged.
- **New offer:** with no MOQ evidence, the row takes the existing `supplier_offers.moq` server default of 1 (`NOT NULL`, no schema change here), so a stored 1 doesn't tell a stated MOQ of 1 from an unstated one. The country stays unknown. The writer itself no longer supplies a default. The shared parser, its DEC-074 check (which still reads a blank MOQ as 1) and every other template are unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** blank means clear (needs a nullable `moq` and makes an empty spreadsheet cell erase evidence); telling blank from absent (changes the shared parser and all its callers for no stated need); skipping offer creation without evidence (changes the 1:1 supplier-listing extension).
- **Validation:** the lead's two-import probe keeps `(5, US)` and stores 2.60 as the current `CSV_WRITER` price. A six-import sequence, run for both an absent and a blank first import, covers the new offer `(1, None)`, an explicit update, blank and absent preservation, a single-column update, mixed blank/stated warehouses and an identical-bytes repeat (skipped, unchanged). Each changed price appends exactly once. Fixtures only. The follow-up of a nullable `moq` or a provenance flag needs a migration.

### DEC-152 · Shopify manual catalogs share the catalog writer and saved store context
**2026-10-23 · proposed (task_cde7ad28ca13)** · P1-07c; DEC-064/087/113/117/120
- **Context:** register `ShopifyImportAdapter` for existing `csv-v1` and `shopify-json-v1`. Both require explicit `sources.config.shopify_import = {store_domain, shop_id, currency}`, validated and copied into the original raw request; retries/corrections reuse that saved context, never current config or a filename. CSV row domains/currencies must match; existing CSV USD defaults remain DEC-064, checked against the explicit context. No source configuration is changed by this task.
- **Identity/mapping:** reuse the catalog models/upsert for stores, listings, variants and snapshots. CSV rows group by handle in their explicit domain; new entities require optional positive Shopify `product_id`/`variant_id` columns, while omitted IDs resolve only one existing handle and SKU (fallback variant title). Ambiguous, missing or duplicate resolved identities fail the capture transaction; no synthetic IDs. Missing availability/title stays null; absent inventory never becomes zero. Sparse CSV metadata does not erase catalog vendor/tags/options. Imports never finalize catalog completeness or delist absent products.
- **Writer/provenance:** captures marked `import_source=shopify_stores` join DEC-120's catalog writer with its unchanged owned fields; `.js` remains the inventory owner. Listing/variant rows retain upload `captured_at`, row/owner `observed_for`, raw ID and parser version; change-only, historical ordering and immutable parser corrections use the existing helpers and schema. Same-file upload remains a no-op under DEC-064/092.
- **Alternatives/split:** duplicate persistence, inferred shop identity and a second writer with overlapping ownership were rejected. No migration, dependency, API schema, flag, seed, compliance or collector-host change. Native Shopify merchant-export header/continuation-row conversion and context entry in Data Health remain P1-07d; this slice accepts the existing Pulso product CSV template and bare browser-saved products JSON. Fixture tests cover grouping, malformed rows, both formats, new entities, provenance, replay, context preservation, current reads and unchanged captures; PostgreSQL/S3 are unverified locally.

### DEC-162 · Shopify imports never rewrite current listing state
**2026-10-23 · proposed (P1-51)** · P1-50 H1, M1–M3, L2, L3; amends DEC-152; DEC-064/113/120
- **Sightings (H1):** an import is not a crawl sighting. It never clears `delisted_at` and never moves an existing listing's or variant's `last_seen_at`; only live catalogs relist or delist (DEC-113). New rows keep `first_seen_at = last_seen_at = captured_at`. Import text (listing title, handle and URL, plus vendor, type, tags and dates when given; variant title, SKU and options) is written only to a new row, or when the import's `observed_for` is on or after the UTC day of the row's `last_seen_at`; otherwise the import writes snapshots only. Ceiling: two imports both dated after the last sighting apply in upload order, because no column records a text's evidence date (adding one needs a migration).
- **Columns (M1):** `Template.required_headers` names headers a file must carry even when its cells are blank. Shopify requires `compare_at_price` and `available`, so a file without them fails in `parse_csv` ("Missing required columns", a 422 in preview, a failed run on commit) before raw storage. Blank cells: compare-at means no compare-at, available means unknown; both are catalog-writer observations and supersede under DEC-120. CSV rows carry no `source_updated_at`, so a current-dated CSV makes it null in the current read. A blank or absent `variant_title`, like `sku`, leaves the stored title unchanged.
- **Aggregates (M2):** an import appends the listing snapshot (price range, minimum compare-at, availability) only when its group lists every non-delisted variant the listing already has. Variant snapshots are written either way.
- **Currency (M3):** the import fails, writing no rows, when the stored store currency differs from the saved context currency, mirroring the `shop_id` check.
- **Live parser (L2) and panel (L3):** a live `products.json` again rejects a null or missing variant `title` or `available`; only imports may leave them unknown. The `.js` panel orders by the latest listing snapshot from live catalog captures only (`LIVE_CATALOG`: `path` starts `/products.json?`), so imports never reorder it.
- **Alternatives:** never updating existing text (titles go stale while the crawler is off); reading an absent column as blank (an absent column is no evidence); rebuilding the aggregate from stored variants plus the file (mixes evidence dates); a writer or evidence-date column (migration). No migration, dependency, flag, API schema, source or collector change; DEC-120 ownership, append-only snapshots and UTC cutoffs are unchanged. L1 (one bad row URL fails the whole file without row errors) is not addressed here and stays with imp-004.

### DEC-166 · Data Health Load more for open gaps and run targets
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-075)** · consumes DEC-133 / DEC-163 pages; vault Features/Data Health, Design/Tables & Data Density
- **Decision:** the open-gaps list and each run's targets end in one explicit **Load more** footer. The first page stays as before (the shared `useHost()` page of 50 gaps; the run detail's 50 targets). Each press fetches one more page with `limit=200`, the API maximum, so going past 200 rows takes one press. The first press starts from the first page's `next_cursor`; each later press uses the previous page's cursor. Nothing older loads until pressed: no infinite scroll and no automatic history walk. Gap ranges merge across all loaded pages, so a range cut by a page boundary reads as one. The first open-gaps press also reveals the loaded ranges beyond the 8-row preview; when the first page is already complete the press only reveals them ("Show N older ranges") and sends no request.
- **Consistency:** the extra pages are a separate infinite query whose key carries the cursor the first page ended on. While a refetch (the 5-minute interval or Refresh) leaves that cursor unchanged, Refresh refetches the loaded pages in order. When it moves (a newer gap, a fill), the extra pages drop away and the list returns to its first page, rather than skipping or repeating rows. Closing a run drawer drops that run's loaded targets.
- **States:** while a page is pending the button reads "Loading…", is `aria-disabled` and ignores presses, so a second press sends nothing. A failed page keeps every loaded row, shows the cause and error ID inline with Copy error ID, and the same button retries it. Focus stays on the button; once nothing is left the button unmounts and focus moves to the footer count (`tabindex=-1`). Counts claim only what the API gave: gaps say how many ranges are shown and how many slots are loaded, with "older gaps not loaded yet" until the chain ends ("all N missed slots in the last 90 days"). Targets count against the run's `target_count` from the newest loaded page, reading "at least" when targets landed after that count.
- **Fix in scope:** `useSourceRuns` passed `initialPageParam: undefined`, which openapi-react-query sends as `cursor=0`. The API rejects that (`ge=1`), so the drawer's runs list failed against the real API; the e2e mock ignored the query. It now starts at `null`, which is omitted.
- **Alternatives:** making `useHost()` itself the infinite query (one cache entry, but every consumer, the nav badge included, would refetch every loaded page each 5 minutes after the owner leaves the page); one infinite query that fetches its own first page (a duplicate first-page request on every load); infinite scroll as Design/Tables & Data Density suggests for long tables (an automatic fetch this task rules out); 50-row Load more pages (four presses for 250 rows).
- **Limits:** loaded rows aren't virtualized. The 90-day window bounds open gaps to a few thousand slots, reached only by pressing repeatedly. The host card's "At least N missed slots" still counts the first page only.

### DEC-167 · Copyable IDs take keyboard focus and select themselves
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-062)** · extends DEC-138; vault Design/Accessibility, UI States
- **Decision:** the ID beside a CopyButton in ErrorState, PanelError and the not-authorized user ID is one shared `SelectableId` span: `tabIndex=0`, and keyboard focus selects the whole ID (`selectAllChildren`), ready for Ctrl/⌘+C. The browser's own copy works when the clipboard API is denied or missing. It keeps `select-all`, so pointer and manual selection are unchanged. Screen readers get `role="textbox"`, `aria-readonly` and an `aria-label` ("Error ID" / "User ID"). Focus shows the global `--ring` outline (rounded-sm), and `hit-area` gives it a 44 px coarse-pointer target. No new information is shown: the same ID as before.
- **Keystrokes:** from a failed Copy button, Shift+Tab twice (past Retry) then Ctrl/⌘+C in ErrorState and PanelError; once in not-authorized, where the ID sits right before Copy. Tab order follows the visual order.
- **Alternatives:** a native read-only `<input>` (full caret semantics, but its value drops out of page text selection, it can't wrap inline, and one-click select-all needs click handling); selecting the ID automatically when a copy fails (fewer keys, but it changes DEC-138's hint and couples CopyButton to the ID element); a plain focusable span without a role (no accessible name).
- **Limits:** a non-editable ARIA textbox has no caret, so arrow keys don't move through the ID and Ctrl+A selects the page. In not-authorized, `truncate` clips the coarse-pointer hit area to the ID's box. The not-authorized ID renders only for a real Clerk session, so Playwright covers the shared component through ErrorState and PanelError; the real page waits on imp-064.

### DEC-174 · Import preview shows the API's parsed row sample, ordered from the rows themselves
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-080)** · DEC-117/117b, DEC-145; vault Features/CSV Export & Import, Design/Tables & Data Density
- **Decision:** the CSV import preview renders `ImportPreview.rows` (the API's first 20 valid rows) with no extra click, last in the preview: after the counts, the row errors and a failed import's error line, so what needs acting on comes first in reading and Tab order (Import → Row errors → Retry → Copy error ID → sample), under the heading "Valid rows · first 20 of 1,284" / "all 3" / "1 row". Values show as the API parsed them (no reformatting, money math or re-parsing); a blank optional cell is "—" with "Blank in this row" on hover and for screen readers. Headers are the template's column names. The sample has no live role: the existing polite status still speaks only the counts.
- **Column order:** `parse_csv` builds each row in template order and leaves out blank cells, so each row is an ordered subset of the template. The web merges them so a column follows every column some row puts before it; only two columns no sampled row holds together fall back to first-seen order. A column blank in every sampled row is not shown.
- **Layout:** a focusable, `relative` scroll region (keeps the "—" atom's `sr-only` text from widening the page) that hugs its columns and scrolls inside at 375px; columns whose values are all numbers or digit strings (money, rates, counts, ISO dates) are right-aligned with tabular figures; long text truncates at 16rem with the full value on hover.
- **Alternatives:** fetching `GET …/import/template` for an exact header (an extra request with its own loading and error states, for a rare ordering tie); hard-coding template columns in the web (drifts from `TEMPLATES`); numbering sample rows (the API sends no file line numbers, and a 1–20 index would read as one).

### DEC-184 · Retry deadline in the Sources row and re-enable dialog
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-094)** · aud-010 A10-03, aud-011 B11-06; DEC-032; SPEC §4.2; vault Features/Data Health, Design/Typography
- **Decision:** the Sources status note has two parts. The reason (disabled reason, skip note) truncates at 192 px in the table and wraps on the phone card. "Retry after <Oct 22, 15:00 UTC>" sits on its own line and is never truncated. The note and the source type label use `text-label` (12/16). When `retry_not_before` is later than the page's data time (`now`, the newest DH-1 fetch, which also stamps "As of"), the re-enable dialog's description adds one callout: "Retry after <UTC> (in 1h). You can re-enable now, but runs before then are deferred." The textarea placeholder then drops "retry-after passed". At or after the deadline, or with no deadline, the dialog is unchanged.
- **Unchanged:** re-enabling stays allowed before the deadline. The PATCH body stays `{enabled, reason}`, and nothing writes `retry_not_before`: the framework still defers live runs before it (`lifecycle.py`, `RunDeferred("retry_not_before")`). No API, guard or policy change.
- **Alternatives / why:**
  - Widening the column or lifting the 192 px cap: rejected, because the table already uses its width at 1024–1440 px and imp-081 adds a row action.
  - One line with only the reason truncated: rejected, because "retry after" plus the time takes about 160 of the 192 px.
  - The wall clock when the dialog opens: rejected, because the rest of the page reads one snapshot. The two can differ by at most the 5-minute refetch, and a stale "future" errs toward caution.
- **Validation:** `apps/web/e2e/data-health/retry-deadline.spec.ts` covers the unclipped deadline at 1440 and 375, 12 px type and status text, the dialog stating a future deadline once with no "passed" wording, an unchanged PATCH body and keyboard flow, and unchanged copy for a past deadline and for no deadline.

### DEC-179 · Import panel: a running import keeps its Source select
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-089)** · DEC-117b, DEC-145, DEC-175; imp-081 report P2; vault Features/CSV Export & Import, Features/Data Health
- **Cause:** the Source select's change called `commit.reset()` and re-checked the file for the new source. Mid-import that detached the panel from the running commit: the server still imported the file, but its outcome, Open runs and the data refresh never appeared, and the file was re-checked against another template.
- **Decision:** while the commit is pending, the select is `aria-disabled`, with its text and chevron in `--subtle-foreground` and `pointer-events: none`, and its `onChange` returns early, so React puts the value back. Once the import ends, the select works as before: a change re-checks the open file and clears the outcome.
- **Alternatives:** `opacity-50`, as on the panel's buttons, also fades the focus ring, to 1.93:1 in the light theme; the token colour keeps the ring at 3.96:1 light and 11.25:1 dark. Native `disabled` drops the select from the tab order and blurs it, and DEC-145's "focus elsewhere is left alone" test focuses it mid-import. Re-binding the outcome to the commit's own source would keep the select live, but the success handler clears the file and preview, so it would also discard the newly picked source's check. A cancel control is new UX, and the API has no cancel.
- **Why:** this matches the panel's other locks, which are `aria-disabled` with the reason in the status ("Importing <file>…"), and imp-081's row entry, which also refuses to switch during an import. It is web-only, with no API, copy or dependency change.

### DEC-182 · Import panel: a running import keeps its file against Choose another and a drop
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-092)** · DEC-145, DEC-179, DEC-175; imp-089 report "Found, not fixed"; vault Features/CSV Export & Import, Features/Data Health
- **Cause:** **Choose another…** and a file dropped on the drop zone both call the panel's `check()`, which calls `commit.reset()`. Mid-import that detached the panel from the running commit: the server still imported the file, but its outcome, Open runs and the data refresh never appeared, and the new file was checked instead (reproduced with a held fixture commit, keyboard, pointer and drop).
- **Decision:** `check()` returns early while the commit is pending and the file is a different `File`. That covers every file-replacement caller (the picker's input, a drop, a preview Retry) in one place. The Source select passes the same file, so its behaviour is unchanged here; imp-089 (DEC-179) locks it. **Choose another…** is `aria-disabled` while importing, opens no picker on click, Enter or Space, and dims its text to `--subtle-foreground` with `opacity-100`, like DEC-179's select, so its focus ring keeps full strength. A drag over the zone during an import gets `dropEffect = "none"` and no highlight; `preventDefault` stays so a stray drop never opens the file in the tab. After success or failure, replacing works as before.
- **Alternatives:** guarding only on `commit.isPending` would also stop the select's re-check on `main`, leaving the old source's preview under the new source until imp-089 merges. Native `disabled` drops the button from the tab order and blurs it mid-import. Guarding only the button would leave the drop path and any later caller open. A cancel control is new UX, and the API has no cancel.
- **Why:** one guard where all file paths meet, the panel's existing aria-disabled lock pattern, web-only, with no API, copy or dependency change.

### DEC-185 · Locked Import button: secondary tokens, not opacity
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-095)** · imp-092 report leftover (P3); AGENTS.md §7–8 visible focus ring, AA; vault Design/Accessibility
- **Cause:** the CSV panel's Import button stays focusable while locked (`aria-disabled`: importing, or blocked by the source). The Button's `aria-disabled:opacity-50` also fades its `:focus-visible` outline, to 1.95:1 on the light card and 3.61:1 on the dark card (measured from screenshot pixels; the ring token is 3.96:1 and 11.25:1).
- **Decision:** a local class change on that button only: `aria-disabled:opacity-100` with `aria-disabled:bg-secondary aria-disabled:border-border aria-disabled:text-subtle-foreground`. The locked button reads as a quiet secondary control (label 4.98:1 light, 5.29:1 dark) and the ring keeps the token's full contrast. `e2e/data-health/import-focus-ring.spec.ts` measures all four sides of the ring in both themes at 1440 and 375px.
- **Alternatives / why:** keeping the primary fill with `bg-primary/50` gives a translucent pair whose contrast depends on the surface. Changing the shared Button's `aria-disabled` styling would restyle every locked button in the app, which needs its own audit. The same token dim is the one imp-089 and imp-092 use for their locked controls, so the panel ends up with one locked treatment.

### DEC-175 · Per-source import entry: rows open the panel; an open file is never switched silently
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-081)** · DEC-117/117b, DEC-145; vault Features/Data Health, Features/CSV Export & Import, Design/UI States ("Import CSV instead")
- **Decision:** every sources-table row (desktop table and phone card) gets a ghost action beside Run now, labelled from `SourceHealth.csv_import`: **Import CSV** (`ready`), **Check CSV** (`preview_only`, hint "No import adapter yet: check a file against the <source> template now.") or **Import CSV** `aria-disabled` with "No CSV template for <source> yet." (null). The hints use the shared tooltip, so focus shows them. The action selects that source's existing template in the existing panel (same select, template link, preview and commit endpoints), focuses **Choose CSV…** and scrolls it to the middle of the view (`smooth`, instant under reduced motion). It sends no request and enables nothing.
- **Open work:** with a file open for another source (checking, checked, failed check or failed import), the row doesn't switch. The panel shows "<file> is still open for <source>. Switching checks it against <target> instead." with **Switch to <target>** focused (the line is its description); Switch does exactly what the Source select does. While the import is running, the line reads "<file> is importing into <source>. Choose <target> once it finishes." and takes focus itself (`tabIndex=-1`); there is no Switch, because resetting a running commit would lose its outcome. The question lapses with its file (imported or replaced) and when the open source is chosen. A file over 20 MB was never sent, so it switches at once. With no file open, the row switches at once and clears a finished outcome, like the select. No live region is added; DEC-145 still decides focus after an import.
- **Clicks:** a non-default source's file takes 3 (row action, choose, Import), with no scrolling; before, 4 plus a scroll (native select open and pick, choose, Import). The preselected source stays 2 from the panel itself.
- **Alternatives:** opening the file picker straight from the row (skips the template and readiness the user should see first; browsers differ on scripted pickers); a confirm dialog (a modal for a reversible switch); queueing the switch until a running import ends (changes the source under the user later).

### DEC-196 · Data Health: a missing gap summary reads Unknown, and attention counts become floors
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-133)** · aud-014 A14-02; amends DEC-096's state list and badge rule; DEC-040, DEC-123; changes no API
- **Cause:** Overdue comes from `HostHealth.latest_gap_ends`. The Sources table, run drawer and nav badge defaulted it to `{}` while the host call was loading or had failed, so a source with an open gap fell through to Healthy or Partial, and the panel note and badge counted 1 instead of 2.
- **Decision:**
  - `sourceState` and `attentionCount` take `latest_gap_ends` or `undefined` (host loading or failed). DEC-096/DEC-123 precedence is unchanged: Blocked, Disabled, Running and Failing don't depend on the host; Overdue still wins when the summary is there; Skipped and Never run keep their states. Partial and Healthy, which only the summary can clear of Overdue, read **Unknown** instead (muted dashed pill, question-mark icon, label in text). Its status note says "Gap check didn't load" (short enough for the table's truncated note), and its hint gives the cause and the next step: "Missed runs come from the collector host's gap check, which didn't load, so this source may be overdue. Retry on the Collector host card." Never Healthy (DEC-040).
  - **While the host call loads**, those pills keep the loading skeleton, with no note. The panel note's count and the nav badge stay hidden until both calls settle. DEC-096's "nothing while loading" now covers the host call too.
  - **When the host call has failed**, the count is a floor whenever a source the summary could make Overdue is left (Unknown, Skipped or Never run): the panel note reads "at least N need(s) attention" and the badge "N+", announced as "at least N sources need attention". At 0, nothing shows, never "0+". When every source is settled before the gap check (blocked, disabled, running, failing), the count is exact.
  - The run drawer's pill uses the same rule.
- **Alternatives:** hiding the whole Sources table until both calls land (it holds back Blocked and Failing, which need no host); a "?" badge at zero (an alarm on every host blip); keeping Partial for partial runs (it still hides a possible Overdue).
- **Why:** a missing input is never a good value. The owner must not read "Healthy", or a smaller count, when the check that would say otherwise didn't run. Each source keeps the state it can prove, and the counts say "at least" instead of understating.

### DEC-198 · Data Health: Overdue names its missed slot; the source name is the visible runs cue
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-124)** · aud-010 A10-02, A10-04; imp-124, imp-125; DEC-096, DEC-123, DEC-133, DEC-196; vault Features/Data Health; changes no API
- **Missed slot (imp-124):** the row, phone card and run drawer of an Overdue source show its latest missed slot on one line and the reason on the next, worded as the Open gaps table words it ("Missed Oct 22, 10:00–12:00 UTC" / "Host offline"). The slot is the merged range ending at `latest_gap_ends[source]` when that gap is on the first gap page the host call returns; otherwise the line reads "Missed a slot ending <end>" from the complete summary, with no reason. Overdue precedence and imp-133's Unknown state are unchanged: the note shows only for Overdue.
- **Link:** the note is a link to `#open-gaps`, the Open gaps heading (`tabIndex=-1`, scroll margin for the phone top bar). A click or Enter focuses the heading, which scrolls only as far as needed. A native fragment jump is not used: from lg the shell scrolls inside `main`, but the document is also scrollable (escaped absolutely positioned boxes make it 1,319 px at 1440×900), so the jump shifted the whole shell up by 419 px. In the drawer the link closes the drawer and focuses the heading instead of the opener; if the heading isn't rendered, focus returns to the opener as before.
- **Touch:** under a coarse pointer the table's status cell gets 12 px vertical padding and the note 16 px above it, and the drawer's note 6 px more, so the pill's and the link's 44 px hit squares don't overlap each other or the row above (A11Y-02).
- **Runs cue (imp-125):** the existing name button carries it: the name is underlined at rest (`--border-strong`, `currentColor` on hover) and followed by a chevron (`--muted-foreground`). The accessible name stays "<source>: open runs", the path stays 1 click, and Tab order gains no stop. The Overdue row gains 1 stop, its note link.
- **Table width:** the note is a fixed 180 px in the table, which holds a same-day slot on one line and keeps the table's min-content (1,133 px) inside its 1,140 px region at 1440. A longer slot wraps; it is never truncated.
- **Alternatives:** a separate "Runs" (vault "View runs") button in Actions adds a Tab stop per row and a third button to the 375 px card's action row; link-coloured names (`--info`) add a second accent to a dense table; one " · " line overflowed the table at 1440 or broke mid-time at 192 px; reading older loaded gap pages needs state that lives inside the Open gaps list, and a source's newest gap is on the first page unless 50 newer gaps precede it.

### DEC-197 · Data Health: a failed refetch keeps the data on screen under one stale line
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-132)** · DEC-096, DEC-166, DEC-196; vault Features/Data Health, Design/UI States (Refreshing, overlays), Design/Accessibility (live regions)
- **Decision:** a Data Health query that has data and then fails a refetch keeps that data (TanStack `isRefetchError`). Only a query with no data yet (`isLoadingError`) shows its existing error. On top of the kept data, `StaleLine` (`atoms.tsx`) shows one line: what failed, "Showing data as of <UTC time>", why (the problem detail, or PanelError's API-down copy), **Retry** and, for an API problem, the error ID with **Copy error ID**. The time is that query's `dataUpdatedAt`, or the page header's time at page level. It uses the warning tint, because the data is old, not wrong. Its icon and text carry the meaning without colour.
- **One line per failed request:** when both core calls (`/sources`, `/host`) fail, one page-level line under the header speaks for every panel, and the panels show none (`useCoreDown`). Otherwise each request's own panel shows it: Sources, Collector host, Backups, Coverage. Panels that only reuse another panel's call keep their cached data and add no line of their own (Coverage's source names, the CSV import panel's sources, Open gaps' host data). This matches aud-014 A14-08.
- **Drawer:** the run drawer is modal and hides the page line, so its runs and targets always show their own line. A failed "Load older runs" page keeps the runs already loaded, and its line's Retry asks for that page again. imp-136 (A14-05) replaces this with the `LoadMore` footer.
- **Unchanged:** the first-load API-down page and first-load panel errors, the nav badge and the sidebar footer (DEC-196). Nothing is announced: background refetches are never announced (vault Design/Accessibility). An announcement for a failure the owner triggered, and focus after Retry, belong to imp-135 (A14-04).
- **Alternatives:** showing a line in every panel during an outage (5 identical lines); a toast (it goes away while the data is still old); greying the old data (meaning carried by colour, and harder to read just when it is needed).
### DEC-131 · Collector seeds and source controls: container step, evidence gates, private seeds
**2026-10-23 · proposed (P1-42)** · task_00a3ac8984f3; Lead Playbook §6 steps 3–4; builds on DEC-065, DEC-069, DEC-073, DEC-076, DEC-090, DEC-104
- **Operations:** `uv run --frozen python -m infra.scripts.collector seeds | enable <source> | disable <source> --checkout C:\dev\pulso` reuse the P1-13/P1-14 contract unchanged: primary checkout, clean `main`, not ahead of `origin/main`, no `.collector-hold` (rechecked before every git, pytest and docker command), the atomic lock, the UTC status file (`op` is `seeds`, `enable-<source>` or `disable-<source>`) and the hidden detached child. Sources are a closed set: `fx_frankfurter`, `shopify_stores`, `cj`, `google_trends`; `backup` and anything else is refused before any command runs.
- **Where it runs:** collector Postgres must be running. The database step is `docker compose -p pulso-collector … run --rm --no-deps -T worker python -m infra.scripts.collector.sources <op>`: a one-shot container from the stack's worker image, whose `.env.collector` already selects the collector database. The host passes no database settings and reads no env file. The worker image gains only the seed loader and that module (`infra/docker/worker.Dockerfile`), so `update` must rebuild the image after this merges.
- **Seeds:** the host validates `infra/seeds/seed_v1.yaml` with the existing loader and requires DEC-073 recorded as approved plus file approval `seed_v1` / `approved`. A draft file is refused: loading it would pin `seed_v1` with every entry disabled, and DEC-065's same-version rule would then refuse the approved copy. One owner-made, gitignored `infra/seeds/private*` file may add stores, as `{"stores": [{"domain", "name", "enabled"}]}` (JSON-compatible YAML, full-line `#` comments). It is appended to `stores` and validated together; more than one file, extra keys, duplicates or bad domains refuse with "details withheld". Seed JSON reaches the container on stdin and never enters an image (`.dockerignore` also excludes `infra/seeds/private*`); the container suppresses the loader's diff and prints counts only. Repeats are no-ops, and loading seeds enables nothing. A private file added after `seed_v1` is loaded is a content change and needs a new approved seed version.
- **Enable gates, fail closed, no override flag:** every gate is logged open or closed. Evidence kinds: named fixture tests that must report only `N passed` (a skip or failure closes the gate) when run at the clean checkout, whose commits are all on `origin/main`; a `merge: <task>` commit in `HEAD`; a DEC entry whose status line starts with `approved `; a marker in a repo doc; and `closed` for evidence this command cannot verify.
  - **All sources:** P1-03b merged; the framework block tests (one disable event across runs; Retry-After deadline honoured); the global 30/min limiter test; the source's `parse()` fixture tests.
  - **Seed sources:** `approved_targets()` non-empty, checked in the container while the source row is locked.
  - **`shopify_stores`:** SPK-2 results section in `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md`; DEC-090 approved (the owner's go); DEC-090 conditions (a)–(d) each mapped to existing fixture tests (the worker's reading, for review); (e) closed until a fixture test proves inventory-based labels are unavailable; P1-38 and P1-39 merged (P1-37 M1/M2).
  - **`cj`:** P1-38 and P1-39 merged; the owner's review of CJ's user agreement and `CJ_API_KEY` are closed. **`google_trends`:** P1-09c merged; owner-run OPEN-1/2 evidence and `SERPAPI_API_KEY` are closed. Keys are never inspected. A closed gate opens only through a reviewed change to this protected table.
  - Today only `fx_frankfurter` has every gate's evidence.
- **Source changes:** enable refuses a `blocked` source (only the owner's typed-reason Data Health re-enable clears a block), never changes `retry_not_before` or the block count, and appends a `source_events` row with `actor=collector-cli`. Disable sets `disabled`, keeps a block's `blocked` status, and appends an event. Enabling an enabled source or disabling a disabled one is a no-op with no event. One source at a time and the Data Health check between them stay with the operator, as the Lead Playbook says.
- **Alternatives:** bind-mount the checkout into the one-shot container (its code could differ from the running image); bake seeds into the image (private domains in a layer); run the database step on the host with `DATABASE_URL` (the host would need collector secrets); check only that tests exist (a failing or skipped test is not evidence); an evidence or `--force` flag (a bypass).
- **Validation:** fixture tests only (fake subprocesses, temporary checkouts, in-memory SQLite); the mapped node ids pass on this branch, and a simulated `/app` layout imports the container module. No Docker build, collector database, host operation or source request. Unverified locally: the image build and real `compose run` stdin piping.

### DEC-143 · Collector approval grammar and names-only key evidence
**2026-10-23 · proposed (P1-45)** · P1-43 R1/R2; corrects DEC-131 implementation defaults
- **Approval boundary:** SPK-3, DEC-073 seeds and CJ's DEC-136 gate share `owner_approved()` and one `OWNER_STATUS_PATTERN`. The pattern is empty until the owner sets the required status wording plus its dated grammar at merge time; empty always refuses. `re.fullmatch` rejects proposal wording and extra prefixes/suffixes. The existing clean-main, zero-commits-ahead-of-`origin/main`, hold and operation-lock checks remain prerequisites, so checkout DEC evidence must already be on main. DEC-073/090/136 are unchanged.
- **Key evidence:** CJ uses persisted key `cj`. Its agreement gate now consumes DEC-136; its key gate and the Trends key gate require `CJ_API_KEY` / `SERPAPI_API_KEY` respectively among non-empty names in the collector env file. The host passes file text directly to a pure names-only helper, matching `^([A-Z0-9_]+)=\s*[^\s#]` on individual lines so an empty assignment cannot consume the next line. Only names leave that helper; values are never retained, compared, returned or logged. Tests supply strings in memory, never env files. This supersedes DEC-131's no-env-read and permanently closed key/agreement descriptions, without adding an override or changing source policy.
- **Remaining evidence:** Shopify DEC-090(e) stays closed until a fixture test proves Grade A inventory-label ineligibility; Trends OPEN-1/2 stays closed until owner-run partial-flag and empty/no-results response evidence exists. Existing merge, fixture, approved-target and blocked-source gates remain. The owner merge, host image rebuild and Data Health verification remain separate operational steps.
- **Alternatives / why:** prefix matching accepts worker proposals; one dated literal cannot match the existing different approval dates; unconditional success or an override bypasses evidence. A shared full-status grammar and a small names-only helper close the review findings without new dependencies, settings, migrations or authorization.

### DEC-200 · Import panel: Row errors get a heading, stack below `sm`, and stay with an import's outcome
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-116)** · imp-116, imp-117 (imp-080 styleseed −1 hierarchy and layout), imp-129 (aud-010 A10-08); DEC-145, DEC-174; vault Features/CSV Export & Import, Features/Data Health; changes no API
- **Decision:**
  - One `RowErrors` block in `import-panel.tsx` serves the check and the import. A visible h3 "Row errors", styled like the sample's "Valid rows" heading, names the scrollable table through `aria-labelledby`. The accessible name stays "Row errors".
  - **Below `sm` (640px)** the Column column is hidden, and each column name becomes a mono, muted line above its problem in the Problem cell. A row's accessible name is unchanged ("19 cj_product_id or sku One identifier required"). From `sm` up, the 3 columns stay, with the name on one line. At 375px, Problem goes from 80px to 259px (16rem).
  - **After an import**, `ImportResult.errors` (the API's first 100, with the "Showing the first N of M" line) shows under the outcome, after Open runs in Tab order. It derives from the commit, so another file or another source (both reset the commit) clears it together with the outcome. Focus (Open runs, DEC-145) and the announcements are unchanged. A repeat upload has no errors, so it shows no table.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Wrapping the Column cell (the brief's first option): its longest word, "cj_product_id", still needs 125px, which leaves Problem 134px (8.4rem) at 375px. Breaking names at underscores leaves 189px and splits identifiers.
  - Letting it wrap at every width squeezed the name onto 2 lines at 1440, beside a full-width Problem column (styleseed round 0).
  - Container queries: the panel is full width, and no other component uses them.
  - A separate post-import list: duplicate markup for the same rows.
- **Why:** the owner fixes skipped rows without choosing the file again (0 extra clicks, from 1 plus a file pick), every problem reads in full sentences at phone width, and the table has a visible name like the sample beside it. Web only.

### DEC-201 · App shell: main is the containing block for escaped absolute boxes
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-147)** · imp-124 report leftover 2; vault Design/App Shell & Navigation; changes no API
- **Cause:** at lg the shell is one viewport tall and only `<main>` scrolls. An absolutely positioned box with no positioned ancestor takes the viewport as its containing block, so `main`'s `overflow-y-auto` neither clips nor scrolls it. On `/admin/data-health` the CSV panel's two `sr-only` live regions (`role="status"`, `role="alert"`) sat at the page end and stretched the document to 1,319 px at 1440×900; a native jump to `#open-gaps` scrolled `main` and then the document, pushing the whole shell up 419 px.
- **Decision:** `<main>` gets `relative` at every width (`shell/app-shell.tsx`). Every page's stray absolute boxes now belong to main's scroll area: at lg the document stays one viewport tall and a jump scrolls `main` only; below lg nothing moves (the document scrolls as before). `e2e/shell-scroll.spec.ts` asserts the document height at 1440 on `/admin/data-health` and `/`, and that a native `#open-gaps` jump and the Overdue note's link both leave the top bar where it was at 1440 and 375.
- **Alternatives:** `overflow: clip` on the shell root (clipping skips boxes whose containing block is above the clipping element, so it changes nothing here); `relative` on the shell root (it contains them, but they stop scrolling with `main` and its `overflow-hidden` box becomes script-scrollable, so a jump could shift the shell inside itself); `contain: paint` on `main` (it also captures `position: fixed` descendants); `relative` on the CSV panel (fixes one page, not the cause).
- **Why:** one class where every page's content passes, no paint or clipping change for anything already positioned, and the phone layout is untouched.

### DEC-202 · While Codex is off, a fresh Claude session does the owed cross-reviews
**2026-10-23 · approved by owner 2026-10-23** · amends how AGENTS.md §6 (DEC-072) review is staffed while Codex is off; DEC-073; DEC-136; Lead Playbook §6
- **Owner's answer (2026-10-23, in the owner's session):** asked whether a fresh Claude session may do the owed cross-reviews while Codex is off: "Yes, let a fresh Claude session do the owed cross-reviews while Codex is off, so the lead can update the collector host and turn on FX and CJ."
- **Decision:** while Codex is off, a fresh Claude session that did not write the merges does the batched cross-review AGENTS.md §6 asks of "the other agent". The first batch covers P1-38 `bc9a77e`, imp-068 `973ad0a`, imp-004 `6b4c03e`, imp-053 `8a4575f`, P1-52 `59e0a67` and P1-51 `b803124`. The report goes to `C:/Users/Xody2/orca/reports/cross-review-2026-10-23-claude.md`, and the verdict line goes in the vault `Agents/Lead State` Log. Once it reports no blocking findings, the collector-host update and the Lead Playbook §6 steps (seeds, `enable fx_frankfurter`, then `enable cj`) are unblocked. Blocking findings get fixed first, as fix tasks.
- **Ends:** when the owner says Codex is back, Codex resumes cross-reviews of Claude's merges.
- **Alternatives:** wait for Codex (keeps FX and CJ off for an unknown time, and CJ's 30-day window is running, DEC-136); let the authoring worker or the lead review its own merge (not independent).
- **Why:** an independent reader still checks every risky merge before it runs on the collector host, without waiting on Codex usage.

### DEC-203 · Data Health holds on CI's Linux font metrics: whole slot phrases, 8 px between table columns, a shimmer that covers its bar
**2026-10-23 · proposed (p1-53)** · red e2e on `main` since 4b23386 (runs 40035086253, 40036060009); amends DEC-198 (Table width); imp-082, imp-124, imp-148; vault Features/Data Health, Design/Motion System (pattern 14); changes no API
- **Why:** Chromium on Linux sets text about 2 % wider than on Windows. The missed slot measures 180.0 px on Windows and 184.5 px on Linux, so DEC-198's fixed 180 px note wrapped it. The 1440 Sources table needs 1,155 px of its 1,140 px region on Linux even with the slot wrapped, against 1,133 px on Windows. At 375 px the loaded host card is 36 px taller than its skeleton and the backups card 14 px. That alone scores 0.024 CLS. When Backups lands after the host card, the shimmer of the skeletons it moves scores 0.151–0.157, CI's value.
- **Slot phrases:** `MissedSlot.slot` is a list of phrases joined by spaces, each kept whole (`whitespace-nowrap`). A line breaks only between phrases: never inside a same-day range ("Missed Oct 22, 10:00–12:00 UTC" is one phrase), after the dash of a cross-day one ("Missed Oct 21, 23:00 –" / "Oct 22, 01:00 UTC"), and before the date of the off-page fallback ("Missed a slot ending" / "Oct 22, 12:00 UTC"). The note loses its fixed width, so the slot sizes its column. The text and accessible name are unchanged.
- **Table cells:** the Sources table's cells (header, rows, skeleton rows, Planned rows) have 8 px side padding between columns and 16 px at the table's outer edges, matching the panel header and the Coverage table (was 12 px everywhere). At 1440 the table then needs 1,103 px on Linux and 1,077 px on Windows of 1,140, and Freshness reads on one line in every row (imp-148's acceptance) without its own change. Below 1440 the region still scrolls, but less.
- **Shimmer:** the Skeleton's sweep is the middle third of a box three bars wide, starting one bar to the left, and moves one bar (transform only). The sweep looks the same, but the box never clips to nothing. The old shimmer did at each sweep start, and Chrome then scores a skeleton moved by a panel above as a jump from the viewport's origin. With the covering box the same 36 px move scores 0.019 at every shimmer phase. Reduced motion still shows a static bar.
- **Phone skeleton rows:** below sm the host card's Run now and Open gaps bars are two lines tall (36 px) and the backups run-strip bar 32 px, as their fixture values wrap at 375 px. Other copy or widths may still move the card a little; the covering shimmer keeps that shift proportional.
- **Alternatives:** a wider fixed note (`md:w-48`) still depends on the font and overflows the Windows table; dropping "Missed" from the slot saves 12 px, not the 19–40 px Linux needs; NBSP and word-joiner characters inside the slot string change its text and accessible name; `background-position` shimmer animates paint, outside the transform/opacity rule; relaxing the 16 px line or the < 0.1 CLS assertions hides a real wrap and a real 0.15 jump.

### DEC-206 · Offline: mutations never wait for the connection; one shell banner; locked controls say why
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-134)** · aud-014 A14-03; DEC-185, DEC-197; vault Design/UI States (Offline (browser)), Design/Accessibility, Features/Data Health; changes no API
- **Cause:** every mutation used TanStack's default `networkMode: "online"`. While the browser was offline a mutation paused (pending, nothing sent), and `QueryClient` resumed paused mutations on reconnect, so a disable the owner cancelled offline was sent when the connection came back (aud-014 R8).
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Mutations** default to `networkMode: "always"` (`providers.tsx`): an offline mutation is attempted at once, fails into its existing error path, and is never resumed. **Queries keep `"online"`**: offline they pause, and on reconnect they refetch (reads are safe, and the banner says why nothing updates).
  - **(b) One banner** in the shell, under the top bar, while TanStack's `onlineManager` is offline (the same signal that pauses queries): "**You're offline.** Showing data as of <oldest active query's last fetch, UTC>. Refresh and actions are off until you reconnect." `role="status"`, warning tint layered over the page background, sticky under the top bar below `lg`.
  - **(c) One behaviour per control.** *Unavailable with the reason* "You're offline. Reconnect to use this.": Refresh, the source switches, Run now (after its other blockers, which still hold once reconnected) and Choose CSV… (and a drop) show it in their TapTooltip (hover, keyboard focus, tap); Import N rows shows it in its existing reason line under the button (`aria-describedby`). *Fail at once* into the existing error path: the Disable / Re-enable dialog's submit, if the dialog was opened before the connection dropped ("Couldn't disable CJ Dropshipping: you're offline. Try again once you reconnect."), and a check started by the Source select or by a file chooser opened before the drop (the check's error line with Retry). The row's Import CSV stays available: it only brings the panel into view.
  - **(d)** Locked Refresh, Run now and switches dim with tokens, not opacity, so the focus ring keeps 3:1 (DEC-185's rule; Refresh's ring in light went from 1.96:1 to 3.97:1). The switch track keys on `aria-checked`, because a tooltip trigger wrapping the switch replaces its `data-state`.
  - **(e)** `TapTooltip` is inert without a hint, so a control whose reason comes and goes stays the same element and keeps keyboard focus when the connection drops.
- **Alternatives:** every control fails at once (each press costs a round trip to learn the cause, and the transport copy blames the API or the PC); queries `"always"` too (the 5-minute refetch would put a stale line blaming the API on screen every 5 minutes offline); a `navigator.onLine` hook of our own (it can disagree with TanStack's pausing, which starts online and follows the window events); removing the paused mutation on Cancel (keeps the queue, and every other dialog would need the same); a service worker (none in v1, vault UI States).
- **Why:** nothing pressed offline is ever sent later (1 → 0 in the e2e), every offline state says why (0/3 → 3/3), and the owner learns it is offline without pressing anything. Web only.

### DEC-209 · Logging: every logger sits under `pulso`; the JSON allowlist grows one named key at a time
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-110)** · aud-013 F-16; SPEC §12 (structured JSON logs); vault Data/Sources/Source - CJ Dropshipping; changes no API, schema or dependency
- **Cause:** `configure_logging` attaches the JSON handler to the `pulso` logger only, with `propagate` off, and `JsonFormatter` copies only named `extra` keys. The CJ logger was `getLogger(__name__)`, which is `pulso_sources.cj`, outside that tree. So `cj_points_projection` printed through a root handler without its number, or printed nothing with no root handler (aud-013 F-16).
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** Loggers are named under `pulso.` explicitly (`pulso.sources.cj`), never `__name__`: our package names (`pulso_core`, `pulso_sources`, …) are not under `pulso`.
  - **(b)** `JsonFormatter` keeps its explicit allowlist of `extra` keys as the redaction boundary. A key is added only together with a call site that passes it; this adds `source` and `projected_points`.
  - **(c)** `collection_failed` drops `run_id`. `collect_run` sets `job_id` to the same id for its whole body, so the line already carries it.
- **Alternatives:** attach the handler to the root logger (third-party lines join the JSON stream, and a Celery worker replaces root handlers by default); copy every non-standard record attribute (any `extra` holding a body or a key would reach the logs); add `run_id` to the allowlist (duplicates `job_id`).
- **Why:** the projection is the check on CJ's daily points and must be visible before CJ joins the worker plans. `extra=` call sites that lose keys: 2/11 → 0/11.

### DEC-211 · CJ account-activity countdown over explicit evidence
**2026-10-24 · proposed (P1-08e)** · CJ-2; completes the DEC-116 split; DEC-095/136; vault Data/Sources/Source - CJ Dropshipping
- **(a) Evidence and countdown:** `pulso_sources.cj.activity.Evidence` is the only input: the account's latest positive-amount transaction as `kind` (`owner_entry` | `csv_export`), `transaction_at` and `observed_at` (aware, normalized to UTC, transaction not after observation), `amount_minor` (strict int > 0) and `currency` (three capitals). It is immutable and rejects extra fields, so a token or auth body never validates. `countdown(evidence, now)` returns `unknown` with no number when there is no evidence, or when it was observed after `now`. Otherwise `days_remaining` = 30 − UTC calendar days since the transaction: `ok` above `WARN_DAYS` (7), `warn` at 1–7, `expired` at 0 or less (API likely disabled, `1600014`). A naive clock raises.
- **Day count:** UTC calendar dates (AGENTS.md §3), so `expired` never comes later than 30 full days after the transaction in any timezone; CJ's own day boundary is unverified. Stale evidence only shortens the countdown, since a later transaction can only extend it.
- **(b) No persistence yet:** nothing could write evidence without an API, UI or CSV-template change, which this task excludes, and a `sources.config` writer must re-read the row under its lock (imp-149) with a PostgreSQL test. A later task stores `Evidence.model_dump(mode="json")` under `sources.config.cj_account_activity` (no migration) through that row-locked write, reads it back with `Evidence.model_validate` and treats invalid stored evidence as `unknown`, then shows the status on the CJ row in Data Health.
- **Alternatives:** deriving activity from token issuance, auth responses or API-created orders (none proves an account-wide transaction amount; DEC-116); elapsed 24-hour periods instead of UTC dates (breaks the UTC-day rule and can show one day more left than the UTC calendar count); persisting now with no caller (dead code plus a lock-sensitive write).
- **Why:** the owner runs CJ until the 30-day rule lapses it (DEC-136); a countdown from real evidence warns before the lapse, and without evidence Pulso shows no number rather than a guess. Fixtures only: no CJ request, migration, API shape, UI, scheduler or source change.

### DEC-214 · Lead lock: a holder whose Orca run has ended frees the lock at once
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-160)** · vault Agents/Improvement Backlog imp-160; Session Log 2026-10-24 "Lead reliability"; refines DEC-072's atomic lead lock; no new dependency; CLI unchanged
- **Cause:** a holder counted as dead only after 20 minutes without a beat. Run 65 (01:30Z) ended its turn at 01:42:05Z to wait for a background check. Orca marked the run `completed` at 01:44:05Z and closed its terminal, but the lock (last beat 01:41Z) still blocked the 02:00Z precheck, so run 66 was skipped. The recorded `pid` belongs to the short-lived `lead_lock.py` process, so it says nothing about the holder.
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** `acquire` records the holder's Orca automation run in `owner.json` as `automation` and `run`. That run is the `dispatched` one whose `terminalPaneKey` equals the caller's `ORCA_PANE_KEY` (or `ORCA_AGENT_PANE`) in `orca automations runs --limit 10 --json`. The lookup happens before the `mkdir`, so the lock dir never sits without an owner file while Orca answers. Nothing is recorded outside an automation run (an owner session, another pane) or when Orca can't be read.
  - **(b)** `check` (the automation precheck) and `acquire` treat the lock as free when `orca automations runs --id <automation> --limit 10 --json` lists the recorded run with a final status **and** an empty `terminalPtyId`. Final means Orca's own `isFinalAutomationRunStatus` set: `completed`, `dispatch_failed` and the `skipped_*` statuses. The empty `terminalPtyId` means Orca has also closed or dropped the run's terminal. A final run whose terminal is still open stays held. Orca marks a run `completed` once the agent's turn ends and its screen goes idle, and on 2026-10-23 run 52 woke up from a background task 47 s after `completed` and ran `beat` and `git push`.
  - **(c)** Every other answer keeps the 20-minute beat rule: `dispatched` or an unknown status; the run missing from the newest 10; `orca` missing, failing, timing out (20 s) or printing something unparseable; or no recorded run. A beat older than 20 minutes still frees the lock, even while Orca shows `dispatched`.
  - **(d)** `orca` is found on `PATH`, else at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\orca\resources\bin\orca.exe`. Orca's host service spawns the precheck with its own environment, which may lack Orca's bin directory.
  - **(e)** Known ceiling: if Orca drops a run's terminal while the agent process lives on, the lock frees while that agent could still be woken. An idle lead session from 2026-10-23 is still open in `pulso-lead` with no run record pointing at it. Such an agent's next `beat` fails once the new lead holds the lock, so it stops at its next step, the same as after a stale takeover today. Upgrade path: also record the agent's process (pid and start time) and require that it has exited.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Run status alone, as the backlog row suggested: it frees the lock while a finished run's agent can still act (run 52).
  - Recording a long-lived ancestor process and checking that it's alive: the precheck runs plain `python`, so this needs psutil or ctypes. Windows reuses pids, and an idle agent left open for hours still counts as alive.
  - A shorter stale timeout: a live lead doesn't beat during a 10-minute merge check.
- **Why:** Orca's run record says when a run is over. Runs 69 and 70 (2026-10-24) went `completed` with their terminal closed within 2 min 18 s and 9 s of finishing (a read-only poll every 20 s). After a dead holder, the next precheck now passes once Orca closes the holder's terminal, instead of 20 minutes after its last beat. Slots lost after a dead holder: 1–2 → 0.

### DEC-207 · Data Health: Open gaps read only the host call; a failed host call says the gap check didn't load
**2026-10-23 · proposed (imp-145)** · imp-145, imp-146; aud-014 A14-02 (`coverage.tsx:130`); DEC-166, DEC-196, DEC-197, DEC-198; vault Features/Data Health; changes no API
- **Cause:** Open gaps come from `GET /v1/admin/host`, but they rendered inside the Coverage heatmap's success branch. With `GET /v1/admin/coverage` loading, failed or empty, the list was gone, and with it the `#open-gaps` heading that the Overdue note links to (DEC-198): the row link did nothing and the drawer link returned focus to the opener. With only the host call failed, the list vanished without a line.
- **Decision:**
  - The Open gaps block sits below whatever the heatmap shows (skeleton, error, empty state or rows) and branches only on the host call (`openGapsView` in `model.ts`): host loading shows the open-gaps skeleton (imp-082's heading and two ranges); host data shows the list or "No open gaps in the last 90 days."; a host call that failed before any data shows one line.
  - **The line:** "**Gap check didn't load,** so open gaps can't be listed. Retry on the Collector host card." It reuses DEC-196's "Gap check didn't load" and next step, and the Unknown state's question-mark icon (muted). It has no Retry of its own: the host card carries the error and Retry (DEC-197, one line per failed request). It never reads as an empty list or as "no gaps".
  - Without source names (sources loading or failed) a range reads its source key. A failed host refetch keeps the cached gaps, as before.
- **Unchanged:** the Overdue and attention rules (imp-091, imp-093, imp-124, DEC-196), error counts, the heatmap's own states and the drawer's focus fallback to the opener when the heading is absent.
- **Alternatives:** keep the list inside the heatmap branch and repeat it in each error and empty branch (three copies); a Retry button on the line (a second Retry for one failed request, against DEC-197); hiding the line and relying on the host card (the list vanishes without a reason, aud-014 A14-02).

### DEC-213 · TapTooltip: a reason opened on focus survives the scroll its focus causes
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-145)** · imp-145 fix, imp-156; DEC-206 (c), (e); vault Features/Data Health; changes no API or dependency
- **Cause:** Radix Tooltip closes whenever an ancestor of its trigger scrolls. Focusing a control that is partly or fully out of view scrolls `main` (the lg scroll container), and that scroll's event lands after the focus has opened the reason, so the reason opened and closed within one frame. The trigger kept focus but lost its description. `offline.spec.ts:94` (Refresh at 1440) hit this whenever main's scroll landed after the focus: 4/4 for the lead on imp-145, and 2/2 here once the spec stopped scrolling first. The imp-145 code itself doesn't change the timing: main sits at the same scroll offsets (946, 95, 76 px) with or without it.
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** `TapTooltip` ignores a close that Radix requests from a scroll event (`window.event.type === "scroll"`) while its trigger is the focused element. Every other close stays as Radix has it: blur, Escape, a press, another tooltip opening, and a hover reason when the pointer leaves or the page scrolls under it.
  - **(b)** Its content gets `hideWhenDetached`: while the trigger is scrolled out of its scroll container, the reason is hidden (still the trigger's description) instead of floating over the top bar, and it shows again when the trigger is back.
  - **(c)** The offline e2e no longer scrolls a control into view before focusing it, and `data-health.spec.ts` focuses an off-screen locked Run now. Both fail without (a).
- **Alternatives:** keep scrolling first in the tests (the product bug stays: a keyboard user tabbing to an off-screen locked control loses the reason, imp-156); `preventScroll` on focus (a Tab focus can't take it, and the control would stay off-screen); our own capture-phase scroll listener that flags the close (needs to be registered before Radix's and cleared after the dispatch; more code for the same test); keep every reason open while focused (two tooltips at once when another one opens on hover).
- **Why:** focus-driven scroll closes a just-opened reason 1/1 → 0/1 (the new e2e), and the offline spec passes at 1440 and 375 with `--repeat-each=3 --retries=0` without any scroll-first step. `window.event` is legacy but specified by the DOM Standard and supported by every engine we target.

### DEC-216 · CJ activity evidence: owner entry, row-locked store and the Data Health countdown
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-161)** · completes DEC-211 b; DEC-136; imp-149 lock pattern; vault Data/Sources/Source - CJ Dropshipping, Features/Data Health
- **(a) Write:** `PUT /v1/admin/sources/cj/activity` (operation `put_cj_activity`, the same admin dependency as the other admin routes). The body `ActivityEntry` subclasses `Evidence`, so every Evidence check applies. `kind` can only be `owner_entry`, so token issuance, auth responses, API orders and `csv_export` all get a 422. `currency` defaults to `USD`. `observed_at` defaults to the server's UTC now, and a body value in the future gets a 422. The route stores `model_dump(mode="json")` under `sources.config.cj_account_activity`. It first refreshes the source `FOR UPDATE`, then changes only that key. A later entry replaces the stored one, so a mistyped order can be corrected. No migration, and no audit event.
- **(b) Read:** the `cj` item of `GET /v1/admin/sources` gains `activity` `{state, days_remaining, as_of (transaction_at), observed_at}`, computed with `countdown(Evidence.model_validate(stored), now)`. Missing or invalid stored evidence reads `unknown` with every other field null, never an error and never 0. Other sources omit the key (`exclude_if`), so it is optional in the OpenAPI schema.
- **(c) Display:** the CJ row (the table's Status cell and the phone card) shows two lines. The first is the countdown in words with an icon: "No evidence yet", "N days left" (`ok` in the neutral tone, `warn` in warning), or "Expired · −N days". The second is "Last order <UTC date>" (the evidence's as-of, with full times and source on hover) and **Add order** or **Update order**. The dialog takes the order's date and time read as UTC (`max` is the time it opened), the amount, and a currency that defaults to USD. The amount is converted to minor units with the currency's own decimals (`Intl`), and a positive amount is required. Entering an order takes 2 clicks: Add order, then Save order.
- **Alternatives:** a body schema separate from `Evidence` (duplicates its checks, which could drift); returning `activity: null` for other sources (the brief asks for the key to be omitted); a new Data Health column (wide for one source); showing the date as "as of" right after the countdown (reads as "N days left as of <date>", when the days are counted from now).
- **Why:** before this, the countdown had no writer or reader, so the CJ row could never warn before the 30-day `1600014` lapse. CJ rows showing an evidence-based activity status: 0 → 1/1. Fixtures only: no CJ request.

### DEC-217 · Data Health Retry: focus follows a pressed Retry to its outcome; only its failure is announced
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-135)** · aud-014 A14-04; DEC-145; vault Design/Accessibility (live regions), Features/Data Health; web only, no API, dependency or copy change
- **Cause:** a query with no data goes back to `pending` while it refetches, so a panel's skeleton replaces its `PanelError`, Retry included, and focus falls to `<body>`. The API-down page goes the same way: both core calls are pending under "Refreshing…". Neither error had a live region. Measured on `168b413`, 4 of 4 page/panel presses left focus on `<body>` and 0 outcomes were announced. aud-014 counted 3 of 4 on `50293f8`; with `retry: 1`, the panel error now also leaves during the retry delay.
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** A `Panel`, and the page's API-down error with its stale line, is a retry scope (`data-health/retry.ts`). A press on a `PanelError` or `StaleLine` Retry is followed until every query settles: TanStack `useIsFetching`, counted only while a press is followed. Offline counts as not settled, because a paused fetch hasn't run.
  - **(b)** A press failed if, once settled, the pressed error is still shown (a stale line) or an error appeared since the press (a panel error that left while refreshing). That error's Retry takes the lost focus, and its sr-only `role="alert"` reads "<title or what>. <detail>" once. Otherwise the press restored its scope, and the scope's heading takes the lost focus: the Panel `h2` (`tabIndex=-1`, global focus ring, the Open gaps heading's scroll margin). The page-level scope uses the first panel's heading, Collector host. Success is announced only by that focus move.
  - **(c)** "Lost" means `document.body`, as in DEC-145. `useTakeLostFocus` moves to `retry.ts` and the import panel shares it. Focus the user moved elsewhere is left alone. An error already shown before the press belongs to another call and never counts as the failure.
  - **(d)** Refetches nobody pressed Retry for (the 5-minute interval, tab refocus, Refresh) never reach a scope: no alert and no focus move. The run drawer's errors have no scope, because Radix keeps focus in the dialog (aud-014: acceptable).
- **Alternatives:**
  - Keep each error mounted while it retries. That changes every panel's pending branch, in files other tasks own.
  - Move focus to the heading at press time. Success would go unannounced, and a failure would come back after focus had left.
  - Decide from the refetch promise. It settles before TanStack's batched notification re-renders the panel, so the new error may not be on screen yet.
  - A polite "Coverage loaded". It would be read twice, along with the focus move.
- **Why:** presses that leave focus on `<body>` went from 4 of 4 to 0 of 4 (e2e at 1440 and 375). Pressed failures announced went from 0 to 1 each; background refetches stay at 0 announcements and 0 focus moves (asserted).

### DEC-218 · Data Health: open gaps wait for source names; the host-failed line leads with its label and links to the host card's Retry
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-152)** · imp-152, imp-158, imp-159 (imp-163, imp-164 duplicates); p1-53 report leftover 1; imp-145 report leftovers 1–2; DEC-196, DEC-197, DEC-207; vault Features/Data Health; changes no API or dependency
- **Cause:** when `GET /host` landed before `GET /sources`, the host card's latest-gap line and the Open gaps table read a source key ("latest google_trends"), then rewrapped when the names arrived (first-load CLS 0.072 at 375 for that order, 1/1 key-named renders). With the host call failed, the Open gaps line led with its cause, so a scan found the section name mid-sentence (0/1 label-first), and "Retry on the Collector host card" pointed at a card far above at 375 with no link (scroll + 1 click).
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** While sources is loading, the host card's Open gaps fact (when there are gaps) and the Open gaps block (when there are ranges) keep their skeletons, sized like the loaded line (one line, two on a phone). If sources fails, ranges read keys as before (DEC-207): the key is the only name there is. This narrows DEC-207's "without source names (sources loading or failed) a range reads its source key" to "sources failed".
  - **(b)** The host-failed line reads "**Open gaps** · Gap check didn't load. Retry on the Collector host card ↑", with the loaded heading's "·" separator, the Unknown icon kept in front.
  - **(c)** "Collector host card" is an in-page link (`HostRetryLink`, `href="#host-title"`, an up arrow like GapsLink's down arrow). Following it (click, tap or Enter) focuses the host card's Retry; focus scrolls it into view, clear of the phone's top bar. It prevents the fragment jump for the same reason as GapsLink (imp-124). It renders only while the host call has failed, so the Retry exists; it finds it by its label inside the host card's section, so PanelError takes no new prop.
  - **(d)** The Unknown pill's hint (DEC-196) stays plain text in its tooltip: a `role="tooltip"` holds no control (ARIA APG), TapTooltip's content is click-through so an open hint never covers a control, and it closes on blur, so a link inside it could not be reached by keyboard. The persistent note under the pill, "Gap check didn't load", is the same `HostRetryLink` instead (accessible name "Gap check didn't load (retry on the Collector host card)"), in the table row and the phone card. Under a coarse pointer at md and up it sits 16 px lower, as GapsLink does, so its 44 px hit square clears the pill's.
- **Alternatives:** a name-independent phrasing ("latest Oct 22, 10:00 UTC", loses which source); a Retry on the line itself (a second Retry for one failed request, against DEC-197); a link to the card heading (a second Tab or click to reach Retry); an interactive tooltip or a toggletip popover for the hint (a control in a tooltip, and about 30 lines of focus handling in the TapTooltip every reason uses).
- **Why:** 375 first-load CLS for host-before-sources 0.072 → 0.000 and key-named renders 1/1 → 0 (`host-before-sources.spec.ts`); label-first 0/1 → 1/1; host Retry reached in 1 click or 1 Enter with focus on it at 1440 and 375, from the Open gaps line (`open-gaps-host.spec.ts`) and from the Unknown note (`host-unknown.spec.ts`).

### DEC-220 · Offline: a Data Health Retry is locked like Refresh; an import that fails offline names the connection
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-153)** · imp-153, imp-157 (imp-134 report leftovers 1 and 5); DEC-185, DEC-206 (a), (c), DEC-217 (a), (d); vault Design/UI States (Offline (browser)), Features/Data Health; web only, no API, schema or dependency change
- **Cause:** (1) The import panel's error line used its transport copy for every failure without an API answer, so a check or import cut off by the connection, or a check the Source select started offline, said "The API didn't answer…" under the offline banner. (2) Queries keep `networkMode: "online"` (DEC-206 a), so a `StaleLine` or `PanelError` Retry pressed offline started a paused refetch that DEC-217 (a) followed until reconnect, with nothing on screen meanwhile.
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** The import panel reads `onlineManager.isOnline()` when a check or import fails (its mutations' `onError`, as `actionFailure` does) and keeps that cause after reconnecting. Offline, the line reads "You're offline. Retry once you reconnect." (an import adds "; a retry never imports the same file twice."), and its alert drops "Select Retry to try again.", which the line already says. A failure with an API answer, or one while online, reads as before.
  - **(b)** Offline, the Retry of a `StaleLine` and of a `PanelError` is locked like Refresh (DEC-206 c): `aria-disabled` and still focusable, dimmed with tokens (DEC-185; a primary Retry takes the secondary tokens, like Import N rows), with "You're offline. Reconnect to use this." in its TapTooltip. A press only shows the reason. It starts no refetch, so no scope follows it (DEC-217 a), nothing is announced and focus stays on Retry. DEC-217 (a)'s offline clause now covers only a press made online before the connection dropped. The reconnect refetch is TanStack's, unchanged and silent (DEC-217 d). The run drawer's errors get the same lock.
