- **Alternatives:** an inline "You're offline" message beside an enabled Retry, with the press still followed through the reconnect (a second kind of announcement beside DEC-217's failure-only alert, and a Retry that looks available under a banner that says refresh is off); reading the online state at render time (a check that failed offline would turn into an API failure on reconnect); locking the import line's Retry too (offline it fails at once and says so, DEC-206 c).
- **Why:** offline import failures that blame the API 1 → 0, and offline Retry presses with no feedback 1 → 0 on both the stale line and the panel error (`offline-feedback.spec.ts`, 1440 and 375). Every offline control now behaves one of DEC-206's two ways: unavailable with its reason, or failing at once and naming the connection.

### DEC-221 · Import claims: a stale claim ends as `failed/abandoned` under the source lock
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-104)** · aud-009 F-12; narrows DEC-092's in-progress skip and DEC-108's "recovery alone abandons"; DEC-098 threshold; DEC-140; vault Features/CSV Export & Import
- **Decision:** a retry (`commit_import` on an existing capture) and a replay (`replay_import`) already hold the source row `FOR UPDATE` while they look for a queued/running run on the capture. In that transaction they first end each such run that is an import (`trigger = 'csv'`) and has reached the stale threshold. The run gets the end fields `recover_source` writes (DEC-098): `status failed`, `complete false`, `finished_at` now, and `abandoned` as `error_code` and `error_message`. The abandonment commits together with the new claim. Retry and replay then reuse the stored capture, with no second capture and no new S3 PUT. A younger claim still returns `skipped` / `in_progress`, as before.
- **Threshold:** no new setting. DEC-098's rule: the greater of 2 h or twice the run's budget, measured from `started_at`, falling back to `queued_at`. Imports run on `execute_run`'s default budget, `COLLECTOR_WALL_TIME_SECONDS` (45 min), so the threshold is 2 h by default. `stale_after` lives in `pulso_workers`, which packages must not import.
- **Scope:** only import runs linked to this capture. A replay of a collector capture never abandons a scheduled or catch-up run from the API; `recover_source` stays the only path for those, under the free execution lock. No schema change and no migration.
- **Known ceiling:** unlike recovery, which first checks that the execution lock is free, the API has no liveness check for an import. An import still executing more than 2 h after it started could finish after it was abandoned and overwrite its own end fields. Its observations dedupe on `(entity_id, raw_payload_id)`, so the retry and the straggler write each row once. Upgrade path: a heartbeat in the import run's params, if imports ever run that long.
- **Alternatives:** moving `stale_after` into `pulso_sources` (touches `pulso_workers`, outside this task); a separate import timeout setting (a second knob for the same rule); a literal `abandoned` status (the API's and the web's status vocabularies don't know it, and recovery already writes `failed/abandoned`); waiting for `recover_source` (it runs only where `PULSO_SCHEDULER=on`, so a claim on Railway or a dev API never clears).
- **Why:** a 30-day-old claim blocked 1/1 retries and 1/1 replays on SQLite and PostgreSQL 18; now 0/1 each, with one capture and one S3 PUT in total. A claim started under 2 h ago still blocks 1/1. Tests for an old claim: 0 → 8 (stale and fresh, retry and replay, both dialects).

### DEC-222 · Inline text links: a 1 px ring offset and side margin keep the focus ring off the words beside them
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-169)** · imp-169; imp-152 report leftover (styleseed −2 polish); DEC-218 (c); vault Design/Accessibility (Visible focus); changes no API or dependency
- **Cause:** the one global ring (`:focus-visible`, 2 px outline at 2 px offset) reaches 4 px past a control. Beside an inline link that is more than a space (3.16 px at 13 px Geist), so on the Open gaps line the ring of "Collector host card" overlapped "the" by 0.84 px and the "." after it by 4 px (`inline-link-ring.spec.ts` on the old code).
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** A shared utility, `link-inline` (globals.css), for links inside running copy: `margin-inline` 2 px (`--spacing(0.5)`) and, on `:focus-visible`, a 1 px `outline-offset`. The ring keeps its token, its 2 px width and `outline` (so forced colors still draw it); only its geometry changes, and only on links that carry the class. Buttons, inputs and every other control keep the global ring. Padding would not help: the outline sits outside the padding box, so only space outside the link (margin) or a tighter offset moves the ring away from its neighbours.
  - **(b)** Punctuation straight after an inline link has no space to absorb the ring, so that call site adds `me-1` (4 px end margin), as the Open gaps line does for its ".".
  - **(c)** `HostRetryLink`'s up arrow trims its glyph's 2 px side bearings with `viewBox="4 0 16 24"` in a 2 × 3 box (`ml-1` keeps the arrow where it was), so the link's edge, and the ring, sit on the arrow as they sit on the text. Without the trim the arrow's built-in space would sit inside the ring and push the "." 2 px further away at rest. A negative margin on the icon instead makes Chromium draw a broken outline round the overflowing icon.
- **Alternatives:** a 0 px offset with no margin (no layout change, but the ring hugs the glyphs and the "." still overlaps); a 3 px symmetric margin (clears both sides, but reads as a double space before the link); a background-fill focus style for inline links (GOV.UK-style; a second focus language next to the green ring); a global rule for `p a` (would also reach button-styled links inside paragraphs).
- **Why:** ring to "the" −0.84 → +2.16 px at 1440 (at 375 the link wraps to the next line); ring to "." −4 → +1 px at 1440 and 375; a Button's and the source picker's ring unchanged (2 px solid at 2 px offset, no margin), all asserted in `inline-link-ring.spec.ts`. At rest the line gains 2 px on each side of the link ("the" to "Collector", and the arrow to the "."): the 4 px end margin less the 2 px trimmed from the arrow.

### DEC-223 · Data Health attention: an expired CJ activity countdown counts; warn does not
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-166)** · imp-161 report leftover; narrows nothing in DEC-096's states, adds one input to its badge rule; DEC-216; DEC-196 floors; vault Features/Data Health; web only, no API change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Expired counts.** A source needs attention when its state is Blocked, Failing or Overdue (DEC-096), **or** it is enabled and its `activity.state` is `expired`. `attentionCount` applies the rule, so the Sources panel's "N need attention" note and the DH-3 nav badge (sidebar, tablet Open menu, phone More) both include it. A source is counted once, even when it is also Blocked, Failing or Overdue.
  - **(b) Warn doesn't count.** It is a heads-up already on the row: an amber "N days left" with its hint. *ok* and *unknown* (no evidence) don't count either.
  - **(c) A disabled source doesn't count**, even when expired. DEC-096 keeps Disabled out of the badge because the owner chose it, and a disabled CJ runs no collection that the lapse could stop. The row still shows "Expired · −N days" in red. Re-enabling it would surface the problem as Failing or Blocked on the next run (`1600014`).
  - **(d) Floors (DEC-196):** without the gap summary, the count reads "at least" only when an *uncounted* source could still turn Overdue. An expired source is already counted, so the gap summary can't add it.
  - **(e) Reason on the row:** a counted CJ keeps its run-based pill (for example Healthy, because the last run succeeded) and, directly under it, the red "Expired · −N days" line with its icon and hint (DEC-216 c), in the table and in the phone card. A counted source never shows a Healthy pill with no visible reason.
- **Alternatives:** counting *warn* too (the badge would light for up to 7 days before anything stops working, and DEC-096 keeps the badge to "act now"); counting expired on a disabled source (counts a lapse that stops nothing until the owner re-enables it); a new "Lapsed" pill state (needs `atoms.tsx`'s `StatePill`, which another branch owns, and the pill describes the last run, which did succeed); counting it only in the panel note (the badge and the note must agree, DEC-096).
- **Why:** an expired countdown means CJ has likely turned its API off (`1600014`), so CJ collection stops working: real attention. Expired or warn CJ states with a recorded decision went from 0/2 to 2/2. Expired is now counted in the panel and in the badge (e2e `cj-attention.spec.ts`: 1 with every other source healthy, 3 beside the blocked and overdue fixtures, 0 for warn and for a disabled CJ), and Vitest covers each state.

### DEC-210 · Collector `status` reports read-only Data Health from the worker image
**2026-10-23 · proposed (data-05)** · task_d484fd9171ce; Lead Playbook §6 steps 4 and 6; vault Platform/Collector Host; builds on DEC-131; protected path, owner merge
- **Decision:** `status` (still guarded, locked and journaled) gains `data_health`. It is `null` unless collector Postgres is running and has an Alembic revision, so a stopped or never-migrated database prints `null`, as `alembic_revision` already does. One `docker compose … run --rm --no-deps -T worker python -m infra.scripts.collector.sources health` container computes it, and its first statement is `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, so any write fails. No new op, migration, schema change or dependency. The API's Data Health helpers are not in the worker image, so the module runs a few plain SELECTs.
- **Fields (JSON, codes, counts and UTC times only):** `as_of`; `heartbeats`, the newest 5 hosts, each with its latest `at` and `age_seconds`; `sources`, every row ordered by key (including `backup`), each with `key`, `enabled`, `status`, `retry_not_before`, `latest_run` (`status`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `error_code` of the highest run id), `runs_24h` (count by status, by `queued_at`), `open_gaps` (unfilled gap rows) and `newest_open_gap` (`slot_key`, `slot_start`, `slot_end`, `reason`). Only these columns are selected: never `config` (seed entries), run `params`, `error_message`, `disabled_reason`, payloads or settings.
- **Defaults:** (a) at most 5 heartbeat hosts, because each rebuilt ops container reports a new hostname. (b) The 24 h window uses `queued_at`, which is always set (`started_at` is null for a queued run). (c) Fail closed: if the container step fails, `status` fails (exit 1, journaled) rather than print a report without `data_health`. The worker image gains `health` only when `update` rebuilds it after this merges; until then `status` fails while Postgres is up, so run `update` first.
- **Alternatives:** a new op (the task prefers `status`; the guards would be the same); psql in the Postgres container, like the Alembic revision (SQL and JSON assembly inside the host script); a partial report when the container fails (it would have to tell a `.collector-hold` refusal from a container failure, and a missing `data_health` could be read as healthy).
- **Why:** Lead Playbook §6 needs a fresh ops heartbeat and real source outcomes after every collector-host change and before any enable. The collector project runs no API, so `status` is the only sanctioned view, and FX enablement waits on it.

### DEC-225 · Run drawer Retry: the drawer is a retry scope; focus Radix parks on the dialog counts as lost
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-168)** · imp-168 (imp-135 report leftover); narrows DEC-217 (c) and (d); DEC-220 (b) unchanged; vault Design/Accessibility (live regions), Features/Data Health; web only, no API, schema, dependency or copy change
- **Cause:** DEC-217 (d) gave the run drawer no retry scope, because Radix keeps focus in the dialog. A pressed "Couldn't load runs" or "Couldn't load targets" Retry that failed again was announced 0 times. Focus stayed on the dialog container, 3 Tabs (runs) or 4 Tabs (targets) away from the new Retry. A restoring press also left focus on the container. Measured at 1440 and 375 on `0c9fd23`.
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** The drawer body is a retry scope (DEC-217 a, b). Its restored target is the drawer title: the `h2`, with `tabIndex=-1`, `rounded-sm` and the global focus ring. After a failed press, the new Retry takes the focus and its alert reads once. After a press that restores, the title takes it. The scope covers the drawer's two `PanelError`s and two `StaleLine`s.
  - **(b)** "Lost" (DEC-217 c) now also means a focused dialog that contains the target. When a focused control is removed, Radix's FocusScope moves focus to the dialog container instead of leaving it on `<body>`. A target outside the focused dialog never takes that focus, so nothing behind the modal can pull it out. On open the drawer focuses itself (imp-073), but only a followed press moves focus from there.
  - **(c)** Unchanged: refetches nobody pressed Retry for stay silent (DEC-217 d), the offline lock (DEC-220 b), and Load more targets' own alert.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Add only the alert and leave focus on the container. Every repeat would then cost a 3–4 Tab walk back to Retry, unlike every other Retry.
  - A drawer-only option on `useRetryScope`: a second "lost" rule for one caller, where the shared rule is one condition.
  - Focus the first restored control (the newest run's toggle, or the targets table). That needs a target per error, and a toggle's name doesn't say the drawer is back.
- **Why:** pressed drawer failures announced went from 0 to 1 per press, for runs and targets at 1440 and 375, and a second failed press announces again. Refetch announcements stay at 0. After a restoring press, focus lands on the drawer title instead of the container. A repeat press after a failure takes 0 Tabs instead of 3 or 4 (`retry-focus.spec.ts`).

### DEC-226 · Review cleanup: one JWKS fetch per outage window, Shopify IDs as canonical text, the web's currency list mirrors the API's
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-151)** · imp-151 (XR-3 to XR-9); DEC-051; DEC-216; imp-108 (`to_minor` BIGINT bound); vault Platform/Auth, Features/Data Health, Features/CSV Export & Import; no migration, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) JWKS failure cooldown (XR-6).** `JWKSClient` (a `PyJWKClient` subclass in `pulso_api.auth`) overrides `fetch_data`. After a failed fetch (a connection error, a reset or TLS error while the body is read, or a body that isn't JSON), every fetch in the next 5 s raises `PyJWKClientConnectionError` at once, which answers 503 `auth_unavailable`. The check runs inside `fetch_data`, so inside PyJWT's client lock: requests that queued behind the failing fetch answer at once instead of each waiting out their own 3 s timeout. A cached key set is still served first. A successful fetch is unaffected, and a valid but unusable answer (`{"keys": []}`) stays 401 and does not start the cooldown.
  - **(b) Shopify IDs (XR-4).** `product_id` and `variant_id` get an `id` column kind: `^[1-9][0-9]{0,18}$` and below 2^63, kept as text. Text keeps the commit path's string identity (`shop:12`) and stays exact past JavaScript's 2^53 in the preview JSON. A CJ `moq` of 0 is a row error in `parse_csv`, the same way `max_days < min_days` is (XR-3). Template ids and `csv-v1` are unchanged.
  - **(c) Currencies (XR-7).** `Evidence` and `ActivityEntry` accept only `pulso_core.money.CURRENCY_EXPONENTS` and `amount_minor` below 2^63. The web keeps a hand-copied `CURRENCY_DIGITS` (`model.ts`) of the same 8 currencies and ISO decimals. `toMinor` refuses any other currency, so the browser's ICU decimals never decide minor units, and the order dialog names the list on the Currency field before anything is sent.
- **Ceiling (c):** the web copy can drift from `CURRENCY_EXPONENTS`. If it does, the API still answers 422 for a currency it doesn't list. Upgrade path: serve the list from the API (a field on `SourceHealth` or `GET /v1/currencies`) once a second screen needs it.
- **Alternatives:** (a) correcting the Auth note's ceiling text only (leaves an n × 3 s queue during an outage); a cooldown check before `get_signing_key_from_jwt` (outside the lock, so requests already queued would still fetch one by one). (b) An integer `id` kind (an `int` loses precision in the web's JSON above 2^53, and the commit path compares IDs as text). (c) Leaving the browser's decimals and relying on the API's 422 alone (a second round trip, and the amount message would blame the amount).
- **Why:** preview/commit agreement on `moq=0` and `product_id`/`variant_id` of `abc`/`0`/`0012`: 0/4 → 4/4. Concurrent JWKS outage requests: 4 queued calls → 1 fetch, all 503 (`test_jwks_outage_answers_queued_requests_from_one_fetch`).

### DEC-227 · Data Health: one failed sources call is one error; panels that reuse it wait in one line
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-139)** · aud-014 A14-08; extends DEC-197's "one line per failed request" to the first load; DEC-196, DEC-218; vault Features/Data Health, Design/UI States (Partial); web only, no API change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One error.** When `GET /v1/admin/sources` fails before any data, only the Sources panel shows the error (PanelError: title, Retry, error ID, Copy error ID, unchanged). Coverage and CSV import read the same call and show no error, Retry or ID of their own. The Sources Retry restores all three.
  - **(b) Coverage keeps its own rows.** Without the source list, the rows are the coverage call's own keys in its key order (the API already leaves internal sources out), under one line: "**Names wait for the source list above.** Rows show source keys, and sources with no runs yet aren't listed." Named rows, in the source list's order, come back with the list. If the coverage call failed too, Coverage shows its own error: two failed requests, two errors.
  - **(c) CSV import waits.** It can't offer a source, a template or an import without the list, so its body is one line: "**Waits for the source list above.** Retry there to choose a source and import."
  - **(d) One shared line.** Both use `WaitsLine` (`atoms.tsx`), set like the Open gaps line that waits on the host card (DEC-218): muted, with the Unknown state's question-mark icon and the lead in the foreground colour. Neither is a live region, so a refetch that fails again says nothing.
  - **(e) No link to the Sources Retry.** The Open gaps line links to the host card's Retry (imp-159) because that card sits far above it. The failed Sources panel sits directly above Coverage, and each link would add a Tab stop.
- **Alternatives:** keeping both PanelErrors without their Retry and ID (still three error blocks for one failure); hiding Coverage's rows behind the line (hides data its own call loaded); a link to the Sources Retry in each line (focus in 1 tap, but 2 more Tab stops); casting key-only objects to `SourceHealth` to reuse `coverageRows` (a type lie that would hide later changes to what it reads).
- **Why:** aud-014 R3a measured 3 error blocks, 3 Retry, 3 Copy error ID and the same ID 3 times for one 503, in 4/4 width and theme combos. Now there is 1 of each (`one-sources-error.spec.ts`, 1440 and 375, dark and light). Tab stops with sources at 503: 40 → 34 at 1440 and 19 → 13 at 375 (11 → 5 inside `main`). Each removed block had 3 stops: Retry, the selectable error ID and Copy. Recovery stays 1 click.

### DEC-229 · Dialog exit at `fast` with `ease.in`; a locked Button has no press scale
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-177)** · imp-177 (imp-165 report leftover), imp-141 (imp-095 report leftover); vault Design/Motion System (pattern 9, Micro-interactions); web only, no API, schema, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Dialog exit.** `DialogContent` keeps its enter (`base`, fade and zoom from 96%) and exits at `fast` with the `ease-in` token. It uses the classes `SheetContent` already has: `data-[state=open]:duration-(--duration-base) data-[state=closed]:duration-(--duration-fast) data-[state=closed]:ease-in`. The scrim was already `fast` both ways, with no easing class (as on the Sheet), and is unchanged. No Dialog or Sheet caller sets its own duration or easing: sources-table's two dialogs, and the run-drawer and mobile-nav sheets. The command palette runs on Motion and already exits at `fast` with `ease.in`.
  - **(b) Left as they are:** the panel's 96% zoom both ways, and the enter's CSS default `ease`. Row 9 asks for 0.97 on enter, 0.98 on exit and `ease.out` on enter. The brief keeps the enter unchanged, so these go to the backlog.
  - **(c) Locked press.** The Button base adds `disabled:active:scale-100` and `aria-disabled:active:scale-100`. Their selector has three parts, so it outranks every two-part `active:scale-*`: the base's 0.98, the icon sizes' 0.94 and any caller's own. One guard in the base covers every size and caller. A locked Button with `aria-disabled:pointer-events-auto` (so its reason shows) still takes the press, its focus and its tooltip; only the scale is gone. Enabled Buttons are unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** guard the press class itself (`not-aria-disabled:not-disabled:active:scale-[0.98]`, repeated on each icon size, and a caller's own press scale would still apply); `aria-disabled:active:scale-100` at each locked call site (every new locked Button would need it too); moving the dialog to Motion like the palette (a rewrite for a timing fix).
- **Why:** the dialog exit went from 200 to 120 ms, and its easing from `ease` to `cubic-bezier(0.7, 0, 0.84, 0)`. The enter is still 200 ms with `ease`. A locked Button's press scale went from 0.98 to 1.0 for md and sm (Run now) and from 0.94 to 1.0 for the icon size. Enabled Buttons still press to 0.98 and 0.94. All asserted in e2e `press-and-exit.spec.ts`.

### DEC-230 · FX: each adapter check names its error_code; a row dated after the capture's UTC day is skipped from that capture
**2026-10-24 · proposed (data-06)** · data-06 (first live FX run 2026-10-24 18:50Z: `partial`, `ValueError`); DEC-031, DEC-088, DEC-160, D4; AGENTS.md §3 (observation rows, history); vault Data/Sources/Source - Frankfurter FX, Features/Data Health; no migration, dependency, CI, schedule, rate-limit, block-rule or framework change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Error codes.** A run records only `type(exc).__name__` as `error_code` and `error_message`, and the framework stays unchanged. Each explicit check in `pulso_sources.fx_frankfurter` now raises its own `ValueError` subclass: `FxNotRateArray` (body is not a JSON array), `FxDuplicateRow` (one pair/date twice in a capture), `FxCaptureMetadata` (naive capture time or bad parser version), `FxUnsupportedRequest` (a non-blended request param) and `FxBackfillRange` (`backfill` start after end). A bad row stays `ValidationError` and unparseable JSON, NDJSON included, stays `JSONDecodeError`. A bare `ValueError` on an FX run therefore no longer comes from an FX check: it comes from raw storage (`store_capture` on the second request, `read_capture`) or the framework.
  - **(b) Next-day rows.** `upsert` skips a row dated after the capture's UTC date: the row isn't stored, the capture isn't failed, and every other row of the capture is stored. The raw capture keeps the row as evidence. A later capture stores it, either the next run's latest or its backfill from the stored watermark. The skip is counted in the run row as `rows_fetched − rows_inserted − rows_unchanged` (FX never updates). The rule applies to CSV imports too, where `parse_csv` already rejects dates after the UTC capture date as row errors, so imports behave as before.
  - **(c) Other shapes keep failing.** An object body or duplicate pair/date rows still fail the capture, now as `FxNotRateArray` or `FxDuplicateRow`. The docs (checked 2026-10-24) say `/v2/rates` "returns a flat array, one row per quote". They say NDJSON comes only when the `Accept` header asks for it, and the adapter sends `application/json`. They document no other shape, so the adapter parses no other shape.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Store the next-day row as published. DEC-031 would still hold: `rate_as_of(d)` needs `date ≤ d` and `captured_at < d+1`. But a blend for d+1 made on day d has only the providers that have already published d+1. FX keeps the first rate per date and pair (DEC-088), so that early blend would become permanent history, and the full blend would only add an `fx_conflict` event.
  - Store it under the capture date. That fabricates a date.
  - Keep rejecting it. Every run after a provider publishes ahead fails, the latest request never stores its same-day rows, and the run never reaches `success`.
  - An `fx_future_dated` source event per capture. That adds a row per run, and `collector status` shows neither the event nor the run's row counts.
- **Why:** the first run's shape (backfill 2025-01-01..2026-10-23, then latest at 18:50Z) runs through `execute_run` with a fake fetch, a frozen clock and in-memory storage (`test_fx_run.py`). Before the change, a latest body with one row dated 2026-10-25 gave `partial` / `ValueError`, the live result. So did an object body and a duplicate row. After it, the next-day row gives `success` / null with 4 of 5 rows stored and 1 skipped. The object body gives `FxNotRateArray`, the duplicate `FxDuplicateRow`, an empty parser version `FxCaptureMetadata` and a `providers` param `FxUnsupportedRequest`, each with the backfill rows kept where a backfill ran.
- **Ceiling:** same-day rows from the 17:00Z run can also be early blends if a provider publishes later that UTC day. First-rate-wins keeps them, as before. That is out of scope here, and a backlog row is proposed in `docs/reports/data-06.md`.

### DEC-228 · Run drawer: a never-run source's "CSV import panel" is one press to the panel on that source
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-172)** · imp-172 (imp-127 report leftover); imp-128 copy rule unchanged; reuses `ImportPanelHandle.open` (imp-081, DEC-175); DEC-222 (b, c); imp-165's exit rule; vault Features/Data Health; web only, no API, schema, dependency or copy change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Where it shows.** The words "CSV import panel" become the button wherever the never-run copy already names them: only when `csv_import === "ready"` (imp-128), alone or after "Press Run now…". That is a subset of the Sources row's rule (`importEntry(...).available`). A preview-only source keeps today's text with no CSV mention: a check adds no run, and the empty state is about a first run. A source with no template keeps today's text.
  - **(b) A button styled as a link.** It looks like the drawer's Open gaps link: border-strong underline, a trimmed down arrow (DEC-222 c), `link-inline`, and `me-1` before the Run-now variant's comma (DEC-222 b). It is a `<button>`, not an anchor, because it changes the panel's Source as well as moving focus. Its name is its visible words.
  - **(c) Hand-off after the exit.** The press only records the key and closes the drawer. `open(key)` runs from the drawer's `onCloseAutoFocus`, where the Open gaps link's focus already goes. By then the modal's page-wide `aria-hidden` and scroll lock are gone. Focus lands where `open(key)` puts it for the Sources row: Choose CSV…, or "Switch to …" when a file is open for another source (DEC-175). `open(key)` is unchanged.
  - **(d) No empty exit.** `RunDrawer` keeps the last source it showed until the exit ends. This is imp-165's `useLastOpen` rule, written locally because `sources-table.tsx` was off-limits. It applies to every way out: Escape, ×, Open gaps and this button.
- **Alternatives:** an anchor to the panel (the fragment can't pick the source, and the press changes form state); a separate "Import CSV" action button under the copy (a second control saying what the sentence already says); calling `open(key)` on the press (focus and scroll would move while the page behind was still aria-hidden and scroll-locked); showing it for preview-only sources too (imp-128 keeps a check out of the first-run copy).
- **Why:** from the drawer to the panel's file chooser took close + scroll + 1 click, now 1 click. By keyboard it took Escape + 3 Tabs + Enter on the row's Import, or Escape + 8 Tabs, now 2 Tabs + Enter, at 1440 and 375. Before, every Escape exit drew only empty frames (FX 5/5, CJ 8/8). Now it draws 0 empty frames, and the import exit keeps the title on every frame (`drawer-import.spec.ts`).
### DEC-232 · TapTooltip: a press opens or keeps the reason; one reason at a time; Retry's reason sits below
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-173)** · imp-173, imp-155, imp-175 (imp-153 report leftovers 1 and 3, imp-134 report leftover 3); DEC-206 (c), DEC-213, DEC-220 (b); vault Features/Data Health; web only, no API, schema, dependency or CI change
- **Cause:**
  - (1) A press toggled the reason, so Enter or Space on a focused locked control, or a click after the 400 ms hover, closed the reason that focus or hover had just opened. Radix closes a tooltip on a press of its trigger twice over: in the trigger's `pointerdown` handler, and in the content's dismissable layer, which counts the trigger as outside (on `pointerdown` for a mouse, on the `click` for touch). The old toggle read the state from before that `pointerdown`.
  - (2) A press set `open` directly. Every open that goes through Radix sends a `tooltip.open` event on `document`, and every open tooltip closes on it; a press-open never sent it. A pointer press already closed another open reason (its dismissable layer, or the blur), so the gap was the keyboard: Enter or Space on a focused control while another reason was open on hover left both open.
  - (3) The Retry reason opened above the button. On a panel error the Error ID line sits right above Retry at every width; on a stale line it wraps above Retry at phone width.
- **Decision:**
  - **(a)** A press opens or keeps the reason and never closes it, at every `TapTooltip`: locked controls and status hints alike. `onOpenChange` ignores a close Radix requests during a `pointerdown` on the trigger (`window.event`, as DEC-213 (a) does for scroll), and the content's `onPointerDownOutside` cancels the dismissal when the press is on the trigger. Escape, blur, a press anywhere else, the pointer leaving and another reason opening still close it. A status hint opened by a tap now closes with a tap elsewhere, not with a second tap on the pill.
  - **(b)** A press that opens a closed reason sends the same `tooltip.open` event on `document` first, so any other open tooltip closes as it does when Radix opens one on hover or focus. This covers `TapTooltip` and plain `Tooltip` both.
  - **(c)** `TapTooltip` takes an optional `side`, passed to `TooltipContent` (Radix's default, top, when unset). The `PanelError` and `StaleLine` Retry pass `bottom` at every width. Radix still flips it above when there's no room below. Every other call site keeps top.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Keep a toggle for status hints only: two press behaviours in one component, and Enter on a focused hint would still close what its focus opened. A ref flag set in our `onPointerDown`: the touch path dismisses on the document `click`, after our click handler, so the flag would need its own reset timing.
  - (b) A module-level store or a React context holding the open id: more code, and a press-open still wouldn't close a plain Radix `Tooltip`. The event is the smallest that works, but its name is internal to `@radix-ui/react-tooltip` (1.4.1). `tap-tooltip.spec.ts` counts open reasons, so a rename fails CI instead of passing silently.
  - (c) `bottom` only on a phone: a media-query hook for one hint, and it would get the run drawer wrong, which is narrow at every viewport, so its stale line wraps the Error ID above Retry there too. The cost of one rule: on a wide stale line the reason now sits over the right end of the coverage chart's first row while it's open, where above it sat over the empty panel header. Choosing the side from whether the Error ID wrapped is left to the backlog. Radix collision handling alone: it flips only at the viewport edge, never around page content.
- **Why:** measured in chromium, before → after (`tap-tooltip.spec.ts`; the numbers from a throwaway probe run on both versions). Enter or Space on a focused locked control with its reason open: reason kept on 0/5 → 5/5 controls (Refresh, a switch, Run now, Choose CSV…, Retry). A click after the hover: 0/5 → 5/5, and the trigger never passes through `closed` (before, each click closed it, so a second click reopened it). A second tap on an open reason: closed → open. Enter or Space on a focused control while another reason was open on hover: 2 open reasons → 1. A second reason opened by a click, hover or tap was already 1 open and stays 1. The Retry reason against the Error ID line: it overlapped on the panel error at 1440 (25 × 11 px) and on both surfaces at 375 (stale line 89 × 16 px, panel error 54 × 11 px); now it is below Retry and overlaps neither, at either width. Each guard in (a) has its own failing check: without the `pointerdown` guard or without `onPointerDownOutside`, the recorded states read `closed, instant-open` for each press.

### DEC-233 · Dialog enter at `ease.out` from 0.97, exit to 0.98; Button press stays at `fast`; e2e reads a held dialog animation
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-183)** · imp-183 (Claude lead merge checks 19:45Z and 20:15Z), imp-182 (imp-177 report leftover); keeps DEC-229 (exit at `fast` with `ease.in`) and imp-165's rule that a dialog keeps its content through the exit; vault Design/Motion System (pattern 9, Micro-interactions); web and tests only, no API, schema, dependency, CI or copy change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Dialog enter and exit scales.** `DialogContent` enters with `data-[state=open]:ease-out` (the `--ease-out` token) and `zoom-in-97`, and exits with `zoom-out-98`. tw-animate-css 1.4.0 takes both numbers (`--tw-enter-scale: calc(97*1%)`), so no arbitrary value is needed. The exit keeps `fast` and `ease-in` (DEC-229), and the scrim (fade only, `fast`) is unchanged. No caller passes its own duration, easing or zoom.
  - **(b) Button press stays at 120 ms (`fast`); the note changes, not the code.** Micro-interactions said the 0.98 press takes 80 ms. The Button has one transition for colour, background, border and scale at `fast` with `ease.standard`. An 80 ms press would need a fifth duration token for one property of one component, or a per-property duration list, and the release back to 1 would run at 80 ms too. The token table has no 80 ms step, and the Button has pressed at 120 ms since Phase 0. The vault note now says `fast` (120 ms), and e2e pins the Button's `transition-duration` at `0.12s`.
  - **(c) How e2e reads a dialog's animation.** A spec that needs the closing (or entering) state injects `animation-play-state: paused !important` for the dialog, so the animation holds at its first frame, waits for `data-state="closed"` with a 2 s bounded assertion that names what it waited for, reads the animation, then removes the hold. That applies to `press-and-exit.spec.ts` and the two exit screenshot specs. The frame samplers that must see real frames (`dialog-exit.spec.ts`, `drawer-import.spec.ts`) keep their per-frame samples and also sample the close itself from a MutationObserver on `data-state`.
- **Alternatives:** an 80 ms press token (one more token, a split transition, and a faster release); a MutationObserver that reads the computed style in the close's microtask (also race-free, but it needs its own timeout and message inside the page, and the screenshot specs need a hold anyway); a bounded frame poll (it fails faster but still misses the close under a stall); a longer test timeout (hides the race).
- **Why:** the old poll found nothing to read when a long task fell between the close and the next frame. The 120 ms exit finished, and the dialog unmounted before the poll looked. With a forced 300 ms long task right after the close, 3 of 10 runs hung until the test timeout. With the hold, the same stall passed 10 of 10 (2.3 s each), and a dialog that never closes fails in about 2.3 s with "the dialog reports closed after Cancel … Expected "closed", Received "open"". The enter went from `ease` to `cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)` and its first keyframe from `scale3d(0.96…)` to `scale3d(0.97…)`. The exit's last keyframe went from `scale3d(0.96…)` to `scale3d(0.98…)`, still 0.12 s with `cubic-bezier(0.7, 0, 0.84, 0)`. All of these are asserted on the computed keyframes in `press-and-exit.spec.ts`.

### DEC-235 · Offline reasons name their action; an open reason opens where it covers nothing
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-154)** · imp-154 (imp-134 report leftover 2), imp-186, imp-187 (imp-173 report leftovers 1 and 2); DEC-206, DEC-232 (c); vault Features/Data Health; web only, no API, schema, dependency, CI or motion change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Wording.** One helper, `offlineHint(action)` in `offline-banner.tsx`, gives "You're offline. Reconnect to <action>." The action is the control's visible verb in lower case, plus the source when the control acts on one source. Per call site:
    - Refresh: "refresh".
    - The `PanelError` and `StaleLine` Retry: "retry". The line beside it already names what failed.
    - A source switch: "enable <source>" or "disable <source>", whichever the press would do. The switch's label shows the current state, so "change" would hide the direction.
    - Run now: "run <source> now", its accessible name. It is still the last of `runBlocker`'s reasons.
    - Choose CSV… / Choose another…: "choose a CSV for <source>" / "choose another CSV for <source>".
    - Import N rows: "import N rows into <source>", the button's own words from one `rowCount`.
    
    `OFFLINE_HINT` is gone.
  - **(b) Stale line side.** `StaleLine` picks the side from its own layout, read by a `ResizeObserver` on the line (not a viewport query, since the run drawer's line wraps at every width):
    - **Above** while Retry starts the line's first row, so nothing of the line is above it.
    - **On the left of Retry** once the actions wrap onto a row of their own. That row is empty left of the actions, the Error ID or the message sits above them, and there isn't enough room below before the content under the line.
    - **Below** otherwise.
    
    A reason beside its trigger (left or right) wraps at `max-w-32` (128 px), so "You're offline. / Reconnect to retry." fits in the space left of Retry at 375. `PanelError`'s Retry stays below (DEC-232 c). Every `TapTooltip` reason keeps `collisionPadding={16}`, the page gutter. It was 0, so a reason sat flush with the viewport edge: the phone switch's at x = 0, and Choose another…'s at 1440's right edge.
  - **(c) Phone cards.** On a phone source card, the switch's and Run now's reasons open below the controls row. The cards and their skeleton go from `py-3` to `py-4`. That gives the 32 px a reason below needs before the next card's name: the 6 px offset, which clears the 4 px focus ring, plus the 26 px reason. The table keeps top.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) The old generic "Reconnect to use this." "change <source>" for the switch, the brief's example. Naming the file on Import: file names can be long.
  - (b) The brief's two-way rule (above when nothing wrapped, else below). At 375, below still covers the top 5 px of the coverage chart's first row: the room under a wrapped line is 27 px. A smaller `sideOffset` would sit on Retry's focus ring. More padding under every stale line would make a taller strip on every phone, for a reason that's open for a few seconds.
  - (c) Below without the padding: the reason's last 2 px sit on the next card's name glyphs, 6 px into its line box. Left of Run now is Check CSV, and a smaller offset would sit on the focus ring.
- **Why:** measured in chromium with `tap-tooltip.spec.ts`'s new check, which lists the text, icons, switches and chart cells under the open reason. The before column comes from the same tests run on `origin/main`'s `atoms.tsx` and `sources-table.tsx`.
  - Offline reasons that name their action: 0 of 7 call sites → 7 of 7.
  - Coverage stale-line Retry: at 1440 it covered 21 chart cells (the first row's newest), now 0, above, over the empty panel header. At 375 it covered the "Shopify stores" label and 24 cells, now 0, on the left.
  - The run drawer's stale-line Retry: no text covered before or after, at 1440 and 375. Before, it sat 8 px over the first run's row edge; now it sits on the left.
  - The Error ID: never covered, before or after.
  - Phone card Run now at 375: it covered 2 lines (Last run, Freshness), now 0, below. The table's Run now at 1440 still opens above.
- **Ceiling:** the page-level stale line at 375 without an Error ID (API unreachable while offline) has Retry beside a five-line message, so no side is free. Its reason stays below, over part of the message (91 × 15 px). A backlog row is proposed in `docs/reports/imp-154.md`.

### DEC-236 · Run drawer recovery: an older runs page fails at the list's foot; a targets Retry focuses the restored targets
**2026-10-24 · proposed (imp-136)** · aud-014 A14-05 (imp-136), the imp-168 report leftover (imp-179); applies DEC-166 to the runs list; narrows DEC-225 (a); DEC-217 (d) unchanged; (d) extends DEC-220 (b) to the `LoadMore` footers; DEC-235 (a)'s copy; vault Features/Data Health; web only, no API, schema or dependency change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Which failure shows where.** The runs list ends in the DEC-166 `LoadMore` footer whenever its first page has a `next_cursor`. An older page that fails is the footer's: every loaded run stays, the cause, error ID and Copy error ID sit at the list's foot, and the button reads Retry and asks for that page only (`fetchNextPage`; the newest page isn't refetched). Focus stays on the button through the failure and the retry. Once no page is left the button unmounts and focus moves to the note ("Showing all 45 runs"). The boxed stale line above the list is for a failed refresh of the loaded pages only (`isRefetchError`: "Couldn't refresh runs", "Showing data as of"). One failure is shown once. The note reads "Showing 20 runs · older runs not loaded yet". The runs API has no window or total, so "all" is said only when the chain ends.
  - **(b) Focus target, with a fallback.** Each expanded run is its own retry scope. A targets Retry that restores ("Couldn't load targets" or "Couldn't refresh targets") focuses the run's targets region (`tabIndex=0`, `rounded-sm` ring). A run with no targets has no region, so the drawer title takes focus. `useRetryScope` takes its restored targets in order, and the first one on the page wins. The drawer scope, and so the runs Retry, still restores to the title (DEC-225 a).
  - **(c) Keep the focused footer button in view.** When a `LoadMore` error line appears above its focused button, the button scrolls into its scroll box's view (`block: "nearest"`, instant). `scroll-mb-4` leaves room below for the ring. This covers every `LoadMore` (runs, targets, open gaps). (d) widens the margin.
  - **(d) Offline, a footer's Retry is locked; its reason opens below, with room** (added when rebasing on `3ed58ed`). (a) moved an older page's failure from the stale line, whose Retry DEC-220 (b) locks offline, to the footer, whose Retry had no lock: offline, a press started a paused fetch and nothing showed. While `LoadMore`'s button reads Retry and the browser is offline, it is locked like the stale line's: `aria-disabled`, dimmed with a token, with "You're offline. Reconnect to retry." in its `TapTooltip`. A press only shows the reason and asks for nothing. "Load older …" offline is unchanged: it pauses until reconnect, as on main. This covers all three footers. The reason opens **below**, because above sit the error line and the note, Copy error ID is on the left, and at 1440 the button ends its row. For room below:
    - the button's scroll margin goes from `scroll-mb-4` to `scroll-mb-12`: the 6 px offset, the 26 px reason and the 16 px gutter, so Radix doesn't flip it above;
    - the drawer's scroll box ends in `pb-12`, not 16 px, so the runs footer, which ends the box, can scroll that high;
    - the targets footer gets `pb-2`, so the reason clears the next run's row.
    
    The open-gaps footer can sit at the page's end with less room, and Radix then opens the reason above. At 1440 that covers nothing (asserted). The imp-186 test in `tap-tooltip.spec.ts` and imp-154's `drawer-stale` screenshots now drive the drawer's remaining stale line, a failed refresh of the loaded runs.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Keep the stale line for an older page too and only relabel the foot button. The failure would then be shown at the top, 1,262 px above the button at 1440, with two controls asking for one page. A footer for the runs that doesn't use `LoadMore` would be a third footer pattern.
  - (b) A `restored` getter ref that picks the region or the title: the same effect without a signature change, but less obvious to read. Focusing the first restored target row: rows aren't focusable, and the region is already the table's keyboard stop.
  - (c) Moving the error line below the button: that changes DEC-166's layout on three surfaces. Scroll anchoring: Chrome anchors to a node above the insertion, so the button still moves down.
  - (d) Top, Radix's default: it covers the error line or the note in 5 of 6 footer and width cases. Left: Copy error ID. A side chosen from the layout, as `StaleLine` does: more code, and at 1440 no side is free. No lock: the press stays silent offline.
- **Limits:** a repeat failure of the same footer page isn't announced again, because `LoadMore`'s alert stays mounted while it retries (DEC-166; left to the backlog). The runs footer counts loaded runs only.
- **Why:** measured in chromium at 1440 and 375 (`load-more.spec.ts`, `retry-focus.spec.ts`; the baselines come from a throwaway probe on `4d7a9ee`). An older page answering 503 with 20 runs loaded: 20 of 20 kept, as before. The failure and its Retry move from the top of the list (23 Shift+Tabs from the focused foot button) to the foot button itself (0 Tabs). The foot button reads Retry instead of "Load older runs". Error lines: 1, never 2. The newest page is requested once. Tabs from focus to the restored targets after a targets Retry: 3 → 0, and a run with no targets lands on the title. The focused Retry is fully in view after a failure (`toBeInViewport({ ratio: 1 })`); before (c) it was pushed partly below the drawer's edge. (d), at 1440 and 375 (`tap-tooltip.spec.ts`, plus a throwaway probe): offline footer Retry locked, 0 of 6 → 6 of 6 (before (d), and with the lock removed, the tests fail at `aria-disabled`); requests sent by an offline press, 0 (asserted); text under the open reason with the lock but before the room (Radix flipped it above), runs and targets at 1440 "The API is restarting. Try again.", "Error ID" and the ID, and at 375 the note on runs, targets and gaps; after, none in all 6. Without the room, 5 of the 6 tests fail; without the targets `pb-2`, both targets tests fail.

### DEC-237 · Phase 2 schema (migration 0005): one 512-d OpenCLIP space, per-listing match decisions, Phase 2 settings columns
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-01)** · Phase 2 task M-1; DEC-028, DEC-029, DEC-030, DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-042, DEC-043; R-15, R-16; AGENTS.md §3 and §7; vault Phases/Phase 2 - Core Product, Data/Product Matching, Data/Data Model
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Embedding model.** M-2 uses OpenCLIP `ViT-B-32` with the `laion2b_s34b_b79k` weights for both image and title embeddings, so `images.embedding` and `listings.title_embedding` are both `vector(512)` and live in one space. Both are nullable and carry an HNSW index with `vector_cosine_ops`. Another checkpoint means a new migration and a re-embed; there is no per-row model column.
  - **(b) `pgvector` 0.4.2** joins `pulso-core` for the SQLAlchemy type, reflection and the `cosine_distance` comparator (free, MIT; it brings `numpy`). It ships no `py.typed`, so mypy ignores its imports like boto3. No torch or open_clip yet.
  - **(c) `match_candidates`** holds one row per listing pair, lower id first (`listing_a_id < listing_b_id`, unique pair). Components `image_cos`, `title_cos`, `phash_sim` are `numeric(5,4)` and nullable (a missing input has weight 0, DEC-040); `price_sane` is nullable (not checkable). Plus `score`, `scorer_version`, `status` (`pending` · `accepted` · `rejected` · `merged` · `split`, default `pending`), `created_at`, `decided_at`. Re-scoring updates the row; history lives in `match_decisions`. The queue index is a plain `(status, score)` btree: Postgres scans it backward for `ORDER BY score DESC` (the DEC-158 pattern), so no DESC expression index.
  - **(d) `match_decisions`** is one row per affected listing: `listing_id` NOT NULL, `from_product_id`, `to_product_id`, `candidate_id` nullable, `actor` (`algo`, `owner` or a user id), `action` (`link` · `unlink` · `merge` · `split` · `accept` · `reject`), `confidence` nullable (an auto-link's score, kept because append-only rows can't be backfilled), `reason`, `decided_at`. A merge of a product with n listings writes n rows. membership(listing, t) is then the `to_product_id` of the latest row with `decided_at < t`, served by the `(listing_id, decided_at)` index (R-15). A generic trigger function `reject_append_only_mutation()` rejects UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE with `<table> is append-only`; `scores` and `forecasts` can reuse it.
  - **(e) `category_mappings`** has primary key `(source_id, source_category)`, `category_id` NOT NULL (no row means unmapped) and `mapping_source` (`rule` · `ai` · `manual`). No taxonomy seed here (M-6).
  - **(f) `product_notes`** (uuid id, `author_id` FK users, body, created_at, updated_at; index `(workspace_id, product_id)`) and **`product_tags`** (primary key `(workspace_id, product_id, tag)`, tag up to 80 chars) use `WorkspaceOwned`: `workspace_id` NOT NULL and indexed.
  - **(g) `product_daily_features`** has primary key `(product_id, feature_date, family, feature_version)`, whose prefix serves `(product_id, feature_date)` lookups, plus `velocity_7d` and `growth_7d` (double, nullable), `coverage` (`numeric(5,4)`, 0–1), `has_gap` and `computed_at`. Schema only; the computation and the leakage test come later.
  - **(h) Settings.** Fee assumptions and target margin already exist. Added: `duty_default_bps` nullable (NULL shows "duty not set", never 0; only `>= 0`, since US duty can pass 100%), `returns_bps` default 0 (0–10000), and `customs_fee_minor` BIGINT default 0 (`>= 0`), in the row's existing `currency`, like `payment_fee_fixed_minor` and `default_cpa_minor`.
  - **(i) Downgrade** refuses when it would discard owner input or history (match decisions, notes, tags, manual mappings, non-default Phase 2 settings), like 0002. Embeddings, candidates, rule/AI mappings and features are recomputable and are dropped. Downgrade is for worktree databases only.
- **Alternatives:** a `listing_ids bigint[]` per decision (can't serve the `(listing_id, decided_at)` replay index); a separate text model (MiniLM, 384-d) for titles (a second model and space; revisit if Phase 2 title precision is weak); a DESC expression index (same plan, and Alembic compares expression indexes poorly); an `id` plus a separate `(product_id, feature_date)` index on features (a redundant index); a second currency column for the customs fee (the settings row already has one).
- **Why:** the HNSW plans are `Index Scan using ix_images_embedding` and `Index Scan using ix_listings_title_embedding` for `ORDER BY <=> … LIMIT 20` (`test_embedding_knn_uses_hnsw`). Updating, deleting or truncating `match_decisions` raises (`test_match_decisions_are_append_only`). Upgrade → downgrade −1 → upgrade passes on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`, and the model metadata matches the migrated schema (`compare_metadata == []`).
- **Not in this migration:** the per-workspace margin view (the Phase 2 note lists it with the migration; DEC-041 puts it in the landed-cost task) and the taxonomy seed (M-6).

### DEC-238 · Product header resource: per-stat provenance (`Sourced | NoSource`), 308 for a merged id, `<Metric>` on TapTooltip
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-02)** · Phase 2 task UX-3, start of Q-1; DEC-040, DEC-232, DEC-235, DEC-237; SPEC §1, §6.4, §10; vault Features/Product Page, Features/Methodology & Confidence; no migration, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Provenance shape.** `pulso_api/schemas/provenance.py` holds the reusable shape. `Sourced` is `{value: int, source (sources.key), source_name, as_of, raw_payload_id, method, stale}`; `as_of` is the UTC `captured_at` of the newest observation the value uses, and `method` is set only on Pulso's own estimates. `NoSource` is `{value: null, reason}` (never 0, DEC-040). A stat is `Sourced | NoSource`. Detail resources wrap each stat this way, so every number in a header carries its own trace. List rows (Discover) keep the review's per-row `as_of` map instead (M22, vault Methodology & Confidence), so 10k-row payloads stay small. The contract test in (b) accepts both, since both carry `as_of`.
  - **(b) Contract test.** `test_every_number_in_a_product_resource_carries_as_of` walks every 2xx response schema under `/v1/products` in the exported OpenAPI. It fails on any object that holds an integer or number without a required `as_of`. Deleting `Sourced.as_of` fails it on `Sourced.value` and `Sourced.raw_payload_id`. The same walker flags `/v1/settings` (bare numbers) and passes `/v1/me` (none), which shows the check bites. A future page wrapper's integer cursor needs an exemption when Discover lands.
  - **(c) `GET /v1/products/{product_id}`** (operationId `get_product`) returns `ProductHeader`: `id, title, image_url, category` (the taxonomy path, null while uncategorised), `first_seen_at`, `currency` (`USD`), `store_count`, `supplier_count`, `price_min_minor`, `price_max_minor`. The stats come from each live listing's (`delisted_at IS NULL`) latest snapshot (`DISTINCT ON listing_id`). Counts are distinct stores and suppliers, traced to the newest snapshot in their set. The price bounds come from USD store listings only, each traced to the snapshot that gives the bound. A non-USD store still counts as a store; converting its price waits for an FX basis. `stale` is `now − as_of > 2 × sources.config.expected_interval` (seconds, Methodology's "> 2×"). A source without an interval is never stale. Products are global market data: the router takes `current_principal` and no workspace filter.
  - **(d) Merged and unknown ids.** A product with `merged_into_id` answers **308** with a relative `Location: {survivor}?{query}`. A relative URL resolves under the web app's `/api` rewrite too; an absolute `/v1/...` path would leave it. It is one hop per request (a chained merge redirects again), and the middleware's `no-store` keeps a browser from caching a merge that can be undone. An unknown id is the 404 problem (`not_found`); a malformed UUID is FastAPI's 422. The web shows both as not found and doesn't retry a 4xx. When fetch has followed a 308, the page `router.replace`s to the survivor's URL with the query string kept.
  - **(e) Tabs.** `?tab=` goes through `resolveTab`: `overview`, `trends`, `suppliers`, `sellers` or `profit`. An unknown or flagged-off value shows Overview, without an error and without rewriting the URL. Each tab has a flag in `TAB_FLAGS`, a constant flipped in code like nav.ts's `CURRENT_PHASE`. Radix Tabs use `activationMode="manual"`. A switch calls `history.replaceState`, which Next syncs into `useSearchParams`: no history entry (Back leaves the page, as the Product Page note specs) and no server round trip. Selecting Overview drops the param, so a clean URL means the default. Each tab body says which task (UX-4…UX-8) builds it and shows no number.
  - **(f) `<Metric>`** (`components/pulso/metric.tsx`) is a button in tabular numerals named `{label}: {value}`. It opens a provenance card on hover, focus and tap through Data Health's `TapTooltip` (DEC-232/235): a press opens or keeps it, one card at a time, and Escape closes it. `TapTooltip` gains a `contentClassName` prop. The Metric card passes `pointer-events-auto`, so a pointer can enter it and select the raw payload id. Every other hint stays click-through. The card shows source, UTC as-of and its age (from the fetch time, so it never ticks), the method of an estimate, stale in words with an icon, and the raw payload id. There is no admin raw-payload view yet, so the id is selectable text, as in the run drawer.
  - **(g) Types.** openapi-fetch's `Readable` helper drops a key typed only `null`, so a response's `NoSource` arrives typed `{reason}`. The web `Stat` type makes `value` optional on that side, and code narrows with `"reason" in stat`.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) A `meta.as_of` map per family on the header (UI States' convention): a stat couldn't name its raw payload, which UX-3's verify asks for. One flat `Stat` with every field nullable: types that can't tell "no source" from a missing field.
  - (d) Follow the merge chain on the server and redirect once: a loop and a cycle guard for a case the matcher doesn't produce yet. An absolute `Location`: it escapes the `/api` rewrite.
  - (e) `router.replace` per tab: a server round trip on a dynamic route for each switch. An env flag per tab: a `.env` change for every flip, and no env var exists to flip yet.
  - (f) A new Popover-based card: a second tooltip pattern beside TapTooltip, which the brief rules out. Keyboard users get the id read out with the card, but can't select it until a focusable card or a raw-payload link exists (backlog).
- **Why:** API tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` cover:
  - 200 with source, `as_of` and `raw_payload_id` on all four stats;
  - a superseded snapshot and a delisted listing are both ignored;
  - a CAD store counts as a store but sets no price;
  - `stale` true past 2× the interval, false without one;
  - every stat `{value: null, reason}` on an unmatched product;
  - 404 problem and 422;
  - 308 keeping `?tab=profit&supplier=99102`.

  Playwright (17 tests): every `?tab=` value deep-links; four unknown values fall back to Overview; all 4 header numbers open a card with source and as-of by keyboard and by tap; 375 px has no horizontal scroll.

### DEC-239 · Discover feed: per-number provenance on list rows, keyset sorts on the feature table, index 0007 for newest, URL-held grid/table
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-03)** · Phase 2 task UX-2; DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-041, DEC-158, DEC-238; SPEC §6.1, §10; vault Features/Discover, Features/Methodology & Confidence; migration 0007 (index only), no dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Resource.** `GET /v1/products` (operationId `list_products`) returns `DiscoverPage {items, next_cursor, as_of, degraded}`. Each item is DEC-238's `ProductHeader` plus `velocity` (`Velocity | NoSource`), `best_margin_minor` (always `NoSource`, "The landed-cost engine arrives with UX-8."), `as_of` (the oldest family feeding it) and `stale`. List rows carry the same per-number `Sourced | NoSource` as the header. This narrows DEC-238(a), which kept a per-row `as_of` map for lists: one shape, so `<Metric>` and the provenance card work unchanged, and at 48 rows a page the payload stays small. `next_cursor` is an opaque string, so the contract test covers the list with no exemption.
  - **(b) Sorts and keyset.** `momentum` (default): G7 descending, no signal last (`coalesce(growth, −∞)`), then `id`. `newest`: `first_seen_at DESC, id DESC`. `fewest_sellers`: store count ascending, then `id`. The cursor is the JSON `[key, id]` of the last row, validated per sort; a bad one is the 422 problem `invalid_cursor`. `limit` defaults to 48, max 100. Merged products never appear.
  - **(c) Momentum** reads `product_daily_features.growth_7d` (G7 = ln((V7+5)/(V7prev+5)), DEC-040), never recomputes it, so Phase 4 shares one implementation. The row is the latest `feature_date` with `velocity_7d` and `growth_7d` both set; on that day the best-graded family wins: `shp_inv` (A) > `shp_bump` (B) > `cj_stock` (unproven), then the newest `feature_version`. Its `as_of` is `min(feature_date + 1 day 00:00Z, computed_at)` (DEC-031); `raw_payload_id` is null (a feature, not an observation); `method` names the family; `gap` is `has_gap`. With no qualifying row, velocity is `NoSource` ("No demand family has a 7-day growth yet.") and the UI says "Insufficient signal".
  - **(d) Filters.** `category`: that exact `categories.id` (subtrees wait for the M-6 taxonomy). `price_min` / `price_max`: USD minor units, matched by overlap with the product's USD store band, so a product without a USD price drops out while a price filter is set. `source`: a live listing from that `sources.key` exists. `first_seen_days`: 7, 30 or 90 days before now. `has_sellers`: store count above 0.
  - **(e) Degraded and page `as_of`.** A source with a live listing on an unmerged product is degraded when it's blocked, not enabled, or stale (its last `raw_payloads.captured_at` is older than 2 × `expected_interval`). The page `as_of` is the newest last capture among those feeding sources. The banner names each one; a card fed by a blocked or disabled source gets a red dot and the banner's word ("source blocked" / "source paused") in neutral text, so a blocked source can't paint every card red.
  - **(f) `GET /v1/categories`** (operationId `list_categories`) lists categories with at least one unmerged product, by path, for the filter. It sits outside `/v1/products`, whose contract test would read the integer id as an untraced number.
  - **(g) Index.** Migration 0007 (renumbered: p2-04's source registration took 0006 first) adds `ix_products_first_seen_at_id` on `(first_seen_at, id) WHERE merged_into_id IS NULL` (lead's answer, AGENTS.md §7). It's ascending and Postgres scans it backward for the DESC sort, the DEC-158 pattern. Momentum, fewest sellers, price and has-sellers stay LATERAL aggregates over the filtered set, ordered and keyset-compared in SQL (DEC-041 volumes); BACKLOG has the revisit at about 50k products.
  - **(h) Web state.** The URL holds `view`, `sort` and the filters (prices in whole dollars); the default sort and empty filters are left out, so a clean URL means the default. Changes use `history.replaceState` (DEC-238 e). The last view is kept in `localStorage` (`pulso.discover.view`, wrapped in try/catch); the URL wins. **V** toggles the view unless the focus is in a field.
  - **(i) Views.** The grid loads the next page from an `IntersectionObserver` sentinel. The table renders only the rows in view (fixed row height from `--table-row-h`, overscan 10) with no new dependency; it pins the name column (10rem on phones) under a sticky header. Neither animates a card or row; only the view switch crossfades (opacity). On phones the filters move into a bottom sheet (`Sheet side="bottom"`), and card numbers sit 44px apart on coarse pointers, so the `hit-area` targets can't overlap.
  - **(j) Small shared changes.** `<Metric>` and its card take `missing` (default "No source yet"; "Insufficient signal" where a source exists but no series qualifies). `NavItem.built` marks Discover built ahead of `CURRENT_PHASE`; raising the phase would link Phase 2 items that don't exist yet.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) DEC-238's per-row `as_of` map on lists: a second shape and a second display path for the same numbers. An integer offset cursor: unstable under inserts, and an exemption in the contract test.
  - (c) Raw 7-day percent change: the Velocity note's display, but it ranks small bases first; G7 is the score's own growth term. Recomputing G7 in the API: a second implementation beside the feature job.
  - (g) A DESC index `(first_seen_at DESC, id DESC)`: the same plan, and Alembic compares DESC/expression indexes poorly (DEC-237). Materialized sort keys for momentum and store count: a refresh job for volumes DEC-041 says won't hurt yet.
  - (i) TanStack Virtual: a new dependency for fixed-height rows, which 25 lines cover.
- **Why:** API tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`: every sort pages every product exactly once, with no repeats; each filter narrows the feed; a merged product never appears; momentum orders by the log formula where raw percent would not, with no signal last; best margin is `NoSource` with its reason; bad cursors, sorts and `first_seen_days` give 422; categories list only those in use. `test_newest_pages_scan_the_first_seen_index_backward` EXPLAINs two newest pages over 20,000 products: a backward scan of `ix_products_first_seen_at_id` reading 49 rows, with no Sort above it. Vitest covers the URL round trip and the remembered view. Playwright (11 tests) covers both views showing the same set from one request, the URL round trip reaching the API, V and the remembered view, a 10,000-row table loading by cursor with under 80 rows in the DOM and no running animations, keyboard Tab + Enter opening a product, every state, and the phone sheet with no sideways page scroll.

### DEC-240 · Listing images: a disabled `listing_images` source on the framework, an exact-host allowlist, the raw capture as the stored copy, a numpy DCT pHash
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-04)** · Phase 2 task M-2 part a; DEC-003, DEC-028, DEC-033, DEC-037, DEC-040, DEC-073, DEC-237; SPEC §4.2, §4.4, §12; AGENTS.md §3, §4, §7; vault Data/Product Matching; one migration (a source row), one new dependency (Pillow)
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Registration.** Migration `0006` inserts source 6 `listing_images` ("Listing images", `public_endpoint`, phase 2, **disabled**). Its flag is `sources.enabled`, like every source: no settings flag, nothing in seeds, and the collector scripts' source list (a protected path) doesn't name it. Worker job `images` runs daily at 06:00 UTC on the `collect` queue (after Shopify 02:00/03:00, CJ 04:00, Trends Mon 05:00). It is registered in `PLANS` with the generic 45-minute budget. Downgrade deletes the row; any run, capture or event that references it makes the foreign keys refuse.
  - **(b) Main image.** The lowest `images.position` of a listing with `delisted_at IS NULL`, the newest row on a tie. Shopify positions start at 1, and image rows are never deleted, so a replaced image leaves its old row at the same position. Only the main image is fetched; the vault's "first 3 images" waits until part b shows it helps.
  - **(c) Exact-host allowlist.** `IMAGE_HOSTS` (comma list, default empty) names the hosts that may be fetched; only `https` URLs on port 443 qualify. Image URLs come from third-party catalogs and CSVs, so the allowlist keeps the fetcher off arbitrary hosts (including the collector host's own services). It also makes "robots and terms checked" a per-host step before any request. An unlisted image is skipped in planning and never requested.
  - **(d) Framework reuse.** The adapter plugs into `execute_run`: honest User-Agent, the live guards (`COLLECTORS_LIVE`, worktree, `sources.enabled`), `Retry-After`, 5xx retries with backoff, the 5-consecutive-429/430/403 disable with one `source_events` row, and run/target evidence for Data Health. Transport is urllib like the Shopify adapter: no proxy, `NoRedirect` (reused), `collector_request_timeout_seconds`. A block stops the run at once (framework rule), so the count grows by one per run; a host that answers robots.txt with 200 resets it each run.
  - **(e) Robots and rate.** robots.txt is its own target per host per run, before that host's images. A non-2xx answer fails it closed, and the host's images skip the run unattempted (no penalty). `Disallow` fails the image with `RobotsDisallowed`. Requests to one host are spaced `60 / IMAGE_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE` seconds (default 30, capped at 30), and `Crawl-delay` or `Request-rate` can only slow that further. Hosts are interleaved, so one host's spacing never idles another. A run plans at most `rate × budget minutes` images per host (1,350 by default). Spacing is in-process only: a worker restart can send one early request per host.
  - **(f) Validation before storage.** In `fetch`, before the framework stores the response: media type image/jpeg, png, webp or gif; at most 5 MiB (the read stops one byte past it); at most 40 MP from the header, before decoding; Pillow opened with only those four formats; a full decode. Each check has its own error class (`ImageContentType`, `ImageTooLarge`, `ImageUndecodable`), so the run's `error_code` names it, and an `image_rejected` warning carries the image id beside the job id. A rejected body is never written anywhere.
  - **(g) Stored copy.** The framework already stores each body once, gzip, named by the sha256 of the original bytes (`raw/listing_images/<UTC date>/<sha256>.bin.gz`). `images.storage_key` is that key, so the image lives in one object, traces to its raw payload, and its retention follows raw payload retention. Part b reads it back by finding the raw payload through the indexed `sha256` and calling `storage.read_capture`, which checks the hash. A future retention job must clear `storage_key` and `phash` with the object, or exempt image captures.
  - **(h) Backoff from run evidence.** No new columns. A failed attempt is a `listing_image` target that is `failed`, or not `success` with a capture linked. The image then waits 20 h × 2^(n−1), capped at 30 days (20 h lets the next daily slot retry it). A deferred or robots-skipped target has no capture and isn't `failed`, so it is retried next run without penalty. An image with a `storage_key` is never planned again.
  - **(i) Empty plan.** The framework records a target-less run as `failed`, and the slot audit answers that with a catch-up and an overdue status. When nothing is due, the plan's only target is one offline JSON capture (`pending`, `unlisted_host`, `backoff` counts), so a quiet day succeeds with no request.
  - **(j) pHash.** `pulso_ml.phash`: grayscale, 32×32 LANCZOS, a 2-D DCT-II as a numpy basis-matrix product (no scipy), the top-left 8×8 block including the DC term, bits above the median, MSB first. This is the `imagehash.phash` algorithm. It is stored as signed 64-bit two's complement; most hashes are negative, because the DC term sets the top bit. `similarity(a, b) = 1 − hamming/64` is M-3's `phash_sim`. **Pillow 13** joins `pulso-ml` and `pulso-sources` (free, MIT-CMU licence, no network calls). `pulso-ml` declares `numpy` (already locked through pgvector), and `pulso-sources` depends on `pulso-ml`.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) A settings flag such as `IMAGE_FETCH_ENABLED`: a second switch beside the source's own, which no other source has.
  - (c) A DNS check that refuses private addresses: it still resolves untrusted names and races the connect; an allowlist is shorter and stricter.
  - (g) A second `img/<sha256>.<ext>` object (the vault's first sketch): doubles storage for every image, and its retention would need a rule of its own.
  - (h) `fetch_failures` / `fetch_retry_at` columns on `images`: a schema change for state the run targets already record.
  - (i) Fetch robots.txt for every listed host even when nothing is due: a request a day per host only to make the run succeed.
  - (j) `imagehash` (pulls scipy and PyWavelets) or `scipy.fft.dct`: heavy dependencies for three lines of numpy.
- **Why:** fixture tests in `packages/sources/tests/listing_images` use generated PNG bytes, a mocked opener and a fake clock, and send no request to any host:
  - a fresh image is stored once under its sha256 key with `storage_key` and `phash` set; a re-run sends zero requests and succeeds;
  - wrong type, oversize, 42 MP, truncated and garbage bodies are rejected, logged and never stored;
  - 403, 429 and 430 each stop the run and produce exactly one disable event at the threshold;
  - `Retry-After: 120` delays the retry by 120 s;
  - per-host spacing holds at 6/min (10 s), and `Crawl-delay: 20` slows its host to 20 s while the other host interleaves;
  - `Disallow` and an unavailable robots.txt send no image request;
  - a disabled source or `COLLECTORS_LIVE=off` sends nothing;
  - the Celery path runs the `images` job end to end.

  pHash tests: identical bytes hash identically; resized 300 px JPEG q70 copies are within Hamming 6 (measured 0–2); five unrelated generated images are more than 20 apart (measured ≥ 22); hashes are signed 64-bit. Breaking the spacing, the robots spacing, the backoff, the `Disallow` check or the pixel cap each fails a test.
- **Not here:** OpenCLIP embeddings (part b), candidates (M-3), any UI, and enabling the source (an owner decision; DEC-073 does not cover it).

### DEC-242 · Block counting and the p2-05 review lows: robots.txt keeps the block count, due off-list images read partial, strict `IMAGE_HOSTS`, a slow host sits the run out, one robots reader, a named FX skip
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-06)** · Phase 2 task P2-06; cross-review XR-11 (medium) and imp-185 (XR-10, XR-12 to XR-15), `docs/reports/p2-05.md`; DEC-033, DEC-069, DEC-230, DEC-240 (d, e, i); SPEC §4.2; no migration, no dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) robots.txt keeps the block count (XR-11).** `Request.resets_blocks` (default true) is false on both adapters' robots.txt requests. A robots answer that isn't 403/429/430 leaves `consecutive_block_count` as it was; a robots answer that is a block still counts. Every other live answer still resets it. So a host that serves robots.txt but blocks every image, or a store that blocks `/meta.json`, reaches `COLLECTOR_BLOCK_THRESHOLD` and disables the source once. This replaces DEC-240 (d)'s "a host that answers robots.txt with 200 resets it each run" and answers imp-200's robots question. Per-object 403s still count, as before (imp-200's other half). A store's own JSON answering 200 (`/meta.json`) still resets the count, as DEC-069 says.
  - **(b) Due images with no listed host read partial (XR-12).** When nothing can be planned, `pending > 0` and every pending main image is unlisted (host not in `IMAGE_HOSTS`, not https on port 443, or userinfo or a backslash in the netloc), the offline plan target ends `partial` with `NoListedHost`. That means an empty or wrong allowlist, or images on a host nobody has checked yet: either way, their pHash stays missing. Nothing pending still reads `success`, and a day with images only in backoff is unchanged (imp-198). A partial run queues no catch-up.
  - **(c) `IMAGE_HOSTS` entries are bare lowercase DNS names (XR-12).** Two or more labels of `[a-z0-9-]`, the last starting with a letter. A scheme, port, userinfo, path, trailing dot, upper case, single label or IP literal fails `Settings` at start-up instead of silently matching nothing.
  - **(d) A host whose next slot is past the run's deadline sits the run out (XR-13).** Before its robots.txt or an image, a host whose next allowed request falls at or after the adapter's deadline yields no request. The target ends `partial` with no capture (no backoff penalty), one `listing_images_host_skipped` log line names the host, and the other hosts carry on. Per-host spacing and limits are unchanged; the skip only stops one host's Crawl-delay from ending the run for the others. The fetch-time deadline check stays as the backstop.
  - **(e) One robots reader for both adapters (XR-14).** `shopify_stores.read_robots` parses robots.txt and returns the spacing: the largest finite, non-negative `Crawl-delay` anywhere in the file (any group, fractions included; urllib keeps only whole numbers in the agent's own group), or the agent's `Request-rate` seconds per request if that is larger. `Request-rate` with 0 requests raises `RobotsZeroRate` (a `RobotsDisallowed`), so that host's or store's target ends partial and nothing more is requested from it this run. It can only slow the crawl.
  - **(f) Userinfo and backslash URLs have no host (XR-15).** `https_host` returns None when the netloc holds `@` or `\`, because urllib connects to the whole netloc. Such an image counts as unlisted.
  - **(g) The FX next-day skip is logged (XR-10).** `upsert` logs `fx_next_day_rows_skipped count=… dates=… raw_payload_id=…` whenever DEC-230 skips rows dated after the capture's UTC date. No run-drawer label.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Per-host block counts from run evidence (the review's option 2): a second block rule beside the framework's, and more code. The field covers both adapters in a few lines.
  - (b) Flag only when no listed host matches any image, stored ones included: needs a second query, and a due image nobody can fetch is worth a partial either way.
  - (c) Lowercase or strip entries silently: hides a mistyped entry. Strict validation fails loudly at start-up, like the other collector settings.
  - (d) Pick the next target by its soonest allowed time (the review's optional part): a scheduler for a case the skip already fixes.
  - (g) A run-drawer label: API and web changes for a rare, expected skip. The log line is the named evidence.
- **Why:** fixture tests (mocked openers, fake clocks, SQLite; no request to any host):
  - with robots.txt answering 200 and every image answering 403, 429 or 430, `listing_images` disables itself at the threshold with exactly one `source_events` row, and the next run sends nothing. For `shopify_stores`, robots.txt 200 and `/meta.json` 429 give the same result over 5 runs. A robots.txt answering a block still counts (the existing tests).
  - An allowlist that matches none of the due images reads `partial`/`NoListedHost` with no request, and nothing due reads `success`. Nine malformed `IMAGE_HOSTS` forms fail settings.
  - With a 3000 s Crawl-delay on host A (budget 2700 s), every host B image is fetched and A's image is partial with no capture. A second run in the same process skips A's robots.txt without stopping B.
  - `Crawl-delay: 10.5` spaces the host at least 10.5 s apart, and `Request-rate: 0/1` refuses as `RobotsZeroRate`. The reader keeps the largest finite value and ignores inf, nan, negative and unparseable ones.
  - Userinfo and backslash URLs have no host.
  - The FX skip is logged for captures at 00:00Z, 23:59:59Z and an aware UTC−5 time, keyed by the UTC date.
- **Not here:** enabling `listing_images` (owner decision); whether per-object 403s should count toward the block rule (imp-200's first half); how a quiet day with images in backoff reads (imp-198).

### DEC-244 · Restore drill: the newest collector dump restores into a throwaway scratch Postgres with every count matching its manifest, and upgrades 0004 → 0007 there in under 3 s
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-08)** · Phase 2 support task P2-08; Lead Playbook §6 step 3; DEC-015, DEC-100, DEC-104, DEC-129; DEC-237 (0005), DEC-240 (0006), DEC-239 (0007); vault Platform/Backups & Restore; `docs/reports/p2-08.md`; no code, migration or dependency
- **Decision:** the drill DEC-015 asks for is recorded here, and it passed. It is the drill Lead Playbook §6 step 3 requires before the collector database, which now holds collected rows, migrates past 0004. It does not replace the open DEC-072 review of 0007 or any other collector gate.
  - **Dump:** `pulso-20261024T205007554184Z.dump` (119,145 bytes), `captured_at` 2026-10-24T20:50:07.554184Z, server 18.6, the newest in `OneDrive\Pulso Backups\db`. It was read only: copied with its manifest to a temp folder outside OneDrive and the worktree. The sha256 of the copy, and of the copy inside the container, equals the manifest's `c6652c87…c47c804`.
  - **Environment:** a throwaway `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` container, `pulso-p2-08-restore`, on `127.0.0.1:55488` with `--tmpfs /var/lib/postgresql` and trust auth. The scratch database was `pulso_restore_check_p2_08`.
