No compose command was run, and the drill didn't use `infra/scripts/restore.py`, which copies into the collector Postgres (a collector-host operation). The collector stack (55432/56379/59000/59001), the dev stack (5433) and `C:\dev\pulso` were not touched.
  - **Restore:** `createdb -T template0`, then `pg_restore --no-owner --exit-on-error` exited 0 in 0.15 s. The dump has no ACL entries, so `--no-privileges` wasn't needed.
  - **Counts:** counted with `infra/scripts/backup.py`'s own `METADATA` SQL (every public base table) and compared with `Manifest.row_counts`. All 27 tables match, and Alembic reads `["0004"]` as in the manifest. The non-zero tables: `fx_rates` 4,627, `host_heartbeats` 461, `currencies` 8, `sources` 5, `raw_payloads` 2, and `alembic_version`, `collector_runs`, `collector_run_targets` and `source_events` 1 each. The other 18 are 0.
  - **Upgrade rehearsal:** `uv run alembic -c alembic.ini upgrade head` on the scratch database only, with `DATABASE_URL` and placeholder S3 key names set for that one process (no `.env` in the worktree, none written). It exited 0 in 2.95 s wall time, including uv and Python start-up; `alembic current` alone takes 1.79 s, so the migrations took about 1.2 s. The head is `0007`.
  - **Counts after:** the 27 existing tables keep their counts, except `sources` 5 → 6: migration 0006's own insert, id 6 `listing_images`, `enabled = false`; ids 1–5 are still there. The six new tables from 0005 (`category_mappings`, `match_candidates`, `match_decisions`, `product_daily_features`, `product_notes`, `product_tags`) are empty. Both `match_decisions` append-only triggers and `ix_products_first_seen_at_id` exist.
  - **Clean-up:** `docker rm -f pulso-p2-08-restore`, after which `docker ps -a` lists no such container; the temp dump copy was deleted. The backups folder's listing and every file's sha256 are identical before and after.
- **What it doesn't prove:**
  - **Raw objects:** the dump is database only (`raw_object_coverage: none`). The 2 `raw_payloads` rows point at RustFS objects that were neither restored nor hash-checked, and no payload was re-parsed. That part of the vault's R-11 criterion stays open; this entry is its "missing raw coverage explicitly recorded".
  - **Scale and locks:** about 5,100 rows say nothing about migration time on a large database (p2-05 measured 0005 at 2.4 s on 300,000 listings and images). No writer was connected; the host's `migrate` and `update` stop consumers and dump first (DEC-104).
  - **Ownership:** the restore ran as `postgres` with `--no-owner`, not as the collector's `pulso` role. Both are superusers, so the upgrade's privileges match.
  - **Tooling and recovery:** `restore.py`, OneDrive sync, R2 and a recovery onto a fresh machine (the RTO guess) were not exercised. Only the newest dump was restored.
- **Alternatives:** `infra/scripts/restore.py --latest` on the collector host. It's the documented drill, but it is a collector-host operation (Lead Playbook §6) and drops its scratch database before an upgrade could run. A drill helper under `infra/scripts/drills/`: `backup.py`'s `METADATA`, `parse_metadata` and `Manifest` already did the counting and comparing, so a one-off command was enough.
- **Why:** the restore and both comparisons are exact (no tolerance). The upgrade is the same Alembic command the collector's `migrate` service runs, on the same image and server version (18.6) as the collector.

### DEC-245 · Product Trends tab: `GET /v1/products/{id}/series` with points only on collected UTC days and coverage gap days; ECharts 6.1.0, loaded only with the tab
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-09)** · Phase 2 task P2-09 (UX-5); vault `Features/Product Page - Trends`; DEC-007, DEC-024, DEC-026, DEC-028, DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-239; SPEC §6.4; no migration; one dependency (`echarts` 6.1.0)
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) A point only on a day the source collected.** A series has a point for UTC day *d* only when `source_daily_coverage` reads `success` or `partial` for one of its sources on *d*. Listing snapshots are change-only, so the day's value carries each listing's newest snapshot captured before *d* + 1 00:00Z (DEC-031). A listing counts until its `delisted_at`. `store_count` is the number of distinct stores with a live listing. `price` is the lowest `price_min_minor` and the highest `price_max_minor` (or `price_min_minor`) over USD rows, sent as `value` and `high`. Each point's `as_of` is the newest capture it uses. Nothing is interpolated or filled.
  - **(b) Gap days come from coverage.** `gaps` lists the days where the series' sources recorded `gap` or `failed` and the series has no point. A `running`, `queued`, `filled` or `unknown` day, or a day with no coverage row, gets neither a point nor a band: the line just breaks. The tab draws gap days as a hatched band from `--muted-foreground` across [*d* 00:00Z, *d* + 1 00:00Z), with a legend entry ("Collection gap · N days, no data"). It never uses colour alone.
  - **(c) Velocity comes from `product_daily_features` only.** `velocity_js` reads family `shp_inv` (A, inventory drops) and `velocity_bumps` reads family `shp_bump` (B, `updated_at` bumps, a lower bound). Each day uses the newest `feature_version`, with value `round(velocity_7d)` and a point `as_of` of min(*d* + 1 00:00Z, `computed_at`), as in DEC-239. With no rows, the series is NoSource ("No velocity computed from … yet"), and the tab says "Insufficient signal" when both families are missing. The pane is "Order velocity". B is dashed and named a lower bound.
  - **(d) Search is NoSource until Phase 3.** `search_trends` is keyword-level, and nothing links a product to a keyword until `product_keywords` (Phase 3). The reason says so. Matching on product titles would be a guessed link.
  - **(e) Ads are always NoSource in Phase 2.** The reason is "No ad source yet. Ad counts arrive in Phase 8 or by CSV import." The tab shows a "No source yet" row, never a zero line.
  - **(f) A merged id answers 308 to `../{survivor}/series` with the query kept**, a relative Location as in `GET /v1/products/{id}`, so it works behind `/api`. An unknown id is 404, and an unknown `metrics` entry is 422 `invalid_metrics`.
  - **(g) One request, a client-side window.** The tab fetches `range=all` once. The range control (7D/30D/90D/All; default 90D) only moves the window over that response, so a switch is one click and never refetches. All starts at the real first date: the earliest point, or the first-seen day. The range and the panes that are on live in the URL (`range`, `overlay`); defaults are left out. Toggling a pane is one click. The API still takes `range` for other callers.
  - **(h) The gap schema is `SeriesGap`.** Calling it `Gap` collides with Data Health's `Gap`, so FastAPI renames both and breaks the generated TS types the Data Health views use.
  - **(i) ECharts 6.1.0, modular, lazy.** The chart uses `echarts/core` with only `LineChart`, grid, tooltip, axis pointer, mark area and the canvas renderer. It loads through `next/dynamic` (`ssr: false`) when the Trends tab renders, as one 523 KB (176 KB gzip) chunk that sits in the route's loadable manifest, not its initial chunks; full `echarts.min.js` is 1.12 MB (368 KB gzip). One theme module (`src/lib/charts/theme.ts`) reads the colour and font tokens at render time and again on a theme change. The panes share one canvas with linked x axes, one crosshair and one tooltip, and each tooltip row gives its source and as of. Every pane header shows its source and as of. The chart animates on its first draw only, and not at all under reduced motion. The keyboard steps through the days (←/→, Home/End, Esc), and an `aria-live` line reads each day.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Forward-fill or interpolate across missing days: invents values (DEC-028, AGENTS.md §3).
  - (b) Infer gaps from missing points: a quiet day and a failed day look alike. Coverage records which is which.
  - (g) Fetch per range: a network wait on every click for a few hundred points.
  - (i) A chart instance per pane joined with `echarts.connect`: more instances and more code for the same synced crosshair. Recharts: DEC-026 chose ECharts.
- **Why:** API tests against Postgres fixtures (`apps/api/tests/test_product_series.py`):
  - points only on `success`/`partial` days;
  - gaps present and absent, with no point on an unobserved day;
  - a capture at *d* + 1 00:00Z belongs to *d* + 1;
  - the newest feature version;
  - every NoSource reason;
  - ranges, metrics, the 308, workspace isolation and the 401.
  Mutating the cutoff to `<=`, or reading `gap` as observed, fails a test. Vitest covers the window, alignment, gap-band and URL-state helpers. Playwright under the E2E auth bypass with route fixtures covers:
  - hatched bands on gap days (canvas pixels) and none on a day with no record;
  - one click per range and per pane, with no refetch;
  - source and as of per pane and per keyboard day;
  - no source yet;
  - the loading, error, no history, short history and stale states;
  - 375 px with no horizontal scroll.
- **Not here:** the forecast fan (Phase 6), an ads series (Phase 8 or CSV), event annotations, search via `product_keywords` (Phase 3), a "view as table" and CSV export, and zoom.

### DEC-246 · Landed cost: one pure function, half-even once per line, `complete` + `missing[]`, exact freight, FX on the rate's own date
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-10)** · Phase 2 task UX-8 part a and its R-16 rows; DEC-030, DEC-036, DEC-040, DEC-088, DEC-237 (h); SPEC §6.4 Profit, §12; vault Features/Product Page - Profit, Features/Product Page - Suppliers; no migration, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Rounding.** Every money line is computed exactly and rounded half-even once, to USD cents. Goods are `q × C` in the supplier's minor units, converted once by `convert_minor(..., rounding=ROUND_HALF_EVEN, inverse=True)` (DEC-088), never `q ×` a rounded unit cost. Basis-point lines (duty, fees, returns) use `round(Fraction(minor × bps, 10000))`: exact integer rationals, and Python's `round` on a `Fraction` is half-even. Duty is on the rounded goods line (customs value is goods only, per the Profit note). `landed_cost_minor` and `margin_minor` are sums of the rounded lines, so a breakdown always adds up. `margin_bps` is `margin / price`, half-even. Currency exponents come from `pulso_core.money` (JPY 0, USD 2, KWD 3). No floats anywhere.
  - **(b) Formula** (`pulso_core.landed_cost.landed_cost`, pure, frozen dataclasses). Revenue is the retail price per order (there is no shipping-charged setting yet). Landed cost = goods + freight + duty + customs fee per order. DDP zeroes duty and the customs fee (the Profit note's acceptance). Payment fee = bps × price + fixed, Shopify fee = bps × price, returns = bps × price (goods and freight aren't recovered). Margin = price − landed − fees − returns: contribution before ads, which is the break-even CPA. CPA, ROAS and target CPA come with the Profit tab (part b).
  - **(c) `complete` + `missing[]`.** Codes: `price_missing`, `supplier_cost_missing`, `freight_missing`, `freight_variant_mismatch`, `duty_not_set`, `fx_missing`, in that order, each at most once. An unresolved input leaves its line null and withholds every total that needs it. Landed cost still shows without a price, but `margin_minor` and `margin_bps` are null unless the row is complete, so an incomplete row has no ranking value. `best()` ranks complete rows only. Owner estimates (request overrides) count as complete, are listed in `estimates`, and carry source `owner_estimate`. Settings values carry source `settings`.
  - **(d) Loader** (`pulso_core.landed_cost_loader.calculate`). Unit cost is the latest variant snapshot `price_minor` when a variant is chosen, else the latest listing snapshot `price_min_minor`. Freight is the latest US quote per origin and method for exactly this listing and quantity. A null-variant quote covers only a listing with at most one live variant. With no method given, the cheapest quote in USD wins, and a quote without FX sorts last. If quotes exist at this quantity but none fits the variant, the code is `freight_variant_mismatch`; otherwise it is `freight_missing`. Never `q × F(1)`. FX is the USD-base rate dated within the 4 days up to the observation's UTC date, newest date first; on a tie, the daily blend beats a manual CSV rate. The rate's own date counts, not its `captured_at`: the daily job usually stores day d's rate on d + 1 (DEC-088 backfills through yesterday). D4's capture cutoff stays for features.
  - **(e) Duty resolution:** request override, then `settings.duty_default_bps` (the US destination default), then not set. Product and category duty rules have no table yet, so they wait for the duty-rules task (a migration).
  - **(f) Supplier and price.** Without `supplier_listing_id`, the endpoint costs every live supplier listing of the product and returns the best complete margin. If none is complete, it returns the one with the fewest missing inputs, then the lowest id. The reference price is the lower median of live USD store prices, one listing's price with its trace. It applies at quantity 1 only; a q-unit order needs a price override.
  - **(g) `POST /v1/profit/calc`** (`calc_profit`). Settings come from the principal's workspace. Bounds at the boundary, all strict types, unknown fields rejected:
    - fee and returns bps 0..10000;
    - duty bps 0..100000 (DEC-237 (h): US duty can pass 100%; the Profit slider goes to 150%);
    - quantity 1..10000;
    - money 0..2^63−1, and a price of at least 1;
    - ids of at least 1.

    An unknown or merged product, or a listing or variant not on it, is 404. The response carries every line, the totals, `complete`, `missing`, `estimates`, and `sources` per input (`source`, `as_of`, `raw_payload_id`).
- **Alternatives:** round only the final margin (the lines wouldn't sum on screen); convert one unit and multiply (q × the rounding error); FX cut off at capture time (every rate a day late, because of the daily job's lag); duty assumed 0, or a partial margin while duty isn't set (R-16 forbids both); `q × F(1)`, or a null-variant quote for any listing (R-16); a 308 redirect for a merged product on a POST (the Profit tab sits on the survivor's URL).
- **Why:** `packages/core/tests/test_landed_cost.py` holds 30 unit tests: the worked example to the cent ($15.03, 5012 bps), fees, duty not set, duty 0 is set, DDP, duty over 100%, returns, customs per order, half cents (0.5 → 0, 1.5 → 2, 2.5 → 2), JPY, KWD and CNY FX with a single rounding, FX missing, missing codes, a loss, owner estimates, and best(). `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py` holds 15 bounds cases and 6 Postgres tests: duty not set → DDP → estimate; workspace isolation; FX on the UTC date with a 4-day look-back and blend-before-manual; exact variant and quantity freight; the unquoted cheap supplier; 404s. 22 one-line mutations of these rules each fail a test (docs/reports/p2-10.md).
- **Not here:** the Profit tab UI (UX-8 part b); the `workspace_product_economics` view with golden-vector parity, and Discover best margin (UX-6 / UX-8 part c); the Suppliers table and picks; duty rules by product and category; bulk-tier unit pricing (the list price is used, the conservative cost).

### DEC-243 · Product Overview tab and context rail: one flat overview resource with per-stat provenance, workspace notes in keyset pages and tags by PUT, optimistic saves with rollback
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-07)** · Phase 2 task UX-4; DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-236, DEC-238, DEC-239; SPEC §6.4, §1, §10; vault Features/Product Page - Overview, Features/Product Page; no migration (0005's `product_notes` and `product_tags` are used as they are), no dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Resource.** `GET /v1/products/{id}/overview` (operationId `get_product_overview`) returns a flat `ProductOverview`:
    - `price_min_minor`, `price_median_minor` and `price_max_minor`;
    - `best_supplier_cost_minor` (`SupplierCost` = `Sourced` + `platform`);
    - `landed_margin_minor`, always `NoSource` ("The landed-cost engine arrives with UX-8.");
    - `first_seen_at` with `age_days`;
    - `velocity` (DEC-239's `Velocity | NoSource`);
    - `appearances` (`{vendor, count}` per source);
    - `ad_count`, `NoSource` ("No ad source is connected yet.").

    Every number is DEC-238's `Sourced | NoSource`, so `<Metric>` and the contract test apply unchanged. This replaces the vault's `{stats, explanation, mini_series, appearances, images, as_of}`: `explanation` (Phase 4), `mini_series` (UX-5) and `images` (M-2 b) join when they exist, as their own fields or resources. An unknown id is 404, and a merged id is the 409 problem `merged` ("Reload the page"). The page reaches the overview only through the header, which already 308s a merged id.
  - **(b) Stats.** Each stat uses the latest snapshot per live listing.
    - **Retail:** USD store listings only (owner decision 3; other currencies wait for an FX basis). The median is the lower middle (`percentile_disc(0.5)`), so it is always a price that was actually observed.
    - **Best supplier cost:** the lowest USD supplier price where `available` is not false and `stock` is null or above 0.
    - **Age:** the UTC date difference from `products.first_seen_at`. It is sourced by the earliest snapshot of any of the product's listings, and is `NoSource` when there is none.
    - **Velocity:** DEC-239(c) unchanged, with the same family ranking and the same reason. The UI says "Insufficient signal", like Discover, rather than "no source yet": a source exists, but no series qualifies.
    - **Appearances:** stores count distinct stores; suppliers count listings, because CJ writes one supplier row for all its listings, so a supplier count would always read 1.
  - **(c) Score cards.** Trend Score, Saturation and Lifecycle are not rendered at all before `ff_scores` (Phase 4) and are not in the payload. The grid holds five cards: 2-up on phones, 3 + 2 from 768 px, and 5 in a row from 1920 px.
  - **(d) Notes.** The workspace keeps many plain-text notes per product (the task brief), not the vault's single autosaved markdown note.
    - `GET /v1/products/{id}/notes` (`list_product_notes`) returns them newest first as `NotePage {items, next_cursor}`: 20 a page by default, at most 100. The cursor is the keyset `[created_at, id]` as JSON; a bad one gets the 422 problem `invalid_cursor`.
    - `POST` (`create_product_note`) takes `{body}`, 1–10,000 characters after trimming; extra fields get 422. It returns 201 with the note.
    - Notes are not edited or deleted here. The author is stored but not shown, because v1 has one user.
  - **(e) Tags.** `GET` / `PUT /v1/products/{id}/tags` (`list_product_tags`, `put_product_tags`). The PUT replaces the workspace's whole set:
    - at most 20 tags of 1–80 characters;
    - whitespace collapsed, and case-insensitive duplicates dropped, keeping the first spelling;
    - the set sorted case-insensitively.

    A whole-set PUT makes a retry idempotent, and the optimistic rollback is a plain restore. The vault's type-ahead from a `tags` table waits, because 0005 stores the tag text on `product_tags` directly.
  - **(f) Workspace scope.** Every notes and tags query filters on the caller's `Principal.workspace_id` from `current_principal`. A PUT deletes and inserts only inside that scope. A product is global market data; its notes and tags are not.
  - **(g) Optimistic saves.**
    - **Note:** it shows at once at the top as "Saving…" (60 % opacity) under a temporary id, and on success the server's note replaces it. On error it is removed, and its text goes back into the box unless something new has been typed. A `role="alert"` line gives the problem detail and the error ID.
    - **Tag change:** it writes the cache at once. On error the previous set is restored, the alert says "Your change was undone", and the tags are refetched.
  - **(h) Rail layout.** The rail is a 20 rem column beside every tab from 1280 px (`xl`), and stacks under the tab panel below that. Three vault items are deferred (backlog rows in `docs/reports/p2-07.md`): the overlay sheet between 1280 and 1440 px, the `\` toggle with its remembered state, and the bottom sheet from More on phones. Until then, stacking keeps the rail reachable at every width without adding new state.
  - **(i) Keyboard.**
    - **N** focuses the note box unless focus is already in a field (the shell's `isTyping`, now exported). **Ctrl/⌘ + Enter** adds the note.
    - **Enter** in the tag box adds a tag. A case-only repeat is refused inline ("Already tagged …"), and an empty box does nothing.
    - Removing a tag moves focus to the next chip's remove button, else the previous one, else the input.
    - The "Where it appears" buttons switch tabs and move focus to the opened tab's trigger.
    - The vault's "Enter on a focused card jumps to its tab" is deferred: each card's numbers are `<Metric>` buttons, so the card can't also be a button.
  - **(j) States.**
    - **Overview:** one request, so one loading skeleton (the exact grid) and one panel error with Retry. On a failed refetch it keeps its data under a stale line, and each card marks its own source as stale. The vault's per-card error waits until the cards have separate requests.
    - **Tags and notes panels:** each has a skeleton, an empty state, a panel error and a stale line. A failed "older notes" page fails at the foot of the list (the DEC-236 pattern).
  - **(k) Motion.** The Overview fades in like the other tabs' panels (opacity, the `fast` token). The vault's 40 ms card stagger is left out, so that one tab doesn't move differently from the rest.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Nesting the stats under `stats`: one more level for no gain. Reusing the header resource: the overview adds the median, the cost and velocity, which the header doesn't carry.
  - (d) The vault's single autosaved note: the brief asks for a list with POST, and an autosave can't roll back cleanly. Offset paging: unstable while notes are being added.
  - (e) `POST` / `DELETE` per tag: two endpoints, and a rollback per tag.
  - (h) Building the overlay sheet now: its `\` toggle and remembered state belong with the page's other shortcuts, and stacking already works.
- **Why:**
  - **API tests** (Postgres, `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`):
    - The overview stats of five fixture products equal a raw-SQL spot check. The fixtures cover CAD and delisted rows, an even count for the median, out-of-stock and unavailable offers, same-day family ranking, and a product with no features. Each stat traces to its payload.
    - An unknown product is 404, a merged one 409, and every endpoint is 401 without a principal.
    - Notes page newest first with no repeats. Bad bodies, cursors and limits get 422. Tags normalize and replace.
    - A note or tag written by workspace A is invisible to B, and B's PUT doesn't touch A's. Removing the workspace filter fails this test.
  - **Vitest:** the tag rules.
  - **Playwright** (12 tests), all against fixtures:
    - the five cards, and the score cards hidden;
    - each of the 8 numbers opening its provenance card by keyboard;
    - the links to Suppliers and Sellers;
    - the no source yet state (never 0), loading, error and stale;
    - N + Ctrl+Enter adding a note;
    - tags added, refused and removed, with focus kept;
    - optimistic notes, and the rollback of notes and tags on a 503;
    - older notes paging, and the rail's load errors;
    - no sideways scroll at 375 px.

### DEC-249 · Matcher (M-3): a hashed scorer config, a USD markup gate, HNSW top-20 candidates, and auto-links that move only product-less listings
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-13)** · Phase 2 task M-3; R-15; DEC-040, DEC-042, DEC-237, DEC-240; AGENTS.md §3 and §7; vault Phases/Phase 2 - Core Product, Data/Product Matching; `docs/reports/p2-13.md`; no migration or dependency
- **Decision:** every value below is a starting guess until the owner-labelled set calibrates it.
  - **(a) Score** (`pulso_ml.matching.score`): the weighted mean of the components present. The weights are image cosine 0.55, title cosine 0.30 and pHash similarity 0.15; pHash similarity is `1 − Hamming/64` on the signed 64-bit hash (`pulso_ml.phash.similarity`). A missing component has weight 0 and the rest renormalise (DEC-040). Components are rounded to their stored `numeric(5,4)` first, and the score to 4 places (half-even), then clamped to [0, 1]. A stored row therefore re-scores to its stored score, and the boundaries compare exactly. The vault's `title_trgm` 0.10 is folded into the title weight: this task builds no `pg_trgm` feature.
  - **(b) Routing:** a score ≥ 0.90 with a sane price links. A score ≥ 0.75 queues as `pending`, and so does ≥ 0.90 with an insane or unknown price. Below 0.75 a pair is discarded and not written. The price is a gate rather than the vault's "cap the score below the line", so the queue shows the real score next to `price_sane`.
  - **(c) Price sanity:** each listing's latest snapshot `price_min_minor` is converted to USD cents with `pulso_core.landed_cost.to_usd` (DEC-246's half-even `convert_minor(…, inverse=True)`), at the stored `fx_rates` rate as of the snapshot's `observed_for` (`rate_as_of`), never compared in raw minor units. For a supplier listing (`supplier_id` set) against a retail one, retail ÷ cost must sit in [1.2, 8]. For two retail prices, or two costs, max ÷ min must be ≤ 8 ÷ 1.2 ≈ 6.67, the widest spread two markups of one cost can have. A missing price, a missing rate or a non-positive price gives `price_sane` NULL, which never auto-links.
  - **(d) Versioned config:** one frozen `ScorerConfig` holds the weights, thresholds, top-k 20 and the band. Its `version` is `m3-` plus the first 12 hex of a sha256 over every value (DEC-040's hash rule). It is written to `match_candidates.scorer_version` and into each auto-link's `reason`. A test changes each field in turn and asserts a new version. The only setting is `MATCHER_ENABLED`, default off; no value has an env override.
  - **(e) Candidates:** every listing with an image embedding (its lowest-position embedded image) takes its top 20 listings by `images.embedding` cosine through the HNSW index. `hnsw.iterative_scan = strict_order` keeps the filters from starving the 20. Excluded: the listing itself, its own store, and listings on its canonical product, meaning that product and every product merged into it (`merged_into_id` followed). Listings without an embedding are counted as `skipped_no_embedding` and never scored. Each source listing is one transaction.
  - **(f) Upsert:** one row per ordered pair. A new pair is written only when it queues or links. An existing `pending` row is re-scored in place; the ORM writes only changed values, so a re-run on the same data writes nothing. `accepted`, `rejected`, `merged` and `split` rows are never touched.
  - **(g) Link direction:** only listings without a product move. One product-less listing joins the other listing's canonical product. Two product-less listings form a new product, named after the older one (`first_seen_at`, then id) and carrying its `first_seen_at`. Two listings already on different products stay `pending`: joining them is a product merge, which belongs to M-5 and the queue. So the algorithm never empties or splits a product. Each moved listing gets `product_id`, `match_confidence` = score and one `match_decisions` row in the same transaction: actor `algo`, action `link`, from NULL, to the product, `candidate_id`, confidence = score, and a reason naming the `scorer_version`. The candidate becomes `accepted` with `decided_at`.
  - **(h) Manual constraints:** a listing whose latest decision has a non-`algo` actor never moves. A listing never joins a product that holds a listing it has a `rejected` or `split` pair with, so a rejected pair can't be linked through a third listing. Both listings are locked `FOR UPDATE` before the decision.
  - **(i) Job:** Celery `ml.match`, routed to the `ml` queue (the workers' concurrency is 1), behind `MATCHER_ENABLED=off`. Off, it logs `matcher_disabled` and never opens a session. The counts (scored, linked, queued, discarded, skipped_no_embedding) go in the `matcher_done` message, because the JSON formatter drops extras. Rates come from `pulso_sources.fx_frankfurter.rate_as_of`, passed in by the worker because `pulso-sources` already depends on `pulso-ml`. There is no Beat entry yet, and the collector host runs no `ml` consumer.
- **Alternatives:** moving the listing with fewer snapshots (it moves listings off established products, and can empty a product or split a group); capping the score on an insane price (hides the real score); per-value env config (unversioned); a pinned version string plus a fingerprint test (two things to keep in step); the vault's title top-10 and pHash ≤ 8 candidate arms (left until M-2 part b gives real embeddings to tune them on).
- **Why:** fixed-vector tests cover both thresholds exactly and one step below each, renormalised missing components, an insane price that only queues, and JPY (exponent 0) and KWD (exponent 3) prices that raw minor units would misjudge. Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` show the neighbour query planned as `Index Scan using ix_images_embedding`, the same-store and same-product (merged included) exclusions, pending-only re-scoring, a rejected pair and a manual decision surviving re-runs, and an R-15 universe where every changed `listings.product_id` has exactly one decision and a second run writes nothing.
- **Not in this task:** OpenCLIP embeddings (M-2 part b; every real row's embedding is NULL today, so the matcher has no work yet), GTIN (M-4), the merge/split service and membership(t) replay (M-5), the queue UI and admin endpoints (UX-1), new singleton products for unmatched listings, an `ml` worker service and schedule on the collector host, and calibration on the owner's labels.

### DEC-250 · Landed-cost review fixes: freight from the newest capture only, lines past BIGINT read `amount_out_of_range`, `missing` in one fixed order, no DDP-unused estimates
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-14)** · P2-12 cross-review findings XR-16 (medium), XR-20 and XR-21 (low); refines DEC-246 (c) and (d), leaving its rounding, FX and completeness rules unchanged; vault Features/Product Page - Profit; no migration, dependency or UI
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Freight from the newest capture (XR-16).** `freight_quotes` keeps only the rows of the newest freight capture per (listing, variant, quantity, origin): newest `captured_at`, and on a tie the later `raw_payload_id`. In SQL: `rank() OVER (PARTITION BY coalesce(variant_id, :single_variant), origin_country ORDER BY captured_at DESC, raw_payload_id DESC) = 1`, over the listing's US rows at that quantity. DEC-246's per-method and cheapest-in-USD rules then apply to those rows only. A requested `method` is filtered after the ranking, so a method's own last capture never counts as the newest. A method the newest capture no longer lists is never priced. Without a method it can't win. When it is requested, the result reads `freight_missing`, unless another variant's newest capture still lists it, which reads `freight_variant_mismatch` as before. The query reads one listing through its index, and Postgres stops each partition at rank 1 (a `Run Condition` in the plan).
  - **(b) A null-variant quote on a single-variant listing** shares the variant's newest capture (`coalesce(variant_id, the single live variant)`). DEC-246 (d) already treats the two as one freight question, and two separate "newest" captures would let an older null-variant method win again. On a multi-variant listing the key is `variant_id` itself.
  - **(c) Lines past BIGINT (XR-20).** Any computed line or total outside ±(2^63 − 1), the column type and the API's integer contract, is withheld as null and named `amount_out_of_range`. This covers goods, freight, duty, landed cost, the payment fee, the margin and `margin_bps`. It is the seventh `missing` code, so the row is incomplete and its margin null. A non-USD amount that `convert_minor` rejects past BIGINT reads the same way, so USD and non-USD amounts behave alike. Without a method, a freight quote past BIGINT sorts last, like one without FX. The Shopify fee and returns are at most the price (0..10000 bps at the API and by the settings check constraints), so they are not checked. The bound stays BIGINT, not 2^53: DEC-246 (g) already accepts money up to 2^63 − 1.
  - **(d) `missing` order (XR-21).** `missing` is always sorted by `pulso_core.landed_cost.MISSING`: DEC-246 (c)'s order, then `amount_out_of_range`. An unknown code raises an error instead of being dropped. The API's `Missing` literal must equal `MISSING`, and a test checks this.
  - **(e) DDP (XR-21).** DDP zeroes duty and the customs fee, so `duty_bps` and `customs_fee_minor` feed nothing. Under DDP they are left out of `estimates` and of `sources`, whether they come from an override or from settings. This narrows DEC-246 (c)'s "overrides are listed in estimates" to overrides that are actually used.
  - **(f) No freshness flag.** `sources.freight.as_of` already carries the chosen quote's capture time, and the stale cue belongs to the Profit tab (UX-8 part b).
- **Alternatives:** keeping per-method history and adding a stale flag (an old method could still win while marked fresh enough); grouping strictly by `variant_id`, so on a single-variant listing an old null-variant quote kept winning; answering a 422 for amounts past BIGINT (one corrupt supplier row would fail the whole best-supplier pick, and the router would have to map `ValueError`); bounding at 2^53 (it contradicts DEC-246 (g)'s request bounds); keeping DDP-unused inputs in `sources` with a new "unused" flag (a response field for a label nothing reads).
- **Why:** the review's probe (Cheap $3 + Std $5 on day 1, only Std on day 30) answered Cheap at a $2.00-too-high margin with `complete: true`, which is the wrong-value case R-16 exists to stop. `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py` adds 3 Postgres tests: the probe, with a second origin and a single-variant listing; a same-timestamp tie in both orders; and DDP plus a price and fee past BIGINT through the API. It also adds a check that the literal equals `MISSING`. `packages/core/tests/test_landed_cost.py` adds 6 unit cases for XR-20 and XR-21. 40 one-line mutations each fail a test: p2-10's 22, with "customs per unit" applied both to the line and to the landed sum, plus 17 for this change (docs/reports/p2-14.md).

### DEC-253 · Categories: Google Product Taxonomy 2021-09-21 vendored in `pulso_core`, an idempotent loader keyed on Google's id, and a reviewed rules file plus a title fallback for `category_source='rule'`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-17)** · Phase 2 task M-6; DEC-040, DEC-237; SPEC §4.4, §6.17; vault Data/Product Matching, Data/Data Model (categories); no migration, dependency, Celery task or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Reference data, not a source.** `packages/core/src/pulso_core/data/google_product_taxonomy.txt` is Google's en-US file with ids (`https://www.google.com/basepages/producttype/taxonomy-with-ids.en-US.txt`). It was fetched once on 2026-10-25 and kept unmodified: version `2021-09-21`, 5,595 categories, sha256 `30039729…478fce`. No licence or reuse terms are stated on the file or on its Merchant Center help page (`support.google.com/merchants/answer/6324436`); only Google's generic Terms of Service apply. We use it inside Pulso to classify products, attributed in `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md` § Reference data. It is not under `infra/seeds/` (a protected path), and it ships in the `pulso-core` wheel.
  - **(b) Loader** (`python -m pulso_core.taxonomy load`). Google's numeric id is `categories.id` (INTEGER; the largest is 543703). `parent_id` is the parent path's id, `name` the last segment, `path` the full ` > ` path. One `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE … WHERE (parent_id, name, path) IS DISTINCT FROM excluded` returns only rows added or changed, so a rerun changes 0. Parsing raises on an orphan or a duplicate id or path. The loader never deletes: a category a future version drops stays for the products that point at it.
  - **(c) Rules** (`pulso_core/data/category_rules.toml`, reviewed configuration). Values are Google paths, not ids, so a reviewer reads them. Loading raises on any path the vendored file lacks. A source category (today `listings.product_type`) resolves in this order:
    1. its source's table (`[sources.shopify_stores]`, `[sources.cj]`);
    2. any Google path or Google category name used only once ("Dog Beds", "Necklaces");
    3. both steps again on the value's last `›` / `>` segment, so CJ's "Sports & Outdoors › Fitness" resolves through "fitness".

    Matching is case-insensitive with spacing collapsed. Each table is per source.
  - **(d) Title fallback.** `[keywords]` holds phrases matched as whole words in listing titles, then the product name. A trailing plural folds away on both sides. The longest phrase wins, then the earliest in the title. The keys cover the 50 seed niches and common product nouns; a single word is used only when it rarely means anything else (no bare "cat", "dog", "ring" or "cap").
  - **(e) Refinement.** A title category that sits under the source category refines it: "Home" + "Sunset lamp" gives Lamps. A title category elsewhere in the tree never overrides the source category: "Pet Supplies" + "Sunset lamp" stays Pet Supplies.
  - **(f) Products** (`python -m pulso_core.taxonomy map` loads, then applies, in one transaction). For each non-merged product with `category_source` NULL or `'rule'`:
    - the source category is the first one that resolves among its listings, by id;
    - the title category is the first title that matches, the product name last;
    - the result of (e) is written with `category_source='rule'`;
    - a product with no match is set to NULL / NULL. Rule categories are re-derived each run, so a removed rule clears its own guesses.

    `manual` and `ai` products are never selected, and the UPDATE's WHERE checks again, so a manual category set mid-run still wins.
  - **(g) Mappings.** Each distinct (source, normalised source category) that the rules resolve is upserted into `category_mappings` with `mapping_source='rule'`. A `manual` or `ai` mapping row wins over the rules when resolving, and is never overwritten: the upsert's `WHERE mapping_source='rule'`. Rule rows that stop resolving are left in place, since nothing reads rule rows back.
  - **(h) No Celery task yet.** The task brief made one optional. The one-shot command covers M-6 until the mapper should run after each collector run. Unresolved products stay NULL, and the UI already reads them as a muted "Uncategorised" (Discover card and table, product header), so no UI changed.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Shopify's open product taxonomy: newer and openly published, but its ids are not Google's, and the data model fixes Google ids (Data Model §Catalog). Downloading at load time: a network call from the loader, and an unpinned version.
  - (c) Ids in the rules file: shorter, but a reviewer can't read them. YAML: a new dependency, where TOML is stdlib (`tomllib`).
  - (d) Tags as a rule family: store tags are mostly merchandising noise ("sale", "new", collection names). Product type plus title keep the rules smaller; add tags if the collector-host coverage report shows a gap.
  - (f) First match only, without refinement: broad product types such as "Home" would leave Discover's category filter coarse. Writing only NULL products: a fixed rule could never correct its own earlier guess.
- **Why:** `packages/core/tests/test_taxonomy.py` (pure, plus Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`):
  - parsing and parent integrity on a trimmed copy and on the vendored file (5,595 rows, every parent present);
  - the loader is idempotent (9 rows, then 0, then 1 after a rename), and all 5,595 rows' parents match their paths;
  - each rule family: Shopify product type (a rule, a unique Google name, a full path, a last segment), CJ category (the `›` path), per-source tables, title fallback (plural folding, longest wins), refinement, no match;
  - every seed niche has a title rule;
  - the apply job writes `rule` categories and mappings, clears a stale rule guess, uses the product name, and changes 0 on a rerun;
  - a manual or AI category survives; a manual mapping wins and is kept;
  - mutation checked: removing both `category_source` guards fails the manual test, and 13 one-line mutations of the module each fail a test (`docs/reports/p2-17.md`).

  `packages/sources/tests/test_category_coverage.py` passes the existing fixture payloads through `shopify_stores.records.parse` and `cj.parse` (253 distinct products) into the mapper: 252 get a category (99.6 %), and "Fixture bowl" stays NULL / NULL.
- **Not here:**
  - CJ listings store no category today: CJ's `parse()` drops it and the listing has no `product_type`. CJ's rules wait for the CJ adapter to keep the panel's category name (a BACKLOG row), so until then CJ products rely on the title fallback.
  - The fixture coverage figure is weak evidence: every Shopify fixture product is typed "Home". Real coverage is measured by `map` on the collector host.
  - AI categorisation (`ai`), a manual-mapping UI, a Celery task, and pruning stale rule mappings.

### DEC-247 · Sellers tab: per-store complete-crawl delisting, active-only spread at snapshot-date FX, stable-panel new_14d, a gap-marked 90-day series, and a band that waits for landed cost
**2026-10-24 · proposed (p2-11)** · Phase 2 task UX-7; DEC-031, DEC-033, DEC-040, DEC-088, DEC-238, DEC-239; review M4, M7, M15; SPEC §6.4; vault Features/Product Page - Sellers, Intelligence/Saturation Score; no migration, no dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Resource.** `GET /v1/products/{id}/sellers` (operationId `get_product_sellers`), workspace-scoped through `current_principal`, returns `SellersPage {rows, next_cursor, spread, band, counts, series_90d, crawler, as_of}`. Filters: `include_delisted`, `new_14d`, `tracked` (the UI sends one at a time); `cursor` and `limit` (1–200, default 100). Rows sort active first, then newest start, then newest listing; the cursor is `[delisted, started_at, listing_id]`. A merged id answers 308 to `../{survivor}/sellers` with the query string kept, as DEC-238 does for the header. Existing tables only.
  - **(b) Whole set per request.** A product's seller listings (a few hundred at most) load in one query, so the summary always covers every row and the keyset page is cut in Python. Moving the keyset into SQL waits until a product carries thousands of sellers (`ponytail:` note in the router).
  - **(c) Still listed.** `delisted` when `listings.delisted_at` is set. Its only writer is the Shopify catalog finalize, which runs only for a store whose `shopify_catalog` target in `collector_run_targets` reached `success`. That target is the per-store unit of a complete crawl: `collector_runs.complete` is the AND over every store in the run, so one slow store would otherwise hide every other store's delisting. With `delisted_at` empty the row is `unknown` when the store is `blocked` or `untrackable`, when its latest non-pending catalog target is `partial` or `failed` (`last_crawl_incomplete`), or when it has never had one (`never_crawled`, such as an imported listing); otherwise `listed`. A pending target (in flight, or left by a crashed run) is skipped. A partial or blocked crawl never produces `delisted`.
  - **(d) Active** means not delisted: `listed` or `unknown`, the same set as the header's store count (DEC-238). `counts.active` and `counts.delisted_30d` count distinct stores.
  - **(e) Started** is the listing's `source_created_at`, else `source_published_at` (basis `store`), else its `first_seen_at` (basis `first_seen`). The UI always says which.
  - **(f) Spread** over active, trackable stores, one point per store (its lowest current price). USD rows use the snapshot's own price; other currencies convert at `rate_as_of` for the snapshot's UTC capture date (DEC-088's carry-forward of the last observed rate before that day's cutoff; it never reads a later rate), with `convert_minor(..., inverse=True, rounding=ROUND_HALF_EVEN)`. A row with no rate is left out and counted in `missing`, together with rows that have no price yet. Untrackable stores are left out and counted in `untrackable` for the footnote. Percentiles use `statistics.quantiles(n=4, method="inclusive")`; p25, median, mean and p75 round to whole minor units. `points` lists the plotted dots, bounded by the product's seller count.
  - **(g) `new_14d` stable panel (review M15).** Neither DECISIONS nor the vault defined it for sellers, so: an active listing whose store Pulso has had for at least 28 days (`stores.first_seen_at <= now − 28 d`, the panel DEC-040 and Saturation Score use) and whose start (e) is within the last 14 days. A store added in a new batch is never "new", whatever its listings' dates. The New in 14 d filter uses the same predicate.
  - **(h) `series_90d`.** 90 UTC days ending today. A day is observed only if some `shopify_catalog` target succeeded on it; its value is the number of the product's stores with a listing live at the end of that day (`first_seen_at` before the next midnight, and not delisted before it), and `as_of` is that day's last successful crawl. Every other day is `gap: true` with `active: null`, never interpolated (AGENTS.md §4). A partial-only day is a gap. Listing history that a relisting reset (`delisted_at` back to null) is not reconstructed.
  - **(i) Crawler** from the `shopify_stores` source row: `enabled`, else `auto_disabled` when `status = 'blocked'` (the framework's block rule), else `disabled`; `since` and `reason` are `disabled_at` and `disabled_reason`. The tab shows its banner whenever the status isn't `enabled`.
  - **(j) Band.** `{low: null, high: null, reason: "needs_landed_cost"}` until UX-8's `landed_cost()` and UX-10's target margin exist. The market p25–p75 alone is never offered as a suggestion: the spread card labels it "Middle 50%", and the band card shows a designed no source yet state.
  - **(k) Running ads** is `NoSource` on every row (review M4); the UI reads "No source yet", never "No".
  - **(l) Provenance.** Each row carries the price snapshot's `as_of` and `raw_payload_id`, `last_seen` and `stale` (twice the source's expected interval, as DEC-238). Every schema in the response that holds a number has a required `as_of`, so the existing contract test covers the new path.
  - **(m) Web.** One chip at a time in `?sellers=` (`all`, `new`, `tracked`; Active only is the default and never written), set with `history.replaceState` like the tab bar; F cycles it. Rows: roving focus, `j`/`k` or arrows move, Enter or `o` opens the listing on the store in a new tab. Store Spy (`/stores/[id]`) is Phase 3, so no row links there yet. Below 768 px sellers are cards and delisted ones fold into a native `<details>` "Delisted (n)" group. The sparkline and strip plot are inline SVG; only the strip plot's dots fade in once (opacity), and rows never animate.
- **Alternatives:** run-level `collector_runs.complete` for (c) (hides per-store delistings behind any one slow store); counting `unknown` as inactive in (d) (a paused crawler would zero the active count); the latest FX rate for (f) (rewrites history with today's rate); stores first seen in the last 14 days for (g) (Pulso's own coverage growth reads as market entry, the M15 trap); carrying a store's last known state across unobserved days in (h) (interpolation); SQL keyset paging (more code for a few hundred rows).
- **Why:** fixture tests on Postgres (`apps/api/tests/test_product_sellers.py`): a listing omitted by a complete crawl reads delisted with its date while partial, blocked and untrackable stores read unknown; CAD converts at the snapshot date's 1.25, not today's 2; EUR without a rate is left out and counted; the untrackable and delisted prices stay out of the spread; only the stable-panel store counts as new; partial-only and uncrawled days are gaps; a two-row cursor walk returns every row once in the same order; the tracked flag follows the workspace; a blocked source reads `auto_disabled`; a merged id redirects. Breaking the incomplete-crawl rule fails a test.
- **Not here:** the real suggested band (after UX-8 and UX-10), Add-by-URL, the price-war alert (Phase 7), per-seller unit estimates and the quantity-break column (Phase 3), store makeup chips (Phase 7), ads data, compare-at, availability and 30-day price change columns, column sorting, and the Store Spy row link.

### DEC-255 · GTIN shortcut (M-4): valid shared GTINs link at score 1.0 before the image matcher, through M-3's link rules
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-19)** · Phase 2 task M-4; R-15; DEC-042, DEC-249; AGENTS.md §3; vault Phases/Phase 2 - Core Product, Data/Product Matching; `docs/reports/p2-19.md`; no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Validation** (`pulso_ml.matching.gtin.gtin14`, pure):
    - whitespace and dashes are stripped, and only 8, 12, 13 or 14 ASCII digits pass;
    - the code is left-padded to 14 digits (GS1's GTIN-14 form), so UPC-A `036000291452` and EAN-13 `0036000291452` compare equal;
    - the GS1 mod-10 check digit must match.
    - Rejected even with a valid check digit: placeholders (all zeros, one repeated digit, or an ascending or descending run once leading zeros are dropped, such as `123456789012`, `012345678905` and `987654321098`), and GS1 Restricted Circulation Numbers (GS1 prefixes 020–029, 040–049 and 200–299; GTIN-8 prefixes 0 and 2). Companies and regions issue RCNs internally, so two vendors can use one for different products.
    - Anything else is ignored, never repaired or guessed. An 8-digit UPC-E reads as a GTIN-8, and its leading 0 makes it an RCN-8, so it never links: a missed link, never a false one.
  - **(b) Groups:** every `listing_variants.barcode`, live or delisted, is normalised and grouped by GTIN-14. A vendor (a store or a supplier; a listing with neither is its own vendor) that puts one GTIN on two or more of its listings drops out of that GTIN, because it isn't using the code as an identifier. So no same-store pair links by GTIN, and one store reusing a code across its catalogue can't pull many products onto one.
  - **(c) Precedence:** the GTIN pass runs first in `ml.match`, before candidate generation.
    - Every pair left in a group gets a `match_candidates` row with score 1.0 and `scorer_version` `gtin-1`. The image, title and pHash components are untouched: NULL on a new row, kept as evidence on a row M-3 had queued.
    - An existing `pending` row is raised to 1.0 / `gtin-1`. M-3 never re-scores a `gtin-1` row, so a GTIN pair is never lowered to its image score and re-runs don't flip it back and forth.
    - There is no price gate: GTIN equality is exact evidence, and M-3's markup band is for fuzzy matches.
    - `gtin-1` is a pinned version, bumped by hand when (a) changes. M-3 scores a row left at an older GTIN version again.
  - **(d) Link:** each pair goes through M-3's `link()` (DEC-249 g, h) with confidence 1.0 and reason `gtin-1: GTIN <14 digits>`.
    - Only product-less listings move. Two product-less listings form a new product named after the older one; one joins the other listing's canonical product. Two listings on different products stay `pending` for M-5 and the queue.
    - Each moved listing gets one `match_decisions` row (actor `algo`, action `link`, `candidate_id`, confidence 1.0000) and `listings.match_confidence` 1.0, and the candidate becomes `accepted`.
    - One transaction per GTIN.
  - **(e) Manual decisions win:**
    - `accepted`, `rejected`, `merged` and `split` pairs are never touched;
    - a listing whose latest decision is manual never moves, and its pair stays `pending` at 1.0 for the owner;
    - a listing never joins a product holding the other side of its rejected or split pair, so a rejected pair isn't linked through a third listing that shares their GTIN.
  - **(f) Flag and counts:** behind the existing `MATCHER_ENABLED` (default off; off, the job never opens a session), with no new flag. The `matcher_done` line adds `gtin_linked` (pairs linked) and `gtin_queued` (pairs left pending).
- **Alternatives:**
  - links without a candidate row: nothing in the queue for two-product or manual pairs, and no `candidate_id`;
  - a separate GTIN flag: the pass is part of the matcher job, which is already off by default;
  - linking same-store pairs: stores reuse barcodes;
  - accepting RCNs: internal numbering collides across vendors;
  - expanding UPC-E: an 8-digit code is ambiguous between GTIN-8 and UPC-E, and precision matters more than recall here;
  - a price gate on GTIN pairs.
- **Why:**
  - Pure tests cover each length, formatting, a wrong check digit, non-ASCII digits, placeholders with valid check digits, and each RCN range.
  - Postgres tests show a UPC-A and an EAN-13 of one code linking at 1.0 with one algo decision per listing, and a second run writing nothing. A wrong check digit, a placeholder, an RCN and a store reusing a code never link. Rejected or split, owner-unlinked, two-product and queued pairs resolve as above.
  - Mutating the check digit, the confidence, the reason, the decided-pair guard, the vendor-reuse rule or M-3's `gtin-1` skip each fails a test.
- **Not here:** supplier barcodes (only Shopify's product `.js` parser stores `barcode` today; neither CJ writer does, so GTIN links are store-to-store until a supplier adapter keeps one), the queue UI, and M-5's merge of two-product pairs.

### DEC-252 · Global search: one grouped resource ranked exact, prefix, substring, then fuzzy; pg_trgm GIN indexes (migration 0008); search rows under the commands in the palette
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-16)** · Phase 2 task UX-9; DEC-238, DEC-239; SPEC §6.18; vault Features/Command Palette & Search, Design/UI States; migration 0008 (indexes only), no dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Resource.** `GET /v1/search?q=&limit=` (operationId `search`) returns `SearchResults {products, stores, suppliers, catalog_empty}`, best first in each group. `q` is trimmed: under 2 characters answers empty groups without a query, over 100 is a 422, and a missing `q` is a 422. `limit` is per group, 1 to 20, default 5. It takes `current_principal` (401 without one) and no workspace filter: only market tables are searched, never notes or tags. Every hit carries `match {field, text, kind}`: which column matched, its value (a listing's or a merged product's title can differ from the hit's), and the kind.
  - **(b) What is searched.** Products by name and by their live listings' titles; stores by `domain` and `myshopify_domain` (stores have no name column, so the myshopify domain stands in for the brief's "name"); suppliers by `name`. A product hit is `{id, title, category}`: text only, no thumbnail and no numbers, so there is no provenance to carry.
  - **(c) Ranking.** `kind` is `exact` (case-insensitive equality), `prefix`, `substring` (ILIKE with `%`, `_` and the escape character escaped), then `fuzzy`: pg_trgm word similarity (`col %> q` at the default 0.6 threshold), which catches a one-letter typo in a word. Fuzzy starts at 3 characters, because 2 characters make one real trigram. Within a kind: higher `word_similarity`, then shorter text, then text and key. One hit per entity: `DISTINCT ON` the key keeps its best candidate, and the product's own title wins a tie with a listing title.
  - **(d) Merged products** resolve to `coalesce(merged_into_id, id)` and appear once, under the survivor's title. That is one hop: a chained merge's middle product would answer 308 again on its page (DEC-238 d), a case the matcher doesn't produce yet.
  - **(e) Where a store or supplier opens.** Discover filters by source, not by store or supplier, so a store or supplier row opens its **top product**: its live listed product with the best G7 (Discover's momentum, DEC-239 c), then the newest, resolved to a merge survivor. `top_product_id` is null when it lists nothing: the row still shows, reads "No products yet" and can't be selected. The footer's Enter hint reads "Open top product" on those rows, and their accessible name says "(opens its top product)". BACKLOG has Discover `store` / `supplier` filters; Store Spy (Phase 3) gives stores their own page.
  - **(f) Store freshness.** A store hit carries `last_crawled_at` and `stale` (older than twice the `shopify_stores` source's `expected_interval`, DEC-238's rule; never stale without an interval). The row reads "Crawled 6h ago" with the UTC time on hover, or "Stale: crawled 3d ago" in the warning token with a glyph.
  - **(g) No source yet.** `catalog_empty` is true only when nothing matched and no product, store or supplier exists. The palette then says "Nothing collected yet" and offers Open Data Health.
  - **(h) Indexes (migration 0008).** GIN `gin_trgm_ops` on `products.name`, `listings.title`, `stores.domain`, `stores.myshopify_domain` and `suppliers.name`, declared on the models with a `trigram()` helper beside `hnsw_cosine()`; `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm` (0001 installs it already) and a drop-only downgrade. The timing test asked for them: without the indexes the store-domain p95 was 239 ms, because word similarity over 30,000 listing titles took 100–190 ms. With them listings use the index; the planner still seq-scans the 10,000-row products table for long queries (about 40 ms), since it prices `%>` per row as cheap, and switches to the index as the table grows.
  - **(i) Palette.** cmdk's `filter` gives every search row (value prefix `search:`) a fixed score of 1e-6 and the commands their usual `defaultFilter` score, so search rows show for any query and their groups sort under the commands that match. Products, Stores, Suppliers and a Search status group mount together, so cmdk keeps that order. cmdk re-sorts only enabled rows, which leaves disabled ones on top, so a store's or supplier's rows with no top product sit in a second group just below (headed only when alone) and the API's order holds. The query is debounced 150 ms (the brief's figure; the vault said 120) and fetched only from 2 characters, with `keepPreviousData`, a 60 s stale time and one retry on a 5xx. States: the first query shows a "Searching…" row; a later one keeps the last results while the search icon turns into a spinner (`aria-busy` on the list; the spinner holds still under reduced motion); "No matches for “q”"; no source yet (g); "Search unavailable. Commands still work." with a selectable Retry. A polite live region announces settled states only ("2 products and 1 store for “la”"). The matched part of a label is emphasised, and a listing-title match reads "Listed as “…”". Esc clears, then closes, as before.
  - **(j) Timing test.** `test_search_p95_under_200_ms_on_a_realistic_catalog` seeds 10,000 products, 30,000 listings, 2,000 stores, 500 suppliers and a feature row per product, then runs VACUUM ANALYZE on them (autovacuum's steady state: a bulk insert leaves a GIN pending list that skews plans). It asserts the p95 of 40 warm requests per query under 200 ms locally and 500 ms when `CI` is set (shared runners are slower), for a store domain, a store prefix, a supplier name and word, product words, a typo and a 2-letter query.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (c) The vault's generated `tsvector` on products for full-text ranking: stemming does nothing for domains or typos, and trigrams cover substrings and typos with one index type. Add it if ranking long product names falls short. Boosting tracked or watchlisted entities waits for those features.
  - (e) A store page, or Discover store and supplier filters, now: outside this task (BACKLOG).
  - (h) No migration: misses the 200 ms target (239 ms). `SET enable_seqscan = off`, or raising `word_similarity_commutator_op`'s cost, to force the index at 10,000 rows: fragile, and the second changes an extension's own object.
  - (i) `shouldFilter={false}` with the palette filtering commands itself: re-implements cmdk's ranking of commands. `forceMount` search rows: cmdk doesn't register them, so it sorts command groups around them unpredictably and `Command.Empty` miscounts.
- **Why:** API tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`: a store domain, a myshopify domain and a one-letter typo of it return the store with its top product (the higher G7) and its stale flag; a supplier name returns it with its top product; ranking is exact, prefix, substring, fuzzy; a listing title finds its product once; a merged product and its listing resolve to the survivor once, and a store listing the merged product opens the survivor; limits default to 5 and cap at 20 (0 and 21 are 422); bounds (under 2 characters, LIKE wildcards literal, 100 after trimming, 101 and a missing `q` are 422); `catalog_empty`; 401 without a session; and the timing test (j). Vitest covers the view state (debounce, stale results kept, an earlier empty answer never shown as this query's, error and retry) and the announcements. Playwright (9 tests, fixtures only) covers a store domain typed key by key making one request and Enter opening its top product, a supplier name, group order and arrow keys skipping rows with nowhere to go, loading and stale results under a spinner, no matches, no source yet, error and Retry, Esc, and 375 px with a tap and no sideways scroll.

### DEC-257 · Merge/split service (M-5): owner decisions in `pulso_ml.matching.decisions`, splits held as cannot-links against the old product, one matching lock, and membership(t) by replay
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-21)** · Phase 2 task M-5; R-15; DEC-031, DEC-042, DEC-237, DEC-249, DEC-255; AGENTS.md §3; vault Phases/Phase 2 - Core Product, Data/Product Matching, Features/Matching Queue; `docs/reports/p2-21.md`; no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Service.** `pulso_ml.matching.decisions` holds four owner operations. Each takes an actor (`owner` or a user id) and a reason, takes the matching lock (f) first, flushes, and leaves the commit to the caller (UX-1's endpoints). Every changed `listings.product_id` gets one `match_decisions` row in the same transaction, from the old pointer to the new, with the candidate when there is one. A moved listing's `match_confidence` becomes NULL: it holds the matcher's score, and the pointer is now the owner's.
    - **merge(A into B)** returns the survivor. Both ids follow `merged_into_id` first, and one product raises. A gets `merged_into_id` = B and `status` `merged`; it is never deleted. Every listing on A, or on a product merged into A earlier, moves with action `merge`. Pending candidates that the move puts on one product become `merged` with `decided_at`.
    - **split(listings, into?)**: the listings must share one product, and at least one stays. Moving a whole product is a merge, so a split never empties a product. They move with action `split` onto `into` or onto a new product named after the older listing and carrying its `first_seen_at` (DEC-249 g's rule; it equals the Matching Queue's earliest-member rule). Existing candidates between moved and staying listings become `split`, unless already `rejected` or `split`. No row is inserted for a pair without one: `score` is NOT NULL and there is no score to write.
    - **accept(candidate)**, pending only: two products merge through merge(), the younger (`first_seen_at`, then id) into the older, and the candidate becomes `merged`. Otherwise a product-less listing joins the other's product, or two form a new product named after the older listing, with action `accept`; the candidate becomes `accepted`.
    - **reject(candidate)**, pending only: one `reject` row per listing with from = to = its current pointer (nothing moves), and the candidate becomes `rejected`.
  - **(b) A split is a cannot-link against the old product, read from the decision log.** Both matcher passes skip a pair when one listing has a `split` decision whose `from_product_id` is, or was merged into, the other listing's canonical product. A split listing is therefore never paired with its old product again, whatever the score or GTIN, including with listings that join that product later (the Matching Queue's "cannot-link against the old product"). The matcher already never moves a listing on a product (DEC-249 g) or one whose latest move is manual (DEC-249 h), so algo never re-links or re-merges a split. (b) also stops the queue from suggesting the merge again.
  - **(c) A reject holds its pair, not its listings.** `manual()` now skips `reject` rows. A listing whose only owner decision is a reject may still auto-link elsewhere, while the `rejected` candidate and DEC-249 h's third-listing veto keep the pair apart.
  - **(d) Two-product pairs** that M-3 and the GTIN pass leave `pending` (DEC-249 g, DEC-255 d) stay pending for the queue. A human accept merges them through merge().
  - **(e) membership(t).** `membership(session, t)` returns each listing's `to_product_id` from its latest decision with `decided_at < t` (ties broken by id), one `DISTINCT ON` pass over the `(listing_id, decided_at)` index. It never reads `listings.product_id` or `merged_into_id`, which hold today's state. A listing with no decision before t, or whose latest decision leaves it product-less, is absent. A merge moves listings with decisions, so a merged product's listings replay to it before the merge and to the survivor from the merge on. A feature for day d uses `t = d+1 00:00Z` (DEC-031).
  - **(f) One lock.** Every matching write takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(0x50554C534D415443)` first: each matcher transaction in both passes, and each service call. A merge can't land between the matcher reading a pointer and moving a listing, which would leave a listing on a merged product. The single bigint key never meets the workers' two-int Beat and source locks.
  - **(g) Supersedes DEC-042's "Phase 6 decides whether features read membership as of *t*":** from Phase 2, any feature that groups listings by product reads `membership(t)` at its cutoff, never the current `listings.product_id`, and the leakage test treats a current-mapping read as a failure. Live views (product header, Sellers, Profit) keep reading the current pointer.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (b) Placeholder `split` candidates for every moved × staying pair: a made-up score, and a listing that joins the old product later would still be re-suggested.
  - (c) Counting rejects as manual: one rejected pair would freeze a CJ listing against every store and send all its matches to the queue.
  - (a) The larger product survives an accept: needs a count, and the older product is the one more likely to carry history. Moving workspace rows (notes, tags) on merge: the vault notes disagree (Product Matching resolves them through `merged_into_id`; Matching Queue's merge dialog moves them), so UX-1 decides with the dialog.
  - (e) A materialised membership table: only if replay measures slow (R-15).
  - (f) Per-product row locks: the matcher locks pairs and products in arbitrary order within a transaction, so a merge could deadlock with it, or miss a listing linked while it scanned.
- **Why:** Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` (`packages/ml/tests/test_matcher.py`):
  - after a manual split, a score and GTIN re-run at 1.0 writes no candidate, decision or product row, and a listing that joins the old product later isn't paired with the split one;
  - a merge moves all three listings (one left on an earlier-merged product included), with one `merge` row each, sets `merged_into_id` and `merged`, settles the pending pair, and a re-run writes nothing;
  - accept merges the younger product into the older, or links two product-less listings onto a new product with one `accept` row each; reject writes one row per listing and leaves each side free to link;
  - membership before a later split and merge returns the earlier grouping, and membership now equals every replayed listing's pointer;
  - a service call holds the matching lock until commit.
  - Seven mutations each fail a test: the split check dropped (from either pass alone too), rejects counted as manual, split marking no candidates, merge moving only the product itself, merge settling nothing, membership ignoring the cutoff, and split skipping the lock.
- **Not here:** API endpoints and the `/admin/matching` UI (UX-1), undo (UX-1's compensating decisions), unlink, moving workspace rows on merge, and feature code (nothing computes product features yet, so no current-mapping fallback exists to remove).

### DEC-251 · Settings page: the existing `/v1/settings` resource grows duty, returns and the import fee; null duty is "not set"; the first read creates the row; one form with one Save
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-15)** · Phase 2 task UX-10; DEC-004, DEC-040, DEC-237 (h), DEC-243, DEC-246; R-16; SPEC §6.18, §5 settings, §6.4; AGENTS.md §7 (stable operation IDs) and §8; vault Features/Settings; no migration, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Endpoints stay where they are.** `GET /v1/settings` (`get_workspace_settings`) and `PATCH /v1/settings` (`patch_workspace_settings`) in `routers/account.py` keep their routes and operation IDs (AGENTS.md §7; the lead's answer to this task). The brief asked for a new `settings.py` router with `get_settings` / `put_settings`; that would have renamed a live operation and duplicated the route, so the page builds on the existing pair and adds no operation.
  - **(b) Fields.** `SettingsResponse` and `SettingsPatch` add `duty_default_bps`, `returns_bps` and `customs_fee_minor` (0005's columns, DEC-237 h). Rates are basis points and money is minor units of the row's `currency`, as before. Bounds mirror the table: fee, returns and target bps 0..10000; money 0..2^63−1; duty `>= 0` with the Profit override's cap of 100000 (1,000%, DEC-246 g), since the table only checks `>= 0` and an `INTEGER` needs a ceiling. Strict integers; unknown fields 422; country and currency are `Literal["US"]` / `Literal["USD"]`, so echoing them is fine and changing them is 422.
  - **(c) Null duty.** In a PATCH an omitted field is unchanged, and `null` is rejected for every field except `duty_default_bps`, where it means "duty not set" (R-16). `null` and `0` stay distinct end to end: `0` makes margins complete with no duty; `null` keeps them incomplete with `duty_not_set`.
  - **(d) First read creates the row.** `workspace_settings()` inserts the default row with `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (workspace_id) DO NOTHING` when it is missing, then reads it, instead of a 404. Provisioning already creates the row, so this only covers a workspace made another way; `POST /v1/profit/calc` uses the same helper.
  - **(e) The page** is one form with one Save, not a Save per section: the v1 surface is eight fields, and one Save is the fewest clicks (edit, then Save or Ctrl/⌘+S). Sections: Market (US / USD as read-only text with a lock, not disabled inputs), Fees (processing % + fixed $, Shopify transaction fee %, CPA $), Targets (target margin %), Duty default (a Not set / Set a rate segmented choice; the rate box opens empty, never pre-filled with 0; plus the import fee per order), Returns (return rate %). The owner types percent and dollars; text is parsed digit by digit into bps and cents, never through a float.
  - **(f) Saving.** Save and Reset are `aria-disabled` (not `disabled`) while the form is clean, so focus stays put after a save. Errors show on blur and on a blocked save, which also focuses the first invalid field and announces "Fix N fields to save: …". A failed save keeps every typed value and shows the problem detail with its error ID; a 422's `errors[].loc` maps onto the field it names. A background refetch replaces the form only while it holds no unsaved change; closing or reloading the tab with unsaved changes asks first (`beforeunload`).
  - **(g) States.** Loading is a skeleton at the final layout; a failed first load is the panel error with Retry; a failed refresh keeps the form and shows the stale line with its as-of time; a fresh workspace lists the assumptions to check (duty not set, processing 0%) with one-click links to the fields. The save bar shows "as of" the data's fetch time; the table has no `updated_at`, so there is no "last changed" time yet.
- **Alternatives:** a new router with renamed operations and a PUT (rejected by the lead under AGENTS.md §7); an empty box meaning "not set" (ambiguous with a half-typed value); guessed defaults such as 2.9% + $0.30 or a 5% return rate (the table defaults are 0, and changing them needs a migration); a per-section Save with an impact preview (the preview needs the margin view, UX-8 part c); an in-app navigation guard (the App Router has no route-blocking API, so only `beforeunload` is covered).
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_workspace_settings.py` holds 25 boundary cases (every field below 0 and above its cap, floats, strings, booleans, null, country, currency, extra fields) and Postgres tests for: defaults on the first GET (one row after two GETs), the round trip with duty 0, null, 15,000 and 100,000 bps, an omitted duty staying put, A's PATCH never changing B under real tokens with a forged `X-Workspace-ID`, and the UX-10 verify: raising the processing rate from 290 to 590 bps moves the next `POST /v1/profit/calc` margin from 1503 to 1413 cents (payment fee 117 → 207), then duty null withholds the margin and duty 0 completes it. Vitest checks the conversions (every bps 0..10000 round-trips exactly) and the validation; 12 Playwright specs cover load, edit and save in two clicks, Reset, validation, a failed save, a 422 on its field, not set vs 0%, keyboard order with visible rings and Ctrl+S, loading, error, stale, 375 px and the shell link.
- **Not here:** Discover's best margin (UX-8 part c / UX-6), so the Discover half of UX-10's verify moves there; the impact preview, optimistic concurrency (`If-Match`) and the conflict state; guessed defaults and labelled-guess chips; shipping charged to the customer, a DDP default, max ship days, display timezone, workspace name, duty rules by category and product, and the Phase 4–7 sections (testing rules, AI budget, notifications), which need columns or tables this task doesn't add.

### DEC-261 · The Shopify crawler stays off: the owner won't risk their Shopify stores
**2026-10-25 · approved by owner 2026-10-25** · supersedes DEC-003's risk acceptance; declines DEC-090; DEC-010; DEC-136; SPEC §4.2
- **Owner's answer (2026-10-25, in the owner's session):** after a plain-English rundown of DEC-090, including that Shopify's Terms of Service §1 item 9 may bind the owner as a merchant and that the worst case is a warning, limit or closure of the owner's merchant accounts: "so if this puts our shopify stores at risk we do not need it we need to find a different route"
- **Decision:**
  - `shopify_stores` is never enabled. DEC-090 is declined, and DEC-003's risk acceptance is withdrawn. The collector's `enable shopify_stores` gate already refuses, since DEC-090 is not owner-approved.
  - Nothing in Pulso fetches from Shopify storefronts or Shopify-run hosts: no crawler run, no URL-paste fetch, and no Shopify host in `IMAGE_HOSTS`. Manual CSV import of data the owner obtained by hand stays available.
  - Views that rely on store data (Sellers tab, Store Spy, store-count saturation) show "no source yet" until another source fills them (DEC-040).
  - The owner picks a replacement route. Options in the owner's session: CJ listing counts (DEC-136, coming online), Google Merchant Center Popular products (the owner's own account, if eligible), Google Trends, and paid or vendor data only with the owner's approval (DEC-010, SPEC §0).
- **Alternatives:** approve DEC-090 (declined: risk to the owner's stores); run the crawler from another network (it doesn't change who the terms bind, and SPEC §4.2 forbids switching networks to dodge a block).
- **Why:** the owner's own Shopify stores matter more than a competitor-store signal.

### DEC-258 · Landed-cost freight: the variant's newest capture day across origins, then each origin's newest capture on that day
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-22)** · P2-18 cross-review finding XR-23 (medium); refines DEC-250 (a) and leaves its (b)–(f), and DEC-246's rounding, FX and completeness rules, unchanged; vault Features/Product Page - Profit; `docs/reports/p2-22.md`; no migration, dependency, API shape or `missing` code change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Newest day first, then the newest capture per origin.** `freight_quotes` first finds the variant's newest `observed_for`, across every origin and source: `max(observed_for) OVER (PARTITION BY coalesce(variant_id, :single_variant))`. Within that day it keeps each origin's newest capture: `rank() OVER (PARTITION BY coalesce(variant_id, :single_variant), origin_country ORDER BY observed_for DESC, captured_at DESC, raw_payload_id DESC) = 1`, and the row's `observed_for` equals that day. An origin with nothing on that day is not priced. Everything else is DEC-250's: the partition key, the filter (this listing, US, this quantity), the method filter after ranking, the later raw payload on a tie, and the newer parse (`id DESC`) on a re-parse.
  - **(b) Why a day.** CJ's tracked plan asks both origins of a variant together (`cj/tracked.py` `plan()`, CN and US), so the variant's newest day is its latest answer. An origin missing from that day either answered `data: []` (stored raw, no row written) or failed. Both read as "no quote from that origin", which fails closed (R-16): without a method it can't win cheapest, and asked by method it reads `freight_missing`. This is the case DEC-250 (a) missed: a method the newest capture no longer lists was never priced, unless the newest capture listed nothing at all.
  - **(c) CSV quotes.**
    - A CSV quote with no `warehouse_country` is its own origin, and it ages out with its day like any other.
    - A CSV backfill (an `observed_for` before CJ's newest day, imported after it) no longer outranks the newer CJ capture. The order is `observed_for` first, as `effective_observation` already orders observations. This changes DEC-250 (a)'s `captured_at`-first order, which P2-18 noted.
    - A CSV quote dated after CJ's newest day stands alone for that variant until CJ's next capture. To keep both origins priced, put every origin in the CSV.
  - **(d) Gap codes unchanged.** There is no new `missing` value. A method or origin that only older days list reads `freight_missing`. One that another variant's newest day lists reads `freight_variant_mismatch`, as in DEC-250 (a).
  - **(e) Ceiling.** A run that crosses 00:00 UTC splits one answer across two days, and the earlier day's origins aren't priced until the next run. That fails closed. The scheduled `cj.freight` slot runs at 04:30 UTC (`schedules.py`).
- **Alternatives:**
  - Rank over the freight `raw_payloads` per variant and origin (the review's fix 2): the loader would have to parse each source's request, and CSV quotes with no origin would need a rule of their own.
  - A no-quote marker row written by the CJ adapter: a writer change and a new kind of observation row, for the same result.
  - Group by collector run instead of by day: a same-day retry of one origin would hide the other origin's answer from earlier that day, and a CSV import isn't one run per variant.
- **Why:**
