  - P2-18's probe: day 1 has CN `CJPacket` $5.00 and US `USPS` $3.00; on day 30, CN answers `CJPacket` $5.00 and US answers `data: []`. It used to answer USPS $3.00, complete, margin 1812. It now answers `CJPacket` 500, margin 1612, complete, and `method: "USPS"` reads `freight_missing`.
  - The CSV probe: a 29-day-old $2.00 quote with no origin, beside CJ's day-30 CN capture. It now answers CJ's newest capture (`ePacket` 450).
  - `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py`:
    - P2-18's strict-xfail test, now unmarked;
    - a newest capture that lists two methods and is re-parsed under a second parser version;
    - an older-day test: the CSV probe, a day-1 CSV backfill imported after day 30, and a second variant whose newest day is older but is still priced.
  - p2-14's test encoded the old rule: a 29-day-old US capture was priced beside a day-30 capture. Its own-origin US capture now sits on the same day.
  - 52 one-line mutations each fail a test: p2-14's 40 and P2-18's 8 (now including the `row_number()` and re-parse mutations that used to survive), plus 4 for this rule: the newest-day filter removed, the newest day taken across the listing, the newest day taken per origin, and `captured_at` ordered before `observed_for` (`docs/reports/p2-22.md`).

### DEC-259 · Provenance contract scope (Q-1): product-facing prefixes, named exemptions for identifiers, a mutation proof, `ProfitCalc.as_of`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-23)** · Phase 2 task Q-1; DEC-040, DEC-238, DEC-239, DEC-246, DEC-252; SPEC §1, §10; vault Features/Methodology & Confidence; `docs/reports/p2-23.md`; no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Scope.** `apps/api/tests/test_provenance_contract.py` walks every 2xx response under four product-facing prefixes: `/v1/products` (Discover, header, overview, sellers, series, notes, tags), `/v1/search` (the palette, DEC-252), `/v1/profit` (`calc_profit`) and `/v1/categories` (the filter list of categories in use). A new endpoint under a prefix is covered on arrival. `COVERED_OPERATIONS` names the operations at merge, and the test fails if a prefix stops reaching one of them. Outside the scope, because they return no product data: `/v1/me`, `/v1/settings` (workspace inputs, DEC-251), `/v1/admin/*` (Data Health's run and source records), `/healthz` and `/readyz`. A later product-facing prefix (a store or supplier page) is added to `COVERED` in its own PR.
  - **(b) The check.** Unchanged from DEC-238 (b): an object schema that holds a number must list `as_of` in `required` (nullable is allowed, as on Discover rows, DEC-239). It now also counts a number inside a list, a map or a union (`items`, `additionalProperties`, `anyOf`/`oneOf`/`allOf`), so a `list[int]` field can't slip past. A `$ref` object is still checked on its own.
  - **(c) Exemptions.** `EXEMPT` maps `Schema.field` to a one-line reason. Only numbers that aren't observations qualify: row identifiers, never a count, price or rate. At merge it holds three: `CategoryOption.id` (Google's taxonomy id), and `StoreHit.id` and `SupplierHit.id` (the `stores` / `suppliers` row a palette hit opens; a hit's only number). The test fails on an exemption the check no longer flags, so the list can't go stale.
  - **(d) Proof that it bites.** `test_dropping_as_of_from_a_covered_resource_fails_the_contract` finds every covered schema that holds a number and requires `as_of` (`Sourced`, `Velocity`, `SupplierCost`, `SellerRow`, `Spread`, `SpreadPoint`, `Counts`, `SeriesDay`, `Point`, `ProfitCalc`, `InputSource` at merge). For each one, it removes `as_of` in a deep copy of the spec and expects a new finding. This replaces DEC-238 (b)'s `/v1/settings` and `/v1/me` self-check.
  - **(e) `ProfitCalc.as_of`.** The one resource the wider scope flagged: 14 numbers (lines, rates and the echoed ids) with provenance only per input in `sources`. It gains a required `as_of`: the oldest `captured_at` among `sources`, so the result is as fresh as its oldest observed input (Discover's "oldest family feeding it", DEC-239 a). It is null when only settings and owner estimates feed it (they have no capture, DEC-246). Nothing is made up, and no line or source shape changes.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) An exact operation-id list with no prefixes: each new product endpoint would stay unchecked until someone added it. An exact `==` on the named operations: every new product endpoint would fail an unrelated test, a second operation pin beside `test_api.py`'s count.
  - (c) Skip every field named `id`: no reason is written down, and a number that only looks like an id passes unseen. `as_of` on `StoreHit` from `last_crawled_at`: the hit's only number is its id, which has no capture time.
  - (e) Exempt all 14 ProfitCalc numbers because `sources` carries per-input `as_of`: 14 exemptions for numbers that are observations, against (c)'s rule. The newest input's capture: it would hide a stale freight quote behind a fresh store price.
- **Why:** On origin/main `26335b6` (p2-16 and p2-15 merged), the contract test covers 12 operations: `list_products`, `get_product`, `get_product_overview`, `get_product_sellers`, `get_product_series`, `list_product_notes`, `create_product_note`, `list_product_tags`, `put_product_tags`, `search`, `calc_profit` and `list_categories`. It passes. Adding an exemption the check doesn't need fails it. The mutation test fails on each of the 11 carriers when its `as_of` is dropped. Before the fix, the contract test failed on the 14 `ProfitCalc` fields. Profit API tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` show `as_of` taking the oldest input (the snapshots at 10-23 23:30Z, not the FX rate stored 10-24), moving to a 10-20 FX rate once one bridges the freight, and staying null at quantity 2 with no observed input. Swapping `min` for `max` fails them.

### DEC-256 · Profit tab: ad cost and net profit in the one function, a calc answer that carries its rates, freight days and named sources, server-only numbers, R-16 reasons in words
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-20)** · Phase 2 task UX-8 part b; DEC-040, DEC-238, DEC-246, DEC-250; SPEC §6.4 Profit; vault Features/Product Page - Profit; no migration, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Ad cost per order and net profit live in `landed_cost()`.** `Inputs.ad_cost_minor` (default 0) gives `LandedCost.ad_cost_minor`, `net_minor = margin_minor − ad_cost_minor` and `net_bps = net / price`, half-even like `margin_bps`. Both are null unless the row is complete, and a net past BIGINT reads `amount_out_of_range` (DEC-250 c). `margin_minor` stays the break-even CPA, and `best()` still ranks on it, so an ad cost never changes the supplier pick. The request takes an optional `ad_cost_minor` (0..2^63−1, strict, an owner estimate). Omitted, the loader applies `settings.default_cpa_minor` through its `SETTINGS` map, with source `settings`.
  - **(b) The calc answer carries what the tab needs to explain it.** Additive response fields, with operation id `calc_profit` unchanged:
    - the rates the lines use: `duty_bps` (null when not set, or unused under DDP, as DEC-250 e), `payment_fee_bps`, `payment_fee_fixed_minor`, `shopify_fee_bps` and `returns_bps`, read from the inputs the loader resolved (`Calc.inputs`), never re-resolved in the router;
    - the chosen freight quote's `freight_min_days` and `freight_max_days` (`Calc.days`, from the same row the loader priced);
    - on each `sources` entry, `source_name` (`sources.name`; "Workspace settings" and "Your estimate" for owner input) and `stale`, by DEC-238's rule (as-of older than twice the source's `expected_interval`; owner input is never stale).
  - **(c) Defaults come from the calc answer.** The tab takes its defaults from the calc answer for the unchanged scenario: what the endpoint applies from Settings (DEC-251's duty, returns and import fee included). It doesn't read `GET /v1/settings` itself; that would copy the loader's override-then-setting rule into the browser. A Settings change shows on the next calc with no tab change. Target margin (target CPA) is not shown yet.
  - **(d) Every number is the server's.** The web app formats only: money at Intl's currency exponent (JPY 0, USD 2, KWD 3), percents from basis points, and integer minor units parsed from a typed price without floats (`profit/model.ts`, Vitest). The one division in the browser is the breakdown's "% of price" display column for non-profit lines. The profit lines use the server's `margin_bps` and `net_bps`. The Playwright fixtures are the vectors that `test_the_profit_tab_gets_net_profit_rates_freight_days_and_named_sources` pins to the endpoint.
  - **(e) Scenario inputs.** Only changed inputs are sent; an unchanged one is the endpoint's default.
    - Sell price: a text input (any value) plus a slider from about half to double the reference price, in 50-cent steps that keep its cents (…49, …99).
    - Units per order: 1–4. Above 1 the market price is not carried over (DEC-246 f), so the owner enters the order's price.
    - Returns 0–25% in 0.5% steps; payment fee 0–10% in 0.1% steps (its fixed part stays the Settings value); ad cost $0–$60 in $0.50 steps. A default above a range's top widens it.
    - Radix sliders: arrows one step, Shift + arrow or Page Up/Down ten, `aria-valuetext` with the unit.
    - Requests are debounced 120 ms; each scenario is cached. While one recomputes, the results dim to 70% under a 2 px hairline (opacity only). A failed recompute keeps the last result with a stale line and Retry. Reset to defaults clears every change.
  - **(f) States.** Incomplete (R-16): a banner lists each `missing` code in plain words; the three results read "Withheld" with what they need; lines show a short gap ("Not quoted", "Not set") in amber with an icon; totals read "Withheld". No supplier listing: no source yet, with the CSV import fallback. No market price: the price input asks for one. Stale inputs: a note naming each source and its last capture, plus the line's source tag. Loading: a skeleton of the layout. First-load error: the panel error with Retry.
- **Alternatives:** net profit computed in the router or the browser (a second copy of the formula, against DEC-246's one function); slider defaults from `GET /v1/settings` (no returns or duty there yet, and the tab would re-resolve override-then-setting, a copy of the loader); a separate `GET /products/{id}/profit` (the calc answer for the unchanged scenario is the same thing); a client-side map from source key to name (drifts from `sources.name`); looking freight days up in the router by raw payload (could read another origin's row).
- **Why:** `packages/core/tests/test_landed_cost.py` adds the worked example's net ($0.03, 10 bps at a $15.00 CPA), a loss, no net on an incomplete row, and a net past BIGINT. `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py` adds the worked example end to end on Postgres (rates, ad cost from Settings, freight days, source names, stale), one price step and an ad-cost estimate, no net without freight, a second supplier listing priced at its own method, and the DDP duty echo. 11 one-line mutations of these rules each fail a test (docs/reports/p2-20.md). Playwright covers the tab against those fixtures: equal landed cost and margin, a slider move, missing freight and duty, keyboard-only use of every slider, and every state.
- **Not here:** DDP toggle, duty slider and "Save as product duty"; shipping method and supplier pickers; waterfall, price × CPA heatmap and sensitivity bars; break-even ROAS and target CPA; the scenario in the URL; the P/A/R/D/0 shortcuts.

### DEC-260 · Review cleanup (imp-224): Discover joins pages by id, the matcher's top k counts listings, below-band pending pairs are rejected, same-product pending pairs are accepted, a bad price or NaN cosine is missing
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-224)** · Improvement Backlog imp-224 (P3); cross-review lows XR-18, XR-19 (`docs/reports/p2-12.md`) and XR-24 to XR-26, XR-28 (`docs/reports/p2-18.md`); XR-17 dropped under DEC-261; DEC-239 (b), DEC-249 (b, e, f, h), DEC-250 (c), DEC-255, DEC-257; `docs/reports/imp-224.md`; no migration, no dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Discover joins its pages by product id, first place wins (XR-19).** `joinPages` keeps the first occurrence, so each card and row has a unique key. Adds to DEC-239 (b): keyset pages over live keys (momentum, store count) can repeat a product whose key moved between page loads, now shown once, and can skip one whose key moved past the cursor until the next refetch. That skip is accepted.
  - **(b) The top k is k listings, not k image rows (XR-24).** The neighbour query keeps its HNSW `ORDER BY distance LIMIT n`. While its rows hold fewer than k distinct listings and the scan isn't exhausted, the matcher asks again with 4× the rows, then keeps the k listings whose nearest image comes first. The ceiling is `hnsw.max_scan_tuples`.
  - **(c) A pending pair re-scored below the queue band becomes `rejected` (XR-25 a).** The re-scored components and score are written and `decided_at` is set. No decision row is written because nothing moves (R-15), so UX-1 can tell it from an owner reject: that one always has `reject` rows (DEC-257 a). `rejected` is the only existing status that means "not one product". Like any decided row, it is never re-scored again. The GTIN pass skips it, and DEC-249 (h)'s third-listing veto applies. That fails closed: a link through a third listing queues instead.
  - **(d) A pending pair whose listings are already on one canonical product becomes `accepted` (XR-25 b).** After both passes, every run settles them in one locked transaction (DEC-257 f): `status = 'accepted'`, `decided_at` set, no pointer moved and no decision row. Same-product listings are never neighbours, so without this sweep such a pair would stay queued forever.
  - **(e) One bad row no longer stops the run (XR-26).** A price that is past BIGINT in USD is no price (`price_sane` NULL, as `cheapest()` does since DEC-250 c). A non-finite component (pgvector's NaN cosine for a zero-norm vector) is missing and renormalises (DEC-040). A pair with no component left is skipped unwritten.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) De-duplicate on the server: a page can't see earlier pages without a session.
  - (b) `DISTINCT ON (listing_id)` over a fixed over-fetch: still starves past the factor. One embedding per listing: changes every pair's `image_cos`.
  - (c) Keep `pending` and have UX-1's query filter `score >= queue` (the review's option): the brief asked for a status. `accepted` or `merged`: they say the pair is one product. Deleting the row (DEC-249 b's "not written"): not a status, and it would re-queue on the next run if the score rose again.
  - (d) Settle only when `link()` moves a listing: misses pairs put together by an owner accept, a split `into` or a GTIN link.
  - (e) Roll back and log per listing (the review's optional part): a broad catch for faults nobody has seen; the two known ones are now guarded.
- **Why:** each finding has a test named for it (`docs/reports/imp-224.md`). On origin/main, the XR-18, 19, 24, 25 a, 25 b and 26 tests fail and XR-28's four pass, because the code they guard is right. Each XR-28 test fails under its mutation from the review. Fixture or Postgres (`pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`) tests:
  - `price_min=2^63`, `price_max=10^30`, `category=2^31` and a `fewest_sellers` cursor of 2^63 each answer 422, and the bounds themselves answer 200;
  - a joined page that repeats an id renders it once, at its first place;
  - two listings crowded out by 21-image neighbours still link;
  - a 0.8300 pending pair re-scored to 0.5750 reads `rejected`, and a pending pair joined through a third listing reads `accepted` with no decision of its own, after which a re-run writes nothing;
  - a GBP listing at 2^63−1 and a zero-norm title vector score and queue while a later pair links;
  - XR-28: a ¥1,500 retail against a $10 cost queues; an owner unlink after an algo link holds; a rejected partner on a product merged into the target vetoes; a stored row re-scores to its stored score.
- **Not here:** XR-17 (Shopify Crawl-delay skip): won't fix, because DEC-261 keeps the crawler off. XR-27, the matcher's FX lookup (imp-230). A re-queue path for (c) rows after a calibration changes the scorer is left to UX-1 or imp-229.

### DEC-266 · membership(t) and `manual()` replay `match_decisions` in lock order (by id); the cutoff stays on `decided_at`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-28)** · refines DEC-257 (e); cross-review XR-29 (`docs/reports/p2-24.md`, vault Agents/Reviews/2026-10-25 Claude - p2-16 p2-19 p2-21 p2-22 cross-review); DEC-031, DEC-257 (f); R-15; vault Data/Product Matching; `docs/reports/p2-28.md`; no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Order.** A listing's latest decision is its highest `id`: `membership(t)` orders `listing_id, id DESC` under `DISTINCT ON (listing_id)`, and the matcher's `manual()` orders `id DESC`. `decided_at` is `now()`, the transaction's start, and an owner request opens its transaction (the auth query) before it takes the matching lock, so a matcher transaction that started later but locked first got the later stamp, and replay returned the product the listing was merged away from (XR-29).
  - **(b) Why id is the lock order.** Every writer inserts decisions only after it takes the matching lock (DEC-257 f: the four service calls, and both matcher passes before `link()`), and the lock is held until commit. `id` is an identity column with Postgres's default `CACHE 1`, so `nextval` is handed out in call order across sessions: a transaction that takes the lock later inserts after the earlier one committed, with higher ids. Within one transaction every `decided_at` was already equal, so the order there is unchanged. Rows already written read correctly with no rewrite: history stays append-only.
  - **(c) Cutoff.** `decided_at < t` stays the cutoff (DEC-031: a feature for day d uses `t = d+1 00:00Z`). The change alters which row wins only among rows already before the cutoff, and only where their stamps disagree with their ids, which is the XR-29 race. A cutoff that falls inside two overlapping requests sees the same set of rows as before; that window is one request long.
- **Alternatives:**
  - A `clock_timestamp()` stamp taken after the lock: a migration for the column default, and rows already written would still read in the wrong order.
  - Taking the lock before the auth query in UX-1's endpoints: every future writer would have to remember it, and it fixes nothing already written.
  - A `(listing_id, id)` index to match the new order: needs a migration; replay is one pass per call at v1 scale (DEC-257 e's ponytail), and `manual()` reads one listing's few rows through the `listing_id` prefix of the existing index.
- **Why:** Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` (`packages/ml/tests/test_matcher.py`): P2-24's strict-xfail `test_xr29_replay_follows_the_order_moves_took_the_lock` passes with its mark removed, and the new `test_xr29_manual_reads_the_move_that_took_the_lock_last` holds an owner unlink that locked after an algo link but carries the earlier stamp. Both failed on the `decided_at` order first. The membership chain and cutoff test and the XR-28 manual test still pass. Seven one-line mutations each fail a test, among them each query put back to `decided_at` order (`docs/reports/p2-28.md`).
- **Not here:** XR-30 (stale redirects mid-run) and the other P2-24 lows. Any `ALTER SEQUENCE … CACHE n` on `match_decisions_id_seq` would break (b); none exists.

### DEC-263 · Matching Queue (UX-1): `/v1/admin/matching` endpoints over the M-5 service, undo as compensating decisions, owner unlink, notes and tags read through `merged_into_id`, one key per decision
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-25)** · Phase 2 task UX-1, imp-249, imp-250; R-15; DEC-028, DEC-040, DEC-249, DEC-255, DEC-257, DEC-261, DEC-266; AGENTS.md §3, §8; vault Features/Matching Queue, Data/Product Matching; `docs/reports/p2-25.md`; no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Endpoints.** A `/v1/admin/matching` router, signed in (`current_principal`), with stable operation ids:
    - `list_match_candidates` (GET `/candidates`): pending pairs whose sides aren't already on one canonical product. Sorts: `impact` (pairs touching a canonical product this workspace has notes or tags on, then score), `score`, and `age` (oldest first). Filters: `source_pair` (`cj+shopify_stores`, sorted), `min_score`/`max_score`, and `version` (`scorer_version`). Keyset `cursor`, limit 1..200. Each page also carries the queue summary (pending count, sources with a stored listing, listings the matcher linked and when it last did, `MATCHER_ENABLED`), the facets, and the scorer thresholds.
    - `get_match_candidate` (GET `/candidates/{id}`): both listings (up to 4 images, price as of its capture, variants, first seen), the components against the thresholds, `scorer_version`, `price_sane`, each side's product cluster (up to 48 members), and `superseded`, set when the pair is decided or its sides already share a product. Note and tag counts are this workspace's only.
    - `create_match_decision` (POST `/decisions`, discriminated on `action`): `accept` and `reject` take a candidate, `merge` takes two products, and `split` and `unlink` take listings (1..500). Each calls the M-5 service with the user id as actor, then commits. A missing row is a 404 `not_found`, and a service refusal is a 409 `decision_conflict` with the service's reason.
    - `undo_match_decision` (POST `/decisions/{id}/undo`).
    - Skip is client-only and writes nothing. Market rows are global, so only the counts are workspace-scoped.
  - **(b) Undo (imp-250) is new rows, never an edit.**
    - A decision is every row one call wrote: one actor at one `decided_at`, which is the transaction time.
    - It can be undone only while each of its listings' latest decision is still that decision's own row; otherwise a 409 reports it as superseded. "Latest" is in id order, which is the order the rows took the matching lock: `decided_at` is the transaction's start and can precede the lock (XR-29). This is the same order DEC-266 gives `membership(t)` and `manual()`.
    - Each row gets a compensating row from its `to` back to its `from`: accept → `unlink`, unlink → `link`, reject → `reject` (nothing moves), merge → `merge`, split → `merge`. The reason starts `undo #<id>`.
    - Undoing a merge makes the merged product `active` again, with `merged_into_id` NULL.
    - Candidates the decision settled go back to `pending`, or to `accepted` where the pair still ends on one product.
    - The matcher's decisions are never undone, and an undo is never undone: to redo, decide again.
    - Undo adds no cannot-link, and a reject's compensating `reject` still isn't manual (DEC-257 c), so a score + GTIN re-run sees what it saw before the decision.
  - **(c) Owner unlink** (`unlink(listings)`): listings of one product leave it with an `unlink` row each, and at least one stays, as in a split. The latest move is manual, so the matcher never links them again (DEC-249 h).
  - **(d) Notes and tags on a merge stay where they are (imp-249).** A merge moves listings only. Notes and `product_tags` keep their product id, as forecasts, scores and features do. The product page's notes list and tag reads follow `merged_into_id` through a recursive CTE (two hops and more), so the survivor shows the union. PUT tags replaces the tags across the whole merged group. The tags response is `TagList` without the 20-tag cap, because a union may hold more than 20.
  - **(e) The page** (`/admin/matching`, nav entry built):
    - **Keys.** A accept, R reject, S skip, M merge, X split, U or Ctrl/⌘+Z undo, J/K next/previous, 1–4 image.
    - **Fields and dialogs.** Keys are ignored while typing in a field or while a dialog is open.
    - **Modifiers and held keys.** Alt and Shift are never shortcuts: Shift+A batch accept isn't built. A held key repeats only J, K and S.
    - **Pacing.** A decision hides its pair and selects the next one before the request returns. A 409 skips the pair, and any other failure puts it back. A key acts only on the pair on screen. List rows never animate, and the pair fades in over 120 ms without blocking a key.
    - **Merge dialog.** The younger product goes into the older (`first_seen_at`, then id), and a swap reverses it. Before confirm it shows the listings that move, and the notes and tags that stay and read through.
    - **Split dialog.** It shows ticked against staying listings, with Split (new product) or Unlink.
    - **Phone.** Read-only, with Accept, Reject and Skip.
    - **Empty states.** No source yet appears when fewer than two sources have a stored listing. It names a second stored source, such as a CSV import, and never promises Shopify (DEC-261).
- **Alternatives:**
  - (b) Deleting or flipping the undone rows breaks the append-only log (AGENTS.md §3). A `reverts` column needs a migration and adds nothing the actor + `decided_at` group doesn't already give. Undoing any decision in the log, not just each listing's latest, would overwrite later decisions.
  - (d) Moving notes and tags on merge rewrites workspace rows and needs its own undo, and a later split couldn't tell which note belonged to which side.
  - (a) Offset pagination shifts pages as pairs are decided. Sorting impact by watchlists, boards and tests waits until those tables exist (Phase 4).
  - (e) Shift+A batch accept (backlog).
- **Why:**
  - `packages/ml/tests/test_matcher.py`:
    - undo of accept, accept-merge, reject and split each writes compensating rows and restores the pointers, statuses and merged product;
    - undoing twice, undoing an undo, undoing the matcher's decision and undoing a missing id all fail;
    - a score + GTIN re-run after each undo leaves every table unchanged;
    - unlinked listings are never re-linked.
  - `apps/api/tests/test_matching.py`:
    - sorting, filters and keyset pages;
    - detail with workspace counts, where B sees 0 of A's notes and tags;
    - each action through the API, where the decision count only grows and undo adds rows;
    - a split or rejected pair stays apart after a matcher re-run through the API path.
  - `apps/api/tests/test_product_notes.py`: notes and tags read through two merge hops and never leak across workspaces.
  - Vitest covers the key map.
  - Playwright: 200 fixture pairs decided with one key each in 19.9 s of machine time.
- **Not here:**
  - Shift+A batch accept;
  - auto-link audit sampling;
  - a stored matcher-run summary (the empty state reads the latest `algo` decision instead);
  - impact from watchlists, boards and tests;
  - cleaning up a product emptied by undoing an accept or split, which stays `active` with no listings.

### DEC-264 · Suppliers tab (UX-6): each row is the Profit calc for that supplier, picks over in-stock rows, out-of-stock rows last, the choice carried in `?supplier=`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-26)** · Phase 2 task UX-6 and its R-16 sub-line; DEC-040, DEC-238, DEC-243, DEC-246, DEC-256, DEC-259, DEC-261; vault Features/Product Page - Suppliers; `docs/reports/p2-26.md`; no migration, flag or dependency; no change to `landed_cost()` or its loader
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One calc, reused.** `GET /v1/products/{id}/suppliers?country=US` (operation `get_product_suppliers`, its own router) answers one row per live supplier listing (`delisted_at` null). Each row's `cost` is what `calc_profit()` answers for `supplier_listing_id = that listing` at the scenario defaults (quantity 1, the market price, Settings). It is the same function `POST /v1/profit/calc` runs, so a row and the Profit tab for that supplier can't disagree, and there is no second formula. Each row carries R-16's `complete` and `missing[]` inside `cost`.
  - **(b) Picks.** Both picks consider in-stock rows only, by DEC-243's rule (`available is not False` and stock null or above 0). **Best margin** is `best()` over those rows' `LandedCost`s, so it ranks complete rows only and an unquoted cheaper supplier never wins. **Fastest shipping** is the lowest `freight_max_days`, then the lower landed cost (a withheld one last), then the lower listing id. It needs a quote's days, not complete costs, so it still picks while duty or the price is missing. Both are computed over every row, not the page.
  - **(c) Order and paging.** Complete rows by margin, best first; then rows with a landed cost, cheapest first; then the rest. Known out-of-stock rows follow every in-stock row in that same order, so the first row is the Best margin pick. The keyset cursor is the row's three-integer sort key (`invalid_cursor` 422 otherwise); limit 1–200, default 100.
  - **(d) Provenance (Q-1).** The page, rows, rating, orders and bulk tiers carry `as_of`, and the operation joins `COVERED_OPERATIONS`. A snapshot term (rating in hundredths of a star with its review count, orders as `lifetime` or a `window` of N days, stock) is `Sourced` from the latest snapshot with its raw payload. An entity term (years active, MOQ, bulk tiers) uses the listing's last sighting as `as_of`, with no raw payload. A term the source doesn't give is `NoSource` with the reason, and the UI says "No source yet". A bulk tier without an integer quantity above 0 and an integer price is dropped.
  - **(e) The choice in the URL.** Opening a supplier in Profit (row click, `Enter`, or the row's or pick card's button) writes `supplier=<listing id>` with `history.replaceState` and switches to Profit, which sends it as `supplier_listing_id`. "Let Pulso pick the supplier" removes it. A reload or shared link keeps it, and Suppliers marks that row "In Profit".
  - **(f) What every row lacks is said once.** When every row misses the market price (DEC-261: the store crawler is off) or the duty rate, one note says so, with the way to fix it, and each row's margin cell says why in a word with the full reasons by hover, focus or tap. In the list, only a loss is coloured.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) A batch variant of `calculate()` for all listings at once: fewer queries, but a second code path through the money math (DEC-072 risk) for a page with a handful of rows. Row cost from `calculate()` directly: the row would miss the Profit answer's fees, rates and named sources.
  - (b) Fastest by `min_days` or the mean: a supplier with a 3–30-day spread would win on its best case. Fastest among complete rows only: duty unset would hide a pick that doesn't depend on it.
  - (c) Out-of-stock rows in margin order: the first row then isn't the Best margin pick under a header that says "best margin first". Hiding them: you lose the reason they never pick.
  - (e) Router state or a `router.push`: a history entry per choice, where the tab bar already uses `replaceState`.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_product_suppliers.py` (12 tests, on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`) compares the picks with `calculate()` + `best()` run directly, checks each row's cost equals `POST /v1/profit/calc` for that supplier, and walks the keyset. A one-line mutation check of the router (17 mutations: stock filter, available/stock rules, `best()` swapped out, Fastest by min days, tie-break, out-of-stock order, sort, cursor, orders semantics, rating scale, tier filter, picks per page, cost per supplier, delisted rows, staleness) fails a test each time. `suppliers.spec.ts` shows two clicks from the product page to a supplier's Profit numbers, with the request carrying `supplier_listing_id`.
- **Not here (backlog rows in the report):** the variant expansion, the method select, the US warehouse pick card, the toast, the freight-refresh, CJ search, manual add and CSV export toolbar, CJ error-type banners, the 90-day cost sparkline and 30-day change, and a stock column. Profit's default pick without `supplier=` (DEC-246 f) doesn't filter stock while Best margin does; the report proposes aligning them.

### DEC-267 · Google Merchant Center Popular products: the owner's weekly CSV download, one append-only row per ranked product, products linked by GTIN on read
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-29)** · Phase 2 task p2-29 (owner priority: the replacement signal after DEC-261); DEC-028, DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-191, DEC-255; SPEC §4.2; AGENTS.md §3; vault Data/Sources/Source - Google Merchant Center Popular Products; `docs/reports/p2-29.md`; migration 0009 (one table and a disabled source row); no dependency, settings flag or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Source and file.** `merchant_bestsellers` (source 7, type `csv`, disabled) imports the owner's Merchant Center download (Analytics › Products › Popular products › Download, .csv) through the shared CSV import, in Data Health like every other template. No API, OAuth, account or key.
    - The download opens with a `Name:` line and a `Filters:` line before its header. The header, read from the owner's first file on 2026-10-25 (no data row read), is `Popularity rank,Title,Brand,Availability,Price Range Min,Price Range Max,Price Range Currency,GTINs`.
    - The parser drops those two lines and turns the filters' Country, Category and Time period into every row's `country`, `category` and `report_week`. A file without them must carry `report_week` and `country` columns.
    - Only a weekly report imports. A Time period that isn't `W` plus a date fails the file with "Download the weekly report".
    - Template columns are Google's headers, normalised, so the download imports unchanged. Renamed headers go through the source's saved `csv_mapping`, as for every template. The fields Google documents for the API and BigQuery but this download lacks (`previous_rank`, `relative_demand`, `relative_demand_change`) are optional columns. An unknown column still fails closed.
  - **(b) Rows.** One `popular_products` observation per row per report week. Global market data, no `workspace_id`.
    - `observed_for` is the Monday that starts the report week: Google's `W20261012` and the Merchant API's `reportDate` both name it. A `report_week` that isn't a Monday is a row error.
    - `captured_at` and `raw_payload_id` come from the capture. The unique key is `(source_id, country, category, observed_for, rank, raw_payload_id, parser_version)`, NULLS NOT DISTINCT (DEC-028). The entity is the ranked entry of one report (country × category × week); a rank repeated in one file rejects the file.
    - A re-imported file skips before parsing (same sha256), and a re-parse by the same parser adds nothing. Rows go in 1,000 per statement, since one weekly download holds about 16,000. A trigger rejects UPDATE and DELETE.
    - Prices are `price_min_minor` / `price_max_minor` plus `currency`. A price without a currency is stored as no price, never assumed to be USD.
  - **(c) Products.** A row keeps its GTINs as Google wrote them and never stores a product. `link_products()` resolves on read:
    - M-4's `gtin14` normalises both sides, so a wrong check digit, a placeholder or an RCN never links (DEC-255 a);
    - `canonical()` follows merges to the live product;
    - a row links only when its valid GTINs reach exactly one live product. None, or two, leave it unlinked, and it still shows.
    - No `match_candidates` or `match_decisions` rows are written: a ranked row isn't a listing, and M-3, M-4 and M-5 move listings only. A product created after an import links on the next read without a re-import.
  - **(d) Weekly change.** This download has no previous rank. Where `previous_rank` is blank, the surface takes the change from the previous week's import of the same country and category, matched on title and brand. A product absent last week reads "new", never 0 (DEC-040). Decided here and built with the surface.
  - **(e) The surface is deferred.** The task passed its 400-line bound, so (a) ships alone. The Google popular list is a backlog row in the report: its own page with rank, signed weekly change with ▲/▼, the report week as its "as of", and loading, empty, error, stale and "no source yet" states. It's a page rather than a Discover column because unmatched rows must show and Discover lists products.
  - **(f) Flag and use.** The source row is the flag: disabled, phase 2. CSV import works on a disabled source, like every CSV fallback (DEC-261 keeps Shopify's). Google allows Popular products data for the retailer's internal use only: it "can't be resold, publicly displayed, advertised, or aggregated across businesses". No public page (a Track Record page, a shared link) may ever show these rows.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) The Merchant API `accounts.reports.search`: OAuth and account work the owner ruled out. A `report_week` column the owner types in: the Filters line already names the week. Monthly reports dated on their first day: two granularities in one table.
  - (b) Title and brand as the key: two products can share both, so a real file would lose a row or be rejected. Row-at-a-time `append_observation`: about 32,000 round trips for one weekly file, inside the import request.
  - (c) A `product_id` stored at import: frozen at that moment, so a product created later never links and a merge leaves a stale pointer. Writing `match_decisions`: they record listing moves, and no listing moves.
- **Why:** tests on synthetic fixture CSVs (`packages/sources/tests/imports/test_merchant.py`, Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`):
  - Google's layout parses with its filters as columns, and renamed headers parse through the mapping;
  - a monthly report fails, a Tuesday `report_week` is a row error, and a repeated rank rejects the file;
  - importing the same file twice leaves 3 rows, and a re-parse inserts 0;
  - a GTIN-14 written with a dash links to its product, a merged product's GTIN links to the live one, a wrong check digit never links, and the same GTIN on two live products links neither.

### DEC-265 · Workspace product economics: `landed_cost()` in SQL held to the Python by golden vectors, a default-scenario view per workspace, and Discover's best margin from its complete rows
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-27)** · Phase 2 task UX-8 part c; R-16; DEC-031, DEC-238, DEC-239, DEC-246, DEC-250, DEC-251, DEC-256, DEC-258, DEC-259, DEC-261; SPEC §6.4 Profit; vault Features/Product Page - Profit, Features/Discover; `docs/reports/p2-27.md`; migration 0010; no flag, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One SQL twin of `landed_cost()`.** Migration 0010 adds four Postgres functions: `half_even()`, `usd_line()`, `usd_rate()` and `landed_cost()`. `landed_cost()` follows `pulso_core.landed_cost.landed_cost` line by line:
    - exact numeric arithmetic, rounded half-even once per line (DEC-246 a), at the currency's exponent from `currencies`;
    - every line and total checked against BIGINT (DEC-250 c), and `missing` in `MISSING` order;
    - margin, `margin_bps`, net and `net_bps` null unless the row is complete (R-16).

    An unknown missing code, an unknown currency or a price ≤ 0 raises, as the Python does. The Python stays the reference. The golden vectors in (b) hold the SQL to it.
  - **(b) Golden vectors.** `packages/core/tests/fixtures/landed_cost_vectors.json` holds vectors generated from the Python function and spot-checked by hand. They cover:
    - the worked example and a loss;
    - half-even ties both ways;
    - JPY, KWD and CNY rates;
    - DDP and quantity above 1;
    - duty not set, each gap alone and several together;
    - an FX rate of 0;
    - BIGINT overflow on each checked line, including p2-12's JPY 2^62 at q = 2.

    Every vector runs through both the Python and the SQL function. The ones marked `view` (quantity 1, no DDP, no owner estimate) also run through the view and through `calculate()` on the same seeded rows. A rule change goes into the Python first: the vectors are regenerated, and the SQL must then match them.
  - **(c) The view is `calculate()` with no overrides.** `workspace_product_economics` has one row per workspace (its `settings` row) and non-merged product. Its inputs:
    - quantity 1, no DDP;
    - Settings' duty, fees, returns and CPA, with source `settings` (duty is listed only when set);
    - the lower median of live USD store prices as the reference price;
    - each live supplier listing's latest snapshot as its unit cost (a latest snapshot without a price is no unit cost, never an older one);
    - FX through `usd_rate()`: the rate's own date with a 4-day look-back, and the blend before a manual rate;
    - freight: the cheapest exact US quote at quantity 1 on the variant's newest capture day (DEC-258). It is US only: `settings.default_country`, which `v1_market` pins.

    The supplier is chosen as `best()` does: the highest complete margin; with none, the fewest missing inputs; then the lowest id. Owner estimates belong to a request, so the view has none. `as_of` is the oldest observed input (DEC-259 e). It is a plain view, so it is live and needs no refresh. A dated reader would put DEC-031's cutoff inside the ranked subquery.
  - **(d) Discover's best margin.** `GET /v1/products` reads the view for the caller's workspace and the page's product ids. `best_margin_minor` becomes `Margin | Incomplete`:
    - `Margin` (a `Sourced`) is `margin_minor` plus `margin_bps` and `supplier_listing_id`. Its source, `as_of` and raw payload are the oldest observed input's, and it is stale when any input is (DEC-238). This is the break-even CPA, the Profit tab's margin (DEC-256 a), so an ad cost never moves it.
    - `Incomplete` (a `NoSource`) carries the `missing` codes and a reason in the Profit tab's words, for example "Needs a freight quote." A workspace without a settings row gets "No workspace settings yet." and no codes.
    - The web cell shows `$4.56 · 38.0%`. It shows amber "Duty not set" when duty is among the gaps, because that is the gap Settings closes, and "No source yet" for any other gap. Its provenance card names every gap.
  - **(e) No margin sort or filter yet.** A margin sort or `margin_min_bp` filter would compute the view for every product in the filtered set on each request, and no index can cover a view built from functions. They need a materialized per-workspace table, refreshed on capture and on a Settings change (BACKLOG).
  - **(f) DEC-261.** The Shopify crawler stays off, so the store price comes only from listings already in the database (CSV imports). Without one, the margin reads "Needs a market price.", shown as "No source yet".
- **Alternatives:**
  - A job that writes a per-workspace table: a second scheduler path, stale between runs; the view answers the next request, which the Settings acceptance asks for.
  - `calculate()` per product in the router: several queries for each of 48 products per page, and it could never be sorted in SQL.
  - A materialized view now: it needs refresh triggers on every capture table and on settings, and no sort asks for it yet (e).
  - Owner estimates in the view: the Profit tab's estimates belong to one request, and no per-product estimate is stored yet.
- **Why:** On `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`, every vector gives the same `complete`, `missing` and minor-unit totals from the Python function, the SQL function, and (for `view` vectors) the view and `calculate()`. Every default-scenario `calc()` in `test_profit.py` also asserts that the view row equals the endpoint's answer. Other tests on the same image:
  - an unquoted cheap supplier can't set the margin;
  - a Settings PATCH moves it on the next request;
  - two workspaces each read their own settings;
  - a merged product has no row.

  The one-line mutations of the SQL and the router are listed in `docs/reports/p2-27.md` with the test that catches each.
- **Not here:**
  - margin sort and filter (e);
  - the overview's `landed_margin_minor` from the view;
  - ship-days, US-warehouse and lifecycle filters;
  - per-product duty.

### DEC-269 · OpenCLIP embeddings (M-2 part b): ViT-B-32 `laion2b_s34b_b79k` pinned to one Hugging Face commit, `ml.embed` behind `EMBEDDINGS_ENABLED=off`, idempotent through the immutable stored copy and `title_norm`, torch only in an optional `clip` extra
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-31)** · Phase 2 task M-2 part b; DEC-040, DEC-237 (a), DEC-240 (g, h), DEC-249, DEC-260 (e); AGENTS.md §3, §7; vault Data/Product Matching; `docs/reports/p2-31.md`; no migration; new free optional dependencies
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Checkpoint.** OpenCLIP `ViT-B-32` with the `laion2b_s34b_b79k` weights, as DEC-237 (a) sized the columns for. The weights are pinned to Hugging Face `laion/CLIP-ViT-B-32-laion2B-s34B-b79K` at commit `1a25a446712ba5ee05982a381eed697ef9b435cf`, file `open_clip_model.safetensors` (605,143,316 bytes, sha256 `ac4f8c4b88af6d963118cbf40ad93176d092abbedfcb752601ae1866352656e6`, MIT licence). Both encoders give 512 dimensions, so the job writes the existing `images.embedding` and `listings.title_embedding` (`vector(512)`, HNSW cosine) with no migration. `pulso_ml.embeddings.MODEL_VERSION` (`ViT-B-32/laion2b_s34b_b79k@1a25a44`) is in every run's log line.
    - **Why this checkpoint:** the columns already fit it. It is the smallest standard OpenCLIP ViT, which matters on a concurrency-1 CPU queue on the owner's PC. Its LAION-2B training pairs are web images with their alt text, close to shop photos with product titles. Images and titles share one space (M-3 compares each within its own kind). The file is safetensors (no pickle) under MIT.
  - **(b) Job.** `ml.embed` (`pulso_workers.ml.embed_listings`) on the `ml` queue: concurrency 1, `--pool=solo` locally. Its flag is `EMBEDDINGS_ENABLED` (Settings `embeddings_enabled`, default off). Off, the task logs `embeddings_disabled` and never opens the database or loads the model.
    - **Lazy imports.** torch, open_clip and huggingface_hub are imported only inside `pulso_ml.embeddings.openclip()`, the only code that loads the model.
    - **Passed in.** `embed(session, encoder, read)` takes the encoder and the capture reader. pulso-sources depends on pulso-ml, so the worker passes `pulso_sources.storage.read_capture`, as it passes `rate_as_of` to the matcher.
    - **Images.** Every `images` row with a `storage_key` and no `embedding`. The raw payload is found through the indexed `sha256`, which the key names, and read back by `read_capture`, which checks the hash (DEC-240 g).
    - **Titles.** Every listing whose `title_embedding` is NULL or whose `title_norm` differs from `title`. The job writes the vector and `title_norm = title` together.
    - **Batches and log.** Batches of 32, keyset by id, one commit each. The run ends with `embeddings_done model=… images=… titles=… image_missing=… image_unreadable=…`.
  - **(c) Idempotent with no key column.** The key is the stored content plus the model version, and existing state already holds each part:
    - **Image content.** An image row's stored copy never changes once set (DEC-240 h: a stored image is never planned again, and a replaced image is a new row). So a NULL embedding means "no vector for this content".
    - **Title content.** `title_norm` holds the exact text its vector came from. A re-titled listing (CJ rewrites `title` in place) is embedded again, and an unchanged one isn't.
    - **Model version.** The schema fixes it (DEC-237 a): another checkpoint means a migration that clears both columns, then a re-embed.

    A re-run encodes nothing and updates no row.
  - **(d) Missing and unreadable images.**
    - **Missing.** A listing without a stored image gets its title vector only. Its image vector stays NULL, never a zero vector: pgvector's cosine with a zero vector is NaN (DEC-260 e). It counts in `image_missing`, the same set as the matcher's `skipped_no_embedding`: a missing input with weight 0 (DEC-040).
    - **Unreadable.** A missing object, a hash mismatch or bytes that don't decode is logged as `embedding_image_unreadable image_id=…` and counted in `image_unreadable`. The image stays NULL and is retried next run, and the rest of its batch still embeds.
  - **(e) `title_norm` until normalisation exists.** `title_norm` is the title exactly as embedded. OpenCLIP's tokenizer already lower-cases, cleans whitespace and cuts at 77 tokens. When title normalisation (vault Product Matching, step 2) lands, it writes `normalize(title)` and compares against that, which re-embeds each title once.
  - **(f) Dependencies.** The optional extra `pulso-ml[clip]` holds `open-clip-torch>=3.3,<4` (MIT), `torch>=2.9,<3` and `torchvision>=0.24,<1` (BSD-style), all free. The lock has `open-clip-torch 3.3.0`, `torch 2.14.1+cpu` and `torchvision 0.29.1+cpu`, plus their dependencies (timm, huggingface-hub, safetensors, …). `pulso-workers[ml]` is `pulso-ml[clip]`.
    - **Index.** torch and torchvision come only from the explicit `pytorch-cpu` index (`https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu`), because PyPI's Linux torch pulls the CUDA stack.
    - **CI and worktrees.** `uv sync --locked --all-packages` installs no extra, so CI and every worktree stay torch-free; `--locked` still checks the lock. mypy ignores the three missing modules.
    - **Tests.** They inject a weight-free fake encoder. No test, CI job or worktree downloads weights.
  - **(g) Worker image (not changed here).** The ml consumer's image installs with `uv sync --locked --no-dev --no-editable --package pulso-workers --extra ml`.
    - **Weights.** It bakes the pinned file at build with `hf_hub_download(WEIGHTS_REPO, WEIGHTS_FILE, revision=WEIGHTS_REVISION)` and sets `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` at run time, so the job never reaches the network.
    - **Scope.** Only an `ml` image or service gets torch; the `collect,default` image stays as it is.
    - **Schedule.** That is an infra and collector-host change for a later task. Until it lands, `ml.embed` has no Beat entry, like `ml.match`.
  - **(h) Retention.** DEC-240 (g)'s future retention job must clear `images.embedding` along with `storage_key` and `phash`, or exempt image captures.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (c) `embedding_key` and `title_embedding_key` columns (sha256 plus model version per row): a migration for state the stored copy and `title_norm` already hold, and DEC-237 (a) ruled out a per-row model column. Re-encoding every title each night and writing only the changed vectors: a full text-encoder pass over every listing, every night.
  - (d) A zero vector for a missing image: NaN cosines in the matcher.
  - (f) torch as a plain dependency: about 1 GB in every CI job and worktree, and the CUDA stack on Linux from PyPI. A dependency group: groups are for development tools (PEP 735), and this is an optional runtime dependency.
  - (a) ViT-B-16 or ViT-L-14: better zero-shot scores at several times the CPU per image, and ViT-L-14 is 768-d (a migration). A separate text model: DEC-237's alternative, to revisit on Phase 2 title precision.
  - (b) Reading S3 inside pulso-ml: a second copy of `read_capture`'s hash check.
- **Why:**
  - **Postgres tests.** `packages/ml/tests/test_embeddings.py` runs on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` with generated PNG bytes and a fake encoder, so nothing touches the network:
    - a stored image, an unfetched image and an imageless listing give 1 image and 3 title vectors, with the two missing images NULL;
    - a second run encodes nothing and leaves every `images` and `listings` row's `xmin` unchanged;
    - a changed title alone is embedded again;
    - a missing object and undecodable bytes are each counted and logged while the other image embeds;
    - the matcher finds nothing before the run, then queues exactly the near pair, with `image_cos` = cos 10° from the new vectors and `title_cos` 1.
  - **Worker tests.** `apps/workers/tests/test_ml.py`:
    - `ml.embed` routes to `ml` with no Beat entry;
    - flag off touches neither the database nor the model;
    - flag on passes `read_capture` and logs the counts;
    - a fresh interpreter importing `pulso_workers.ml` and `pulso_ml.embeddings` has no torch, open_clip or huggingface_hub in `sys.modules`.
  - **Mutations.** Breaking any of these fails a test: the `title_norm` write, the NULL-embedding filter, the title-change filter, a top-level model import, or the missing count.
  - **Local smoke.** `openclip()` with randomly initialised weights (no download; sockets limited to localhost) gave `(n, 512)` float32 unit vectors and wrote norm-1 vectors to pgvector. Loading the real pinned file is unverified until the worker image bakes it.
- **Not here:**
  - the ml worker image, the `ml` consumer and a Beat entry (g);
  - title normalisation;
  - the M-2 verify (95 % of listings with an image embedded within one nightly run), which needs `listing_images` enabled (an owner decision) and real data on the collector host.

### DEC-271 · A product an undo empties is marked `empty` in the same transaction; Discover, its category filter, search and the product page skip it
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-273)** · Improvement Backlog imp-273; closes DEC-263's "Not here" line on emptied products; DEC-257 (a, f), DEC-263 (b, c), DEC-239, DEC-252; AGENTS.md §3; vault Data/Product Matching, Features/Matching Queue; `docs/reports/imp-273.md`; no migration, flag, dependency or API schema change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) The decision service marks it.** `settle()` in `pulso_ml.matching.decisions` runs inside the call's locked transaction (DEC-257 f). Each product a move left or reached becomes `status = 'empty'` when no listing points at it (a delisted listing still counts), or `active` when one does. A merged product keeps `merged`.
    - `undo` settles every product its rows left or reached.
    - `merge` and `split` settle their target, so a merge or a split `into` an emptied product makes it `active` again.
    - Nothing else can empty or refill a product. Split and unlink keep a listing behind (DEC-257 a, DEC-263 c). Accept and the matcher only move product-less listings, or merge through `merge`. A merge's source becomes `merged`.
  - **(b) Readers skip `empty`.**
    - Discover (`list_products`) and its category filter (`list_categories`) add `status <> 'empty'` beside `merged_into_id IS NULL`.
    - Search's product-title candidates add it too. A merged product's title still stands for its survivor, and a listing-title hit only reaches the product that holds the listing.
    - `get_product` still follows `merged_into_id` first. An empty product then answers 404 with code `no_listings`. The product page shows its own state for that code: "No listings left on this product", saying an undo moved its last listing off and that its notes, scores and forecasts are kept, with the Daily Pulse link.
  - **(c) Nothing is deleted or rewritten.** The product row stays, so notes, tags, scores, forecasts and features keep its id, and `match_decisions` only gains the undo's own rows. `products.status` is mutable state (merge already sets it) with no CHECK, so `'empty'` needs no migration.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Readers skip products with no listing (`EXISTS` on `listings.product_id`): no state to keep, and it would cover products emptied before this change. But it changes what a listing-less product is everywhere: eight green Discover, product page and search tests seed one ("Unmatched") as a product with no source yet, and all eight fail.
  - Refuse a merge or split into an emptied product instead of reactivating it: one more 409, for a target the queue never shows; settling it is the same helper.
  - Keep the page's generic not-found copy: it says no product has this ID and that Pulso's links keep working, and neither is true here.
- **Why:** Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`, both failing first:
  - `apps/api/tests/test_matching.py::test_a_product_emptied_by_an_undo_leaves_discover_search_and_its_page`: an accept forms a product, and a split moves a listing onto a new one. Each is listed in Discover, search and the category filter, and its page answers 200. After each undo neither is listed, and each page answers 404 `no_listings`.
  - `packages/ml/tests/test_matcher.py::test_a_product_an_undo_empties_is_empty_until_listings_join_it_again`: undoing an accept and a split marks the product `empty`; a split `into` it and a merge into it make it `active`; undoing that merge marks it `empty` again and its source `active`.
  - Eight one-line mutations each fail one of them: each reader filter dropped (Discover, search, categories, page), each `settle()` call dropped (undo, split, merge), and `empty` written as `active`. A Playwright spec covers the page's `no_listings` state and fails without it.
- **Not here:**
  - Products emptied before this change (an undo since p2-25 merged) stay `active`. A read-only query finds any: `SELECT id FROM products p WHERE status = 'active' AND merged_into_id IS NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM listings WHERE product_id = p.id)`. Marking them is a one-off data fix, so it waits for the owner.
  - An empty product's sub-resources (overview, sellers, series, notes, tags) still answer for its id; the page doesn't call them.

### DEC-272 · Google popular (P2-29 b): one report at a time at `/google-popular`, brands with 10+ rows in a report hidden by default, weekly change computed on read
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-33)** · Phase 2 task p2-33 (owner priority, Lead State Queue 000b piece 1); DEC-040, DEC-166, DEC-238, DEC-267; AGENTS.md §3, §8; vault Data/Sources/Source - Google Merchant Center Popular Products; `docs/reports/p2-33.md`; no migration, dependency, settings flag or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) API.** `GET /v1/popular-products` (its own router, signed-in only, in the Q-1 contract's covered prefixes) serves one report: country US × one category × one week, ranked rows keyset-paged on rank (`limit` 1–500, default 200). The newest imported week by default, `?week=` a report Monday; the week's first category (alphabetical, a category-less file first) by default, `?category=` exact. A report downloaded twice shows its newest capture only. The page names the weeks and the week's categories for the selectors. Each row carries `as_of` = the report week and its `raw_payload_id`; the page carries the source, `captured_at` and `as_of`. The row query runs on the unique key's index (`EXPLAIN` on 400,000 synthetic rows: an index scan with the rank keyset in the index condition); the summary groups the country's rows per request, a ponytail ceiling noted in the code (an index migration when two years of weeks make it slow).
  - **(b) Major brands.** A brand (case-insensitive) with `MAJOR_BRAND_ROWS = 10` or more rows in the same report is `major_brand`. Hidden by default; `?include_major_brands=true` returns them flagged; `hidden` says how many were left out. Why 10: the owner wants dropshippable items, and Google's list is brand-heavy (in the owner's Apparel sample, the top 200 brands are 76% of rows). A white-label item rarely sits beside nine more ranked products under one brand in one category's week; an established brand does. It's a named constant in `apps/api/src/pulso_api/routers/popular_products.py`, computed on read, so changing it needs no migration. A blank brand is never flagged.
  - **(c) Weekly change** (DEC-267 d, built): Google's `previous_rank` when present, else the previous week's report of the same country and category matched on exact title and brand (the best rank if a pair repeats). `value` = previous rank − rank, positive when it moved up. Absent last week: `basis: "new"`, value null, never 0. No earlier week imported: `basis: "no_previous_week"`, value null, the UI's "no source yet".
  - **(d) Stale** = the newest imported week started more than 14 days ago (Google's own lag is up to 2 weeks). An earlier week picked on purpose isn't stale.
  - **(e) Page.** Route `/google-popular`, nav item "Google popular" in Research right after Discover (one click from the sidebar shows the newest week). A dense table: rank, signed change (sign plus ▲/▼, "New" in words), title (a link to the product page when a GTIN reaches a live product; unlinked rows show), brand (with a "Major" chip when shown), availability, price range. Week and category selects and a "Show major brands (N hidden)" toggle (`aria-pressed`, `?brands=all`) live in the URL. States: loading (skeleton), empty = nothing imported (points to Data Health › CSV import), error, stale (banner over the rows), no source yet (no earlier week: "—" in every change cell and a line naming the week to import), plus two filtered empties (no report for that week and category; every row a major brand).
  - **(f) Internal use.** The page states Google's internal-use-only terms. It sits behind sign-in like every app route; no public page may show these rows (DEC-267 f).
- **Alternatives:** (a) every category in one list: ranks collide across categories. (b) a top-N brand list or a share of the report: needs tuning per category size; a workspace hide/unhide list is the later, owner-controlled refinement (backlog). (c) case-insensitive or fuzzy title matching: Google's titles are its own cluster names, stable week to week; exact is what DEC-267 says. (d) stale on the shown week: browsing history would read as stale. (e) a Discover column: Discover lists products, and unmatched rows must show (DEC-267 e).
- **Why:** tests on synthetic imports (`apps/api/tests/test_popular_products.py`, Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`): change from `previous_rank` and from the previous week, "new" when absent, a brand with 10 rows (in two spellings) hidden and shown with the flag while a brand with 9 stays, an unlinked row present, a merged product's GTIN linking to the live product, rank-cursor paging without gaps, the newest capture of a report shown once, stale at 15 days and not at 14. Mutations (threshold 11, case-sensitive brands, no "no previous week" branch, reversed sign, oldest capture winning) each fail a test. Playwright (`apps/web/e2e/google-popular/`): the five states, one-click open, and the toggle by keyboard only.

### DEC-275 · Feature store (part a): an as-of repository as the only table door, `daily_units` and V7/G7 per demand family, `cj_stock` and `shp_inv` rows appended once per version, and the truncation leakage test
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-35)** · Phase 2 [Added] item "start product_daily_features now"; R-06, R-15; DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-044, DEC-239 (c), DEC-257 (e, g), DEC-261, DEC-266; SPEC §7, §8; AGENTS.md §3; vault Intelligence/Feature Store & Leakage Test, Intelligence/Velocity & Acceleration, Intelligence/Demand Signals; `docs/reports/p2-35.md`; no migration, flag, dependency or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One door.** `pulso_ml.features.repository` is the only module in the feature package that imports table models or `membership()`. Feature code gets an `AsOf(cutoff)` with `cutoff = d+1 00:00Z` (DEC-031). It reads captures by `captured_at < cutoff`, target evidence by its raw payload's `captured_at < cutoff`, and membership by replaying `match_decisions` before the cutoff (DEC-257 e, DEC-266). It never reads `listings.product_id`, `merged_into_id` or `observed_for`. A test parses the package's imports and fails if any other module imports a table model.
  - **(b) Families in this part.** `cj_stock` reads CJ stock-writer captures (`/product/stock/getInventoryByPid`) with `cj_tracked` targets per product id. `shp_inv` reads Shopify `.js` captures with `shopify_variants_js` targets per store; a Shopify level counts only under `inventory_management = shopify` and `inventory_policy = deny` (Demand Signals family 1). The capture predicates repeat the source writers' (DEC-120), because `pulso_ml` doesn't import `pulso_sources`. `shp_inv` sees only fixtures and old history (DEC-261). `shp_bump` is part b.
  - **(c) `daily_units`.** For each (listing, capture), units are the sum of its variants' drops since their previous capture; a rise is a restock and adds 0. Several parses of one capture: the newest row wins. An unknown level (a vanished field, or an oversell policy) breaks the chain, and a covered day only counts while every variant's latest level is known, so unknown never reads as zero sales (DEC-040). Two exclusions, both flagged and both guesses tuned in Phase 4:
    - **sync** (`shp_inv` only): more than 60% of a store's listings, counting those first captured by that crawl, drop in one run, and the store has at least 5. CJ is one warehouse, where most tracked products dropping each day is normal.
    - **implausible_drop**: a single interval drop above 30 units and above 10× the listing's trailing 28-day median daily units, where a day without a drop counts 0.
    - Cumulative counts (`parse_count`, `cumulative_units`) are built for the vault's cases but no family uses them yet: a negative delta is 0 with `negative_delta`, and a delta under the rounding step ("1.2K" steps 100) is unknown with `below_precision`.
  - **(d) V7, G7, coverage and gap.**
    - **V7(t)** sums days t−6..t from a covered baseline t−7 to a covered t. No target-coverage run longer than 3 days is allowed, and any stable listing that fails makes the product NULL.
    - **Stable sets** (M8): V7 reads member listings covered at t−7 and t. G7 = ln((V7(t)+5)/(V7(t−7)+5)) compares both weeks over listings covered at t−14 and t. A listing that joins mid-window can't change G7.
    - **Coverage** is the share of member-listing days observed over t−7..t. A day is covered when the target succeeded, an unchanged or 304 answer included (R-06), dated by its payload's capture.
    - **has_gap** is set when V7 is NULL, or when a stable listing missed a day in t−7..t.
  - **(e) Rows and writer.** One row per (product, day, family, version), for products with a member listing that family has seen a known level for. With none, there's no row: Discover's "no source" stays. The writer is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the primary key and never updates, so re-running a day changes nothing (xmin unchanged), and a new version appends. `feature_version` is `{revision:03d}-{sha256(config)[:12]}` (now `001-0754a5e6a014`), which sorts by revision, so DEC-239 (c)'s "newest version" holds. Bump `revision` with any change to the maths.
  - **(f) No migration.** The 0005 table holds V7, G7, coverage and has_gap. The vault's per-family `gap_flags` JSON (missed-run dates) and its `features` JSON wait for the first consumer that needs them (Phase 4 scores, Phase 6).
  - **(g) Leakage test (part a, in the normal pytest run).** A 45-day fixture (`packages/ml/tests/test_leakage.py`) holds every demand case: a restock, an implausible drop, a store-wide sync, a late snapshot (`observed_for` 2 days before its capture), missed runs, a 5-day outage, a failed target, unchanged days, a re-parse, an overselling store, a run planned before midnight and fetched at it, a listing first captured exactly at a cutoff, and the day-40 merge and split stamped exactly at day 39's cutoff. For nine dates, features from the full DB must equal those from a copy truncated inside a rolled-back savepoint. The copy has evidence at or after the cutoff deleted (the append-only trigger disabled inside that savepoint) and today's `listings.product_id` and `merged_into_id` cleared. Part (b), the splitter's purge assertion, arrives with the Phase 6 harness. Trends captures and the CSV backfill join the fixture when a feature reads them.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (d) One stable set for V7 and G7: V7 would need 15 days instead of 8. Excluding a gappy listing instead of NULLing the product: it would hide that listing's restock or sales.
  - (e) Upsert: rewrites history (AGENTS.md §3). A plain hash version: DEC-239's "newest" would compare hashes.
  - (f) Migration 0010 for `gap_flags`: no reader yet; coverage and has_gap carry what Discover and the Trends tab show.
  - (g) A fixture replayed only up to the cutoff instead of a truncated copy: it never sees today's pointers, so it can't catch a current-mapping read.
- **Why:** tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`, plus pure tests (`packages/ml/tests/test_demand.py`, `test_leakage.py`). They cover each `daily_units` case, the 3- and 4-day gaps, the missing ends, unchanged days, G7's prior and the mid-window listing; truncation invariance on nine dates; membership before and after day 40; and a re-run leaving every xmin unchanged while a new version appends. 19 one-line mutations each fail a test. Among them are membership and captures read one row past the cutoff, coverage dated by the target's planning time, membership from today's pointers, and `observed_for` as the capture time; all five fail the truncation test (`docs/reports/p2-35.md`).
- **Not here (part b, `docs/reports/p2-35.md`):** the worker task (yesterday plus a backfill range) and the Beat slot the lead adds, the `GET /v1/products` momentum end-to-end test, `shp_bump`, and gap_flags dates on Data Health.

### DEC-276 · An undone accept frees its listings for the matcher again: `manual()` skips the accept together with its undo, and only `undo()` writes an unlink that names a candidate
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-36)** · refines DEC-263 (b); cross-review XR-36 (`docs/reports/p2-34.md`, vault Agents/Reviews/2026-10-25 Claude - p2-20 p2-25 p2-26 p2-28 p2-29 cross-review); DEC-249 (h), DEC-257 (c), DEC-266; R-15; vault Data/Product Matching, Features/Matching Queue; `docs/reports/p2-36.md`; no migration, flag, dependency or API change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Rule.** `manual()` reads the listing's non-reject rows newest first (id order, DEC-266). An `unlink` that names a candidate is an undone accept. It is skipped together with the row before it, the accept it reverses, and the next row decides. The listing is manual when that row isn't the matcher's, and free when no row is left. So the matcher sees what it saw before the accept: a free listing is free again, and one an owner unlink froze earlier stays frozen.
  - **(b) Why the row before is the accept.** `undo()` reverses a decision only while each of its rows is its listing's latest row of any action, and it writes the compensating row under the same matching lock. So the undo row directly follows the row it reverses. `INVERSE` maps only `accept` to `unlink`.
  - **(c) The marker.** `undo()` copies the undone row's `candidate_id`, and every accept names its candidate. `unlink()` names none, and nothing else writes an `unlink`. A user `reason` is free text and can start with `undo #` (XR-41), so `manual()` doesn't read the prefix: an owner unlink with that reason stays frozen. The marker needs no new column and no migration. A future `unlink()` that names a candidate would break it; a comment at its `record()` call says so.
  - **(d) Scope.** `manual()` decides only product-less movers (`link()`). A product-less listing's latest non-reject row put it on no product, so that row is an unlink (the owner's or undo's). An accept moves only product-less listings, so the row before an accept is an unlink too. The scan therefore meets only unlinks and the accepts they undo. The undo of a merge, a split or an unlink leaves the listing on a product, which the matcher never moves, so no other undo needs a skip.
  - **(e) Observation (the XR-36 review): a split's cannot-link outlives its undo.** The `split` row stays, so `apart()` still keeps the listing apart from its old product's group. That is moot while the listing sits on that product, because `neighbours()` excludes its own product. It would matter only if the listing later left that product without a split. Not changed here.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Reading the `undo #` reason prefix: a user reason can carry it (XR-41), and an owner unlink would then lose its freeze.
  - A `reverts` column, or an `undo` action in the `action_values` check: a migration, and DEC-263 (b) already rejected the column.
  - Refusing a user reason that starts with `undo #` (XR-41's fix): an API behaviour change that belongs to the XR-37..41 cleanup row. This marker doesn't depend on it.
  - Keeping the freeze and amending DEC-263 (b): a misclick and its undo would take a supplier listing, usually the hub its product's store listings link through, out of auto-linking for good.
- **Why:** Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` (`packages/ml/tests/test_matcher.py`):
  - P2-34's strict-xfail `test_xr36_an_undone_accept_leaves_the_listings_free_for_the_matcher` passes with its mark removed: after an accept and its undo, a new 1.0 listing links with the supplier listing.
  - The new `test_xr36_an_owner_unlink_stays_frozen_under_a_forged_reason_and_an_undone_accept` holds an owner unlink whose reason is `undo #<id>: forged`, and still holds it after an accept on top of it is undone, while the other listing of that accept is free.
  - Five mutations each fail a test: drop the skip; skip without the reversed row; treat any unlink as an undo; free a listing whenever an undo is on top; trust the `undo #` prefix (`docs/reports/p2-36.md`).
- **Not here:** XR-37..41, including XR-41's own fix (`undo()` still refuses a decision whose reason starts with `undo #`).

### DEC-278 · Feature store (part b): `ml.features` on the ml queue behind `FEATURES_ENABLED`, yesterday (UTC) by default or an inclusive ISO backfill with one commit per day, and a Discover momentum end-to-end test over the leakage fixture
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-38)** · Phase 2 task P2-35 part b; DEC-031, DEC-044, DEC-239 (c), DEC-249, DEC-269 (g), DEC-275; AGENTS.md §3, §7; vault Intelligence/Feature Store & Leakage Test, Intelligence/Velocity & Acceleration, Agents/Improvement Backlog imp-284; `docs/reports/p2-38.md`; no migration, dependency or CI change; one new flag
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Task.** `ml.features` (`apps/workers/src/pulso_workers/ml.py`, next to `ml.match` and `ml.embed`) routes to `ml` through the existing `ml.*` route. With no args it computes yesterday's UTC date. `start` and `end` (ISO dates) backfill inclusive: `end` defaults to yesterday and `start` to `end`, so `start` alone runs through yesterday and `end` alone runs that one day. Each day is `pulso_ml.features.daily.run()` and then a commit, logged as `features_done day=… written=…`. A failure stops the range; the days before it stay committed, and a re-run skips them (DEC-275 e).
  - **(b) Refusals.** A range that reaches today (UTC) or is reversed raises `ValueError` before the database opens. Rows are append-only, so a day computed before its cutoff would keep a partial row for good.
  - **(c) Flag.** `FEATURES_ENABLED` (setting `features_enabled`, default off, listed in `.env.example`). Off, the task logs `features_disabled` and never opens the database, like `MATCHER_ENABLED` and `EMBEDDINGS_ENABLED`.
  - **(d) No Beat entry here.** The collector host has no `-Q ml` consumer yet (DEC-269 g, imp-284), so a scheduled send would sit in Redis unconsumed. When imp-284 lands, its Beat entries add `"ml.features": {"task": "ml.features", "schedule": crontab(minute=0, hour=7), "options": {"queue": "ml"}}` to `schedules.py`: 07:00 UTC, after CJ (04:00) and listing images (06:00). The owner then turns `FEATURES_ENABLED` on. Unlike `ml.embed`, this task needs no `clip` extra or weights.
  - **(e) End-to-end test.** `apps/api/tests/test_product_momentum.py` loads the leakage fixture (`packages/ml/tests/test_leakage.py`) by path, since test folders aren't packages here. It runs days 8–45 as the backfill would, then reads `GET /v1/products?sort=momentum`. The order must equal each live product's latest feature day with a growth, best grade on that day (DEC-239 c), by G7 then id descending, with insufficient signal last. The order is also pinned by hand: P5, P4, P3, P2, P1, P7. Each card's value, growth, family, grade, gap and as_of equal its row's; P7 (a V7 but no G7) and P2 (unknown since day 36) never read 0.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Requiring both `start` and `end`: `start` alone is the usual backfill ("since history began"). Celery `date` arguments: the JSON serializer carries strings.
  - (b) Allowing today with a warning: the partial row would stay, and only a version bump could replace it.
  - (d) A Beat entry now with the flag off: still a daily send with no consumer, and the schedule belongs with the ml consumer's rollout.
  - (e) Moving the fixture into an importable package or a pytest `pythonpath` entry: it touches part a's test file or shared config for a single reuse.
- **Why:** `apps/workers/tests/test_ml.py` covers registration on `ml` with no schedule; the flag unset and `off` never touching the database; yesterday from a clock pinned at 00:30 UTC; an inclusive 4-day range with run and commit interleaved; and both refusals. Eight one-line mutations each fail a test: yesterday → today, the local date, a naive `now()`, a skipped commit, one commit at the end, an exclusive range, today allowed, and the flag ignored. The API test fails when Discover reads the oldest day first, ranks grade before day, floats insufficient signal to the top, drops the growth filter, or breaks ties by id ascending (`docs/reports/p2-38.md`).
- **Not here:** the Beat entry and the ml consumer (imp-284); `shp_bump`; gap_flags dates on Data Health (backlog rows in the report).

### DEC-277 · Google popular brand list: a workspace hides any brand or shows a major one, applied on read; current preference, deleted in place
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-37)** · Phase 2 task p2-37 (owner priority 2026-10-25, Lead State Queue 000b piece 1 rest, p2-33 backlog row b1); DEC-028, DEC-040, DEC-267, DEC-272; AGENTS.md §3, §7, §8; vault Data/Sources/Source - Google Merchant Center Popular Products; `docs/reports/p2-37.md`; migration 0011; no dependency, network destination, settings flag or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Table.** `popular_brand_rules` (migration 0011, `WorkspaceOwned`): `workspace_id` NOT NULL and indexed, `brand_norm`, `brand` (the spelling last set, for display), `action` (`hide` or `show`, CHECK), `created_at`, `created_by` (users). Primary key `(workspace_id, brand_norm)`, so one rule per workspace and brand. A CHECK holds `brand_norm = lower(brand)`, the same case-insensitive key DEC-272 b counts major brands by (Postgres `lower()`); the API writes it with `lower()` in SQL, so the two can't drift. `popular_products` is untouched: still global and append-only.
  - **(b) Preference, not history.** A rule is the workspace's current setting, like `product_tags`. Setting a brand again replaces its rule (`created_at` becomes when the current action was set, `created_by` who set it) and removing it is a plain `DELETE`. Nothing reads past rules, so no history table. The 0011 downgrade refuses while any rule exists ("restore a backup"), like 0005 with owner input.
  - **(c) API.** Signed-in, workspace-scoped, stable operation ids: `list_popular_brand_rules` (`GET /v1/popular-products/brands`, keyset-paged on `brand_norm`, `limit` 1–500, default 200), `put_popular_brand_rule` (`PUT …/brands` with `{brand, action}`, an upsert that returns the rule) and `delete_popular_brand_rule` (`DELETE …/brands?brand=`, which returns the rule removed or null, so it's idempotent). The brand travels in the body or query, never the path, because Google's brands can contain `/`. No `workspace_id` in any body: the workspace comes from the session.
  - **(d) On read.** `GET /v1/popular-products` loads the caller's rules for the report's brands. A `hide` rule leaves the brand out like a major brand. A `show` rule exempts a major brand from `MAJOR_BRAND_ROWS`. A hide rule wins over the 10-row rule (it's the more specific choice). Rows gain `hidden_by` (`major`, `you` or null); `major_brand` stays the 10-row fact. `hidden` counts every row left out, and the new `hidden_by_you` counts the ones the workspace's rules left out. Both are 0 with `include_major_brands=true`, which still returns every row, flagged.
