  - **(e) Page.** Each row's brand cell has one icon button: Hide brand (EyeOff), Unhide brand on a row you hid, or Always show brand on a major row (both Eye, seen with hidden brands shown). It appears on row hover or focus, always on touch (`pointer-coarse`), and is a real `<button>` with the global focus ring. Chips: "Major", "Hidden" (yours), "Shown" (a major brand you show). The toggle is now "Show hidden brands (N)", and the count line splits major and yours. Each change is optimistic: the brand's loaded rows leave (or are re-flagged with every brand shown), a polite toast says what changed with Undo for 8 s (held while hovered or focused), then the report refetches. On failure the rows come back and the toast says why. When a hidden row's button leaves with it, focus moves to the next row's. Hiding is 1 click; unhiding is 2 (Show hidden brands, then Unhide).
  - **(f) Terms.** A brand list is the workspace's own preference about Google's rows. It aggregates nothing across businesses, and DEC-267 f (internal use only, no public page) is unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** (a) store the brand lower-cased only: the list would show "bigco", not "BigCo". (b) a history table (append-only rules): nothing reads it, and AGENTS.md §3's append-only rule covers observations, scores and decisions, not preferences. (c) the brand as a path segment: breaks on `/`. (d) normalise in Python (`str.lower`): the read compares in SQL, so SQL `lower()` keeps one definition. (e) a Manage brands popover listing every rule with one-click removal: built and tested (commit "Google popular: one-click Hide brand, Manage brands popover"), then removed in the next commit because production code passed the task's ~400-line budget. It's a backlog row; reverting that commit restores it. Until then a hidden brand comes back from any report that lists it. (f) show the row action always: 30 icons of noise in a dense table; hover or focus reveal is the Linear and Vercel pattern, and touch always shows it.
- **Why:** tests on synthetic imports (Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`): `packages/core/tests/test_brand_rules_schema.py` covers two-workspace isolation, one rule per workspace and brand in any case, `workspace_id` NOT NULL, the action and `brand_norm` CHECKs, a plain delete, and the downgrade refusing while rules exist, then downgrade, upgrade clean. `apps/api/tests/test_popular_products.py` covers a brand hidden for workspace A only (B still sees it and can't remove A's rule), hidden rows counted in `hidden` and `hidden_by_you`, every row flagged with `include_major_brands`, a shown brand with 10 rows staying visible, a re-set replacing the rule, paging, and validation. Mutations (no workspace filter on read, no show exemption, `hidden_by_you` counting majors, delete across workspaces) each fail a test. Playwright (`apps/web/e2e/google-popular/`): hide in 1 click with Undo, unhide in 2, keyboard-only hide with focus moving on, always show, and a failed change rolled back.

### DEC-279 · Amazon (AMZ-1): the owner's CSV only, one append-only `amazon_ranks` row per ASIN × category × rank kind × day, products linked by GTIN on read
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-01)** · Phase 3 task AMZ-1 (p3-01); DEC-010, DEC-028, DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-191, DEC-267; SPEC §4.2, §4.3 #5; AGENTS.md §3; vault Data/Sources/Source - Amazon; `docs/reports/p3-01.md`; migration 0012 (one table and a disabled source row); no dependency, settings flag, network destination or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Source and file.** `amazon` (source 8, type `csv`, disabled, phase 3) imports a file the owner exports or compiles, through the shared CSV import in Data Health. No API, account, key or vendor; Pulso never sends a request to Amazon. The help text in `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md` and the vault note says to obtain the data lawfully: CSV is not an exemption from Amazon's terms.
  - **(b) Template `csv-v1`:** `asin,title,category,rank,rank_kind,price,currency,rating,review_count,gtins,observed_for`. `asin`, `title`, `category` and `rank` are required. `rank_kind` is `bsr` (default) or `movers`; `currency` defaults to `USD`; `observed_for` blank means the import day. Row errors carry their row number: an ASIN that isn't 10 uppercase letters or digits, a rank of 0, a rating outside 0–5 or with more than two decimals, an unknown rank kind, and every shared check (required fields, negative money, a future date). An unknown column fails the file closed. The documented template drops the earlier `url` column (the ASIN names the product) and renames `observed_on` to the platform's `observed_for`.
  - **(c) Rows.** One `amazon_ranks` observation per ASIN × category × rank kind × day: an ASIN ranks in several categories and in both lists, so the ASIN alone isn't the entity. Global market data, no `workspace_id`. `captured_at` and `raw_payload_id` come from the capture; unique on `(asin, category, rank_kind, observed_for, raw_payload_id, parser_version)` (DEC-028). An entity repeated in one file rejects the file with both row numbers. A re-imported file skips before parsing (same sha256), and a re-parse by the same parser inserts nothing. A trigger rejects UPDATE and DELETE. Money is `price_minor` + `currency`; rating `NUMERIC(3,2)`; review count cumulative.
  - **(d) Products.** GTINs are stored as written and resolve on read through `link_products()` (DEC-267 c, reused, now typed for both tables): M-4's `gtin14` on both sides, merges followed, linked only when the valid codes reach exactly one live product. Unmatched rows are kept. No `match_candidates` or `match_decisions`.
  - **(e) Data Health.** A source of type `csv` reads "CSV only" in the sources table and run drawer, and the static "Amazon · No source yet" planned row is removed now that the real row exists.
  - **(f) The surface is deferred** (an Amazon list page, or Product page rows): a backlog row in the report. It needs the "manual import" label with its date (vault note acceptance) and the five states.
- **Alternatives:** (c) Amazon rows as `listings` + `listing_snapshots` (the vault note's first design, whose columns exist): one snapshot per listing per capture can't hold an ASIN's ranks in two categories, and Amazon listings would enter the matcher, Discover counts and search without a decision on that. A product id stored at import: frozen, so later products and merges never link (DEC-267 c). (b) a strict `B0` + 8 ASIN: book ASINs are ISBN-10s.
- **Why:** tests on a synthetic fixture (`packages/sources/tests/imports/test_amazon.py`, `fixtures/amazon.csv`, Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`): the fixture and the template parse; five malformed rows fail with their row numbers while the valid row parses; a repeated entity rejects the file naming rows 2 and 3; importing the fixture twice leaves 3 rows, with the second import skipped and a re-parse inserting 0; an UPDATE is refused; a UPC row links to a product whose store wrote it as a 13-digit code, and a row without GTINs stays unlinked.

### DEC-282 · The matcher reads redirects after each lock; a pending GTIN pair whose code is gone goes back to M-3, scored on its stored components
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-267)** · refines DEC-255 (c) and DEC-257 (b, f); cross-review lows XR-30, XR-34 (`docs/reports/p2-24.md`, vault Agents/Reviews/2026-10-25 Claude - p2-16 p2-19 p2-21 p2-22 cross-review); DEC-260 (c), DEC-266; Improvement Backlog imp-267; `docs/reports/imp-267.md`; no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Redirects are read after the lock (XR-30).** The score pass reloads `redirects()` in each listing's transaction, and the GTIN pass before each pair, right after `lock()`. A run no longer loads them once at its start. An owner merge that committed before the lock is then part of every group the transaction reads, so `apart()` sees a split against a product merged mid-run. No merge can commit while the transaction holds the lock.
  - **(b) A pending `gtin-1` pair the GTIN pass no longer produces goes back to M-3 (XR-34).** That happens when one barcode changes, or when a vendor starts reusing the code and drops out of it (DEC-255 b). After the per-GTIN transactions, one locked transaction gives each such row M-3's `scorer_version` and the score of its stored components (`combined()`), or 0 when it has none. Its status stays `pending`.
    - The score pass runs next in the same run and re-scores the pair like any pending pair it meets, so the pair links, stays queued or leaves the queue as `rejected` (DEC-260 c).
    - A pair M-3 doesn't meet keeps the score its own evidence gives. A pair the GTIN pass created has no components, so it reads 0.0000, and a 1.0 never outlives its code.
    - Pairs the GTIN pass still produces, including ones it skips (already on one product, split apart), keep `gtin-1`.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Resolving `from_product_id` through `merged_into_id` in `apart()`'s SQL: needs a recursive query for chains (XR-31), and only `apart()` would read fresh state.
  - (b) Changing only `scorer_version`, as the review suggested: a pair M-3 doesn't meet would keep 1.0 under M-3's version, which reads as an M-3 certainty.
  - (b) Marking it `rejected` at once: the score pass never re-scores a decided row, so a pair that still looks alike would leave the queue for good, and `rejected` also vetoes third-listing joins (DEC-249 h).
  - (b) Deleting the row: an undone accept can return a candidate that decisions reference to `pending` (DEC-263).
- **Why:** Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` (`packages/ml/tests/test_matcher.py`). `test_xr30_a_merge_before_or_during_a_run_never_hides_a_split` (image and GTIN, merge before or mid-run) and `test_xr34_a_gtin_pair_whose_code_is_gone_goes_back_to_m3` fail on `origin/main` `19dc46e` and pass now. Ten mutations of the matcher each fail a test (`docs/reports/imp-267.md`).
- **Not here:** marking a pending row `split` when `apart()` finds one (XR-30's optional part); pending rows the XR-30 race already left behind stay until the owner decides them.

### DEC-284 · `CURRENT_PHASE` names the phase in progress; a route counts as built once its phase is done, or earlier with `built: true`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p2-39)** · Phase 2 end task p2-39 (Lead Playbook §5 step 2); imp-001; vault Design/App Shell & Navigation; `docs/reports/p2-39.md`; no migration, dependency or API change
- **Decision:** `apps/web/src/lib/nav.ts` sets `CURRENT_PHASE = 3`, the phase under way (Phases 1 and 2 are built; Phase 3 started 2026-10-25). `isBuilt()` changes from `phase <= CURRENT_PHASE` to `phase < CURRENT_PHASE`, so a route counts as built once its phase is done. A route the running phase ships early keeps saying so with `built: true`, as Discover, Google popular, Matching and Settings did during Phase 2. The coming-soon page for a route of the phase in progress reads "Phase 3, Signals expansion, is under way and builds this page." and drops its second legend line, which would only repeat the first.
- **Alternatives:** (a) keep `<=` and set 3: Store Spy (`/stores`, `/stores/{id}`, Phase 3, not built) would count as built, so the catch-all page would 404 it, and the sidebar and palette would offer a link to nothing. (b) keep `<=` and set 2: the not-built page would say Pulso is in Phase 2 while Phase 3 tasks run, which is wrong. (c) a `built: false` override per Phase 3 route: one more thing to remember on every phase bump.
- **Why:** `src/lib/nav.test.ts` pins `CURRENT_PHASE` at 3 and the same built set as before (`/`, Discover, Google popular, Data Health, Matching, UI gallery, Settings), plus `/stores` and `/stores/abc` not built. `e2e/p2-39/fixes.spec.ts` checks the Screener page ("Pulso is in Phase 3, Signals expansion.") and the Store Spy page ("is under way", no "This page:" line).

### DEC-283 · AliExpress live lookup (AE-1, AE-2): DS product and freight calls signed per the Open Platform docs, answers in Redis for 24 h, blocks on the shared source state, no source row yet
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-03)** · implements DEC-037; SPEC §4.2, §4.3 #4; AGENTS.md §3, §4; vault Data/Sources/Source - AliExpress; `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md` (AliExpress, docs check 2026-10-25); `docs/reports/p3-03.md`; one flag and three key settings; no migration, dependency or endpoint
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Methods.** `aliexpress.ds.product.get` (`ship_to_country=US`, `target_currency=USD`) and `aliexpress.ds.freight.query` (quantity 1, US, USD, one SKU), sent as `POST https://api-sg.aliexpress.com/sync?method=<method>` with a form body. Both need the owner's seller-authorized DS access token. No affiliate method is called: they return no per-SKU stock or freight.
  - **(b) Signing** (docs 1366 and 1367): every parameter except `sign`, sorted by name in ASCII order, name+value with no separator, blank values skipped as the official SDK does, no path prefix for `/sync` methods. HMAC-SHA256 keyed with the App Secret, upper-case hex. Sent: `app_key`, `access_token`, `format=json`, `method`, `sign_method=sha256`, `timestamp` (epoch milliseconds) and the business parameters. The test reproduces both published worked examples.
  - **(c) Cache.** `pulso_sources.aliexpress.lookup.lookup()` reads Redis key `pulso:aliexpress:v1:<product_id>:<sku_id or ->:US` first. On a miss it calls product.get, then freight.query when a SKU is given, and stores the parsed answer with `as_of` for 86,400 s. Found and not-found answers are cached; failures are not. A per-UTC-day Redis counter caps calls at 500 (the vault note's cap), counted before each call and never refunded. IDs must be digits.
  - **(d) Nothing durable (DEC-037).** No `raw_payloads` row, S3 object, snapshot, observation, feature or `collector_runs` row, and no log line. The vault note's `collector_runs` row per lookup is dropped: AE-2 requires zero new rows, and Data Health still sees the source's block state and disable events.
  - **(e) Gates, each a status rather than an exception.** `ALIEXPRESS_ENABLED` (default off) gives `disabled`. A missing app key, secret or access token gives `not_configured`. A missing or disabled `aliexpress` source row gives `disabled`. A future `retry_not_before`, or the daily cap, gives `throttled`. The real HTTPS transport keeps the collector guard (`COLLECTORS_LIVE=on` outside a linked worktree, plus the contact User-Agent) and otherwise gives `disabled`. Transports are injected, and tests never open a socket.
  - **(f) Blocks reuse the shared state** (the `sources` block columns, `SourceEvent`, the lifecycle's `_retry_after`, `BLOCK_STATUSES` and `COLLECTOR_BLOCK_THRESHOLD`), the way the lifecycle and CJ record them. HTTP 403/429/430, or the documented throttle message ("Api access frequency exceeds the limit. this ban will last N seconds"), is a block. `Retry-After`, or else the message's N seconds, moves `retry_not_before` forward. Five consecutive blocks disable the source with one event, `consecutive_blocks:aliexpress`, and a success resets the count. A `SYSTEM` `error_response` (the docs' "API platform error": authority, permission or signature) disables at once with `aliexpress_auth_rejected`. This fails closed: a signing bug also disables, and the owner re-enables. `ISP`/other errors and transport failures fail only that lookup. The docs name no throttle or auth error code, so the classifier keys on the documented message, the documented type and the HTTP status.
  - **(g) Money.** SKU prices and freight fees parse to USD minor units, and any other currency fails the answer. `shipping_fee_cent` is described as cents, but the doc example is `"172.71"` for "US $172.71". It is parsed as major units and must equal the number in `shipping_fee_format`, so a cents answer fails instead of costing 100×.
  - **(h) No source row and no migration in this task.** Migrations seed no `aliexpress` row (ids 1–4, 6, 7). Adding one is a migration, and it would change Data Health, whose e2e expects "No source yet · arrives in Phase 3" for AliExpress. Without the row every lookup reads `disabled`. AE-3 adds the row, disabled, with its endpoint and UI.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Running lookups through `execute_run`: it stores every capture, and storage refuses `aliexpress` (DEC-037).
  - Affiliate `productdetail.get`/`hotproduct.query`: no token needed, but no SKU stock or freight, and it needs a Portals tracking ID.
  - Counting a `SYSTEM` error toward the five-block rule: a revoked token would get four more calls.
  - A `collector_runs` row per lookup, or a migration with the disabled row now (see d and h).
- **Why:** nine tests in `packages/sources/tests/aliexpress`, 13 runs: the four lookup tests run on SQLite and on Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`, where AE-2 counts rows in every migrated table. Eleven mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/p3-03.md`).
- **Unverified until the first owner-run call:** whether the gateway also needs the SDK's `v=2.0`, `partner_id`, `simplify` or a `session` token name; the units of `shipping_fee_cent`; where product.get's 602/605 appear; the throttle and auth error codes. No AliExpress request was made here.
- **Not here:** the endpoint and the Suppliers-tab rows (AE-3), the `aliexpress` source row, the DS token create/refresh flow, and the R-17 CSV fallback.

### DEC-281 · Product keywords (KW-1): a workspace's confirmed or rejected keyword per product, candidates scored from the product's titles on read, one-click confirm on the Trends tab
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-02)** · Phase 3 task KW-1; DEC-029, DEC-040, DEC-238, DEC-245 (d), DEC-263; SPEC §5 Signals, §6.4 Trends; vault Phases/Phase 3 - Signals Expansion, Features/Product Page - Trends, Data/Data Model; migration 0013; no dependency, flag, source request or schedule
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Workspace scope.** Confirming a keyword is a workspace's choice, like `product_tags` and `workspace_tracked_stores` (DEC-029 "Workspace choices"). So `product_keywords` is `WorkspaceOwned`: `workspace_id` NOT NULL and indexed. This narrows the vault Data Model, which lists it under global Signals. KW-2's weekly Trends set will read the union of confirmed keywords across workspaces, the way the crawl reads tracked stores.
  - **(b) Table (0013).**
    - Columns: primary key `(workspace_id, product_id, keyword)`; `keyword` varchar(100) in normal form (casefolded, curly apostrophe made straight, spacing collapsed); `relevance` numeric(4,3), the title score at the first decision, null for a typed-in keyword; `source` `title` · `manual`; `status` `confirmed` · `rejected`; `decided_by` (FK users); `decided_at`; `created_at`; CHECKs on all three.
    - A new decision updates `status`, `decided_by` and `decided_at` in place, so `created_at` keeps the first decision and `decided_at` the current one. Earlier flips aren't kept.
    - Nothing deletes a row: no endpoint does, triggers refuse DELETE and TRUNCATE, and the downgrade refuses while any row exists. Taking a keyword back means rejecting it.
  - **(c) Candidates on read** (`pulso_api.keywords`, pure functions).
    - **Titles:** the product's name and every listing title, deduplicated case-insensitively.
    - **Phrases:** runs of 1–4 words between stop words, punctuation, a spaced dash and dropped tokens. Dropped tokens are single characters, pure numbers and quantities (`2pcs`, `50ft`, `5000mah`); listing noise such as "new", "hot", "sale" and "free shipping" counts as stop words.
    - **Keys:** a trailing plural folds away (`taxonomy.fold`), and a phrase shows the spelling most titles use.
    - **Relevance:** half the share of titles holding the phrase plus half the mean share holding each of its words (titles often reorder the same words), times a length weight: 1 word 0.5, 2 words 0.85, 3–4 words 1.0. The weights are guesses.
    - **Shown:** a phrase that only ever appears inside one longer phrase with the same evidence is left out. The best 12 not yet decided are shown, best first, then the longer phrase, then A to Z.
    - Nothing is stored, and no Beat task or command runs it.
  - **(d) API** (both operations are covered by the provenance contract).
    - `GET /v1/products/{id}/keywords` (`list_product_keywords`) returns `KeywordList {keywords}`: one list where `status` is `candidate`, `confirmed` or `rejected`.
      - `relevance` is computed from the current titles; it is null when no title holds the keyword, shown as "Typed in", never 0 (DEC-040).
      - `as_of` is the newest listing `last_seen_at`, or the product's `first_seen_at` when it has no listing.
      - The order is relevance, typed-in keywords last, then A to Z.
    - `POST /v1/products/{id}/keywords` (`set_product_keyword`) takes `{keyword, status: confirmed|rejected}`. It confirms or rejects a candidate, or adds a keyword by hand (confirmed).
      - Validation: 1–100 characters after trimming, with at least one letter or digit; extra fields fail.
      - The server sets `source`: `title` when a title holds the keyword. A plural or case variant of a decided keyword decides that keyword. Repeating the newest decision writes nothing.
      - Writes go to the live product, so a merged id answers 409 `merged`. Reads follow `merged_into_id` at any depth (DEC-263), and the newest decision per keyword wins.
  - **(e) UI.** The Trends tab gets a Search keywords section under the chart.
    - Confirmed keywords are chips; only a typed-in one carries a mark. The chip's × rejects.
    - An add field: Enter adds and confirms.
    - Suggested rows show a relevance bar and number, with Confirm and Reject.
    - A Rejected disclosure offers "Confirm instead".
    - A muted no-source line covers per-keyword search interest.
    - Confirming takes one click once the tab is open (two from the product page). Focus moves to the row that takes its place.
    - Saves are optimistic and built on the cache, not the render (imp-278). Each save settles through its own `mutateAsync` promise, because `mutate`'s per-call callbacks fire for the latest call only. Only the newest answer replaces the list. A failure rolls back and says so, even while a later save is in flight.
    - States: loading, error with Retry, stale line, nothing to suggest, failed save.
    - No feature flag: the section is finished.
  - **(f) Not KW-2.** Nothing calls SerpApi or Google Trends, and no confirmed keyword joins a scheduled set. The series' `search` reason now says interest isn't collected for the product's keywords yet, instead of "Keywords arrive in Phase 3".
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Global `product_keywords`, as the Data Model note has it: one workspace's guess would become every workspace's product link.
  - (b) An append-only decision log beside the table: a second table for a history v1 never reads. A status row with who/when, protected by triggers, keeps every decision's current state honest.
  - (c) Storing candidates through a one-shot command: they go stale as titles change, and scoring on read costs a few titles per product. Corpus-wide TF-IDF needs the whole catalog per read. AI extraction is labelled output, not data (DEC-047), and costs money.
  - (d) Separate confirm, reject and add endpoints: three operations for one upsert. A `DELETE` to undo: it would remove a decision.
- **Why:** pure tests (`apps/api/tests/test_keywords.py`, 8) cover the fixture lamp and car-mount titles, noise and quantities, plural folding, punctuation and hyphens, repeated titles, skip and limit, and the normal form. API tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` (`test_product_keywords.py`, 6) cover the round trip with who/when, plural variants, validation, 404/409, two-workspace isolation and merged read-through. Five mutations each fail a test: no workspace filter, no repeat check, candidates not skipping decided ones, no merged read, a variant writing a new row. Schema tests (`test_phase3_schema.py`) cover the delete and truncate triggers, the refusing downgrade and the CHECKs. Playwright (`apps/web/e2e/products/keywords.spec.ts`, 10) covers one-click confirm, reject and confirm instead, add and remove, keyboard-only use, loading, error, empty, stale, a failed save, two quick decisions with the first failing (it fails with `mutate` callbacks) and 375px.
- **Not here:** KW-2 (weekly Trends set and the SerpApi cost line in the add field), interest, slope and sparkline columns, and the "top 50 products have a confirmed keyword" check, which needs the owner's real confirmations.

### DEC-287 · A NUL in free API text answers 422: `NoNulStr` on body text, a NUL-only `DataError` handler for path, query and cursor text, and CSV's existing cell check
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-311)** · follows XR-32 and DEC-282's search fix (imp-267, `docs/reports/imp-267.md` imp-267-b1); Improvement Backlog imp-311; `docs/reports/imp-311.md`; no migration, flag or dependency; OpenAPI gains patterns only
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One type for body text.** `apps/api/src/pulso_api/schemas/text.py` defines `NoNulStr`: `str` with the pattern `^[^\x00]*$`. Every free-text request-body field uses it: product tags (`Tag`), the note body, the three match-decision reasons, the source patch reason, the profit `method`, and `Brand`, which the brand-rule body and the DELETE `brand` query share. Search's `q` moves onto it from its own copy of the pattern (XR-32), so its schema is unchanged. FastAPI answers these with the usual `validation_error` 422 naming the field, before any SQL.
  - **(b) A NUL-only handler for the rest.** Path, query and cursor text reaches SQL as a parameter: the admin source `key`, `/admin/runs?source`, a run's target cursor, the matching queue's `version`, the brand-rule cursor and Discover's `source`. psycopg refuses a NUL there on the client, before anything is sent, with `DataError: PostgreSQL text fields cannot contain NUL (0x00) bytes`. `main.py` maps a SQLAlchemy `DataError` whose cause says "cannot contain NUL" to a 422 problem with code `invalid_text`. Any other `DataError` is re-raised and stays a logged 500. The request's session rolls back as it does for any error.
  - **(c) CSV keeps its check.** `_cell` has refused NUL since `41fde51b`. A NUL cell, or a Merchant Center `Filters:` value that becomes every row's column, is that row's `Invalid <kind> value` error, and the file's other rows import under the usual row-error rules. New tests pin both.
- **Alternatives:**
  - The handler alone: smaller, but OpenAPI wouldn't say NUL is refused, a body error would lose its field location, and the 422 would come only after the request's earlier statements ran.
  - The type on every query and path parameter as well: about six more one-line changes, but JSON cursors decode inside the endpoint and would still need the handler, which already covers the rest.
  - Stripping NUL silently: it changes what the owner typed.
  - Treating every `DataError` (SQLSTATE class 22) as a 422: a numeric overflow or bad date from a server bug would read as the client's fault.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_nul_inputs.py` has 10 typed cases, 6 handled cases, and one proving another `DataError` stays a 500, all on Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`. 15 of its 16 NUL cases fail on `origin/main` `e92e2de`: 11 were 500s, 3 decision reasons met a 404 or 409 first, and the profit method reached no SQL without a supplier listing. Search's `q` already passed. All pass now, and a tab or newline still gets each endpoint's earlier status. `packages/sources/tests/imports/test_nul_cells.py` runs on SQLite and Postgres. 13 mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/imp-311.md`).
- **Not here:** a `\u0000` inside a Shopify JSON import's strings isn't checked before the adapter writes, so that run ends `partial` with error code `DataError` (sanitized, HTTP 200, not a 500). The popular-products `category` query picks a stored report and never reaches SQL, so it needs nothing.

### DEC-286 · Amazon ranks surface (AMZ-1b): `GET /v1/amazon/ranks` serves every imported list at its newest day, ordered and paged list by list; its own page at `/amazon`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-04)** · Phase 3 task AMZ-1b (p3-04); builds on DEC-279 (f); DEC-040, DEC-166, DEC-238, DEC-267 (c), DEC-272, DEC-284; AGENTS.md §3, §8; vault Data/Sources/Source - Amazon, Features/Methodology & Confidence; `docs/reports/p3-04.md`; no migration, dependency, settings flag or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) API.** `GET /v1/amazon/ranks` (operation `list_amazon_ranks`, its own router, signed-in only, no workspace scope: `amazon_ranks` is global market data, as Google popular's rows are). A *list* is one category × rank kind (`bsr`, `movers`). By default every list shows at its newest imported `observed_for`; a day imported twice shows its newest capture only. Filters: `category` (exact), `rank_kind`, `observed_for` (that day's lists instead of each list's newest). Rows are ordered by category, rank kind, rank, then ASIN and keyset-paged on that whole key (`limit` 1–500, default 200; the cursor is the last row's four values as JSON). So a rank is only ever next to ranks of its own list, and two categories' rank 1 rows both show, each in its own list. Each row carries `asin, title, category, rank_kind, rank, price_minor, currency, rating, review_count, product_id, as_of (= observed_for), captured_at, raw_payload_id`; `product_id` comes from `link_products()` on read (DEC-267 c). The page carries `lists` (every list, unfiltered, with its newest day, import time and row count) for the filters, `total`, `as_of` and `captured_at` of the shown lists, and `stale`. `/v1/amazon` joins the Q-1 provenance contract's covered prefixes (DEC-238): every object holding a number requires `as_of`.
  - **(b) Stale** = the shown lists' newest day is more than 14 days old (the Google popular threshold, DEC-272 d); never when `observed_for` is picked on purpose.
  - **(c) No source yet.** A missing `amazon` source row answers `source: null` with nothing else; the page says "Amazon: no source yet". With the row and no import, the page says "No Amazon import yet" and links Data Health › CSV import and the template download.
  - **(d) Page.** Route `/amazon`, nav item "Amazon ranks" in Research right after Google popular (`phase: 3, built: true`, DEC-284). One dense table, one `<tbody>` per list headed "Category › Best Sellers · as of <day> · N ranks"; rank, price, rating and reviews right-aligned and tabular, ASIN in mono, the title a link to the product page when `product_id` is set. The line above reads "Amazon · manual import, as of <day>" with the import time on hover (vault Methodology & Confidence), and every number's cell names the source, day and import time on hover. Category and list filters live in the URL (`?category=`, `?kind=`). States: loading skeleton, no import, error with retry, stale banner over the rows, no source yet, and a filtered empty ("No Amazon list matches").
- **Alternatives:** (a) one list at a time with a default list, like Google popular: the owner's Amazon files are small and several lists side by side is the useful view; the per-list grouping keeps ranks apart all the same. (a) keyset on rank alone: wrong as soon as two lists show, and two ASINs can share a rank in one list. (a) `workspace_id` scoping: the table has none (DEC-279 c). (b) stale on the newest day across every list: a fresh list would hide an old one the owner is looking at. (d) Amazon rows on the Product page Trends tab: p3-02 and p2-39 were changing that tab; a backlog row instead.
- **Why:** API tests on synthetic imports (`apps/api/tests/test_amazon_ranks.py`, Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`): each list at its newest day with an earlier day left out, both rank 1s shown under their own category, keyset pages of 2 rebuild the full order with no gap or repeat, the three filters, a UPC-12 row linking to a 13-digit barcode's product in both lists while an unlinked row stays, an empty table, a missing source row, a re-imported day showing its newest capture once, stale at 15 days and not at 14 or on a picked day. Six mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/p3-04.md`). Vitest for `groupRows()`; Playwright (`apps/web/e2e/amazon/`) for the five states, the filters in the URL, keyboard focus and Load more.

### DEC-291 · Profit's default supplier is Suppliers' Best margin: in-stock listings first, one shared `in_stock()`, the Discover view alike
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-289)** · Improvement backlog row imp-289 (p2-26 report backlog row 2, endorsed by P2-34); amends DEC-246 (f) and DEC-265's view; DEC-243, DEC-264 (b); vault Features/Product Page - Profit, Features/Product Page - Suppliers; `docs/reports/imp-289.md`; migration 0014 (the view only: no table, row or data change); no flag, dependency or API shape change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One predicate.** `pulso_core.landed_cost_loader.in_stock(available, stock)` is DEC-243's rule: not known to be out of stock, so `available is not False` and stock null or above 0. The Suppliers rows, the Overview's best supplier cost and the loader all call it. Nothing else holds a copy.
  - **(b) The default pick.** Without `supplier_listing_id`, `calculate()` reads each live supplier listing's latest snapshot (`captured_at`, then `id`, newest first, as the Suppliers tab reads it) and ranks the listings not known to be out of stock first. A listing with no snapshot yet, or one without stock fields, counts as in stock, as on Suppliers. Within that pool DEC-246 (f) is unchanged: `best()` over complete rows, else the fewest missing inputs, then the lowest id. So whenever Suppliers has a Best margin pick, Profit's default is that listing with the same answer.
  - **(c) Fallback.** Only when every listing is known out of stock does the same rule run over all of them, so the page still shows a number (the best out-of-stock margin, or the least incomplete row). While any listing is in stock, a complete out-of-stock one never wins over it, even when the in-stock one is incomplete (Suppliers then has no Best margin pick either). The response doesn't flag an out-of-stock default yet: a `ProfitCalc` field needs the Profit tab to show it and the typed e2e fixtures updated, so it's a backlog row.
  - **(d) An explicit supplier.** `supplier_listing_id` costs that listing whatever its stock, as before; Suppliers' rows depend on it.
  - **(e) The Discover view.** Migration 0014 replaces `workspace_product_economics` with 0010's text plus two edits, read from 0010's file: a lateral for the latest snapshot's `available IS FALSE OR stock <= 0`, and that flag as the pick's first sort key after "has a listing". A null flag (no snapshot, no stock fields) sorts as in stock. Downgrade restores 0010's view definition exactly. DEC-265's parity (view = loader = default calc) holds. The brief said no migration; the lead chose this view-only one over leaving Discover out of step.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (b) Filter in-stock rows to complete ones only, then fall back to every listing: Profit's default would then cost a known out-of-stock supplier while an in-stock one exists, which is what this row removes.
  - (c) No answer when nothing is in stock: the page would go blank for a product whose suppliers are all sold out, where the best out-of-stock margin is still the useful number.
  - (e) Loader only, with the view left for later: Discover's best margin would disagree with Profit's default whenever the best listing is out of stock, and the DEC-265 parity check in `test_profit.py` fails on any such fixture. A full copy of the view SQL in 0014: 150 duplicated lines where the change is two.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_product_suppliers.py` (three tests), `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py` (one test) and `packages/core/tests/test_landed_cost_vectors.py` (one test: the view and `calculate()` held equal with an out-of-stock fixture), on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`. The cheapest complete listing sold out: before the fix the default calc costs it (listing 2 where Best margin is 4); after the fix the default equals Suppliers' Best margin row's `cost` exactly, and Discover's best margin names the same listing. All out of stock: the best out-of-stock listing, in the loader and the view. An in-stock incomplete listing, or one with no snapshot, beats a complete sold-out one. An explicit sold-out listing is costed. Seven loader mutations and five view mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/imp-289.md`).
- **Not here:** an "out of stock" flag on `ProfitCalc` and the Profit tab (backlog row); variant-level stock (the default pick has no variant, and Suppliers reads the listing's snapshot too).

### DEC-290 · A segmented control's pressed option carries a 2 px brand bar; Profit drops its recompute dim; light chart slots clear 3:1
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-313)** · p2-39 findings B1, B2, B4 (vault Agents/Improvement Backlog imp-313, imp-314, imp-316); WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3, 1.4.11; vault Design/Color & Tokens, Features/Product Page - Profit; `docs/reports/imp-313.md`; no API, migration or dependency change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Pressed mark.** The pressed option of every segmented control is painted by one utility, `segment-thumb` in `globals.css`: the card thumb as before plus a 2 px bar in the component token `--segment-mark` (= `--brand`), inset 0.5 rem from each side along the thumb's bottom. The bar is drawn on `::before` because `hit-area` owns `::after`. Five controls use it: Discover's view toggle, Trends' range, Settings' duty selector, Profit's units per order (its track moves from `background` to `secondary`, like the others; its pressed fill was 1.14 dark / 1.11 light) and the UI gallery sample. `contrast.test.ts` checks `--segment-mark` on `card` and `secondary`: 11.25 / 10.53 dark, 3.96 / 3.56 light. The card thumb alone was 1.07:1 dark and 1.11:1 light on the track.
  - **(b) No recompute dim.** Profit keeps the last answer at full opacity while a recompute is out. The 2 px brand hairline is the visual cue, and `aria-busy` on the results and breakdown is the cue for assistive tech. The 70% dim put `--subtle-foreground` at 3.43 / 2.97 and `--muted-foreground` at 4.47 / 3.60.
  - **(c) Light chart slots.** `--viz-orange-l` `#EB6834` → `#E2612C`, `--viz-aqua-l` `#1BAF7A` → `#0D9488`, `--viz-yellow-l` `#EDA100` → `#A88400` and `--viz-magenta-l` `#E87BA4` → `#CC4C8F`. On white they measure 3.51, 3.74, 3.52 and 4.20, up from 3.20, 2.82, 2.17 and 2.69. On `secondary`, where Trends' pressed pane toggle fills its series swatch, every light slot measures 3.16 or more. Orange was 2.88 there. Blue (4.42) and the dark slots (4.68 to 6.01 on the card) are unchanged. Aqua leans teal and magenta leans violet, which keeps them apart from `--positive` (OKLab ΔE 12.2) and `--negative` (ΔE 14.7). The slots stay apart from each other too: adjacent ΔE is 17.5 for normal vision and 12.0 for protan and deutan (Machado 2009), against 19.6 and 9.1 before. `contrast.test.ts` checks all five slots on `card`, `background` and `secondary` in both themes.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a1) An inset ring around the pressed thumb. In `--brand` it reads as the focus ring. A grey that clears 3:1 on `secondary` in both themes is the `--subtle-foreground` pair (gray-400 / gray-500), a text token used as a border.
  - (a2) A new shared `<SegmentedControl>` component. The five controls use three mechanisms (`aria-pressed` buttons with a `layoutId` thumb, a radio fieldset, a Radix ToggleGroup), so one utility is the smaller change.
  - (b1) Dimming only the value cells. The values are `--foreground`, `--positive` and `--negative`. At 70%, `--positive` measures 3.20 and `--negative` 3.85 in light (`--negative` 3.81 in dark): below AA for the breakdown's 13 px values.
  - (c1) Leaving the light palette and adding a "View as table" disclosure. It is still optional and is left as a backlog row. The lines themselves now clear 3:1.
- **Why:** `src/styles/contrast.test.ts` has 17 new UI pairs per theme (34 tests). On the old tokens 14 of them fail: `--segment-mark` doesn't exist, light series 3 to 5 are below 3:1 on every surface, and light series 2 is below 3:1 on `secondary`. `e2e/imp-313/contrast-states.spec.ts` checks that the pressed thumb paints the bar in the token's colour at 2 px on Discover, Settings and Profit units, and that only the pressed option has it. In dark and light it checks that Profit's busy regions stay at opacity 1 and that every text run clears AA mid-recompute. That test fails on the old `profit-tab.tsx`, which gives `0.7`.

### DEC-292 · Weekly Trends set (KW-2): approved seeds, then every workspace's confirmed keywords, cut at the monthly limit // 5, behind `TRENDS_CONFIRMED_KEYWORDS`; the Trends tab counts with the same function
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-06)** · Phase 3 task KW-2; DEC-011, DEC-065, DEC-073, DEC-099, DEC-154, DEC-157, DEC-263, DEC-281; SPEC §5 Signals, §6.4 Trends; vault Phases/Phase 3 - Signals Expansion, Features/Product Page - Trends, Data/Sources/Source - Google Trends; no migration, no dependency, no source request, no schedule change; `google_trends` stays disabled
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) The set.** `google_trends.weekly_set(session, settings)` builds it, and `plan()` only turns it into targets.
    - The approved seeds come first, read by `approved_targets` exactly as before (DEC-065, DEC-073). A seed with a comma still fails the plan.
    - With the setting on, every workspace's confirmed keywords follow (DEC-281 a), deduplicated on a normal form: casefolded, with spacing collapsed. The first spelling wins, so a seed keeps its spelling and its place.
    - **Confirmed order:** most workspaces confirming, then the earliest confirmation (the oldest `decided_at` among the current confirmed decisions), then A to Z. In v1 there is one workspace, so the set fills first-come and a tracked keyword keeps its slot when another is confirmed.
    - **Which decision counts:** a workspace's newest one per keyword across a product and the products merged into it (DEC-263), the way the Trends tab reads them. A later rejection on the survivor therefore wins over an older confirmation on the merged product.
    - Plural folding (`lamps` is `lamp`) stays in the API's `key()`. Two plural twins decided separately on two products before a merge both count here, as two searches. The code names this limit.
  - **(b) The cap.** The weekly cap is `TRENDS_MONTHLY_SEARCH_LIMIT // 5` (50 at 250), because a UTC month has at most five Mondays. Seeds take slots first.
    - A keyword past the cap, or a confirmed keyword holding a comma, is left out of that week's plan. It gets no target row, no queue and no retry.
    - With the setting on, seeds past the cap are cut too, so `len(set) × 5 ≤ limit` holds at every limit, 0 included.
    - The cap is a projection guard only. The monthly `reserve` (DEC-099) and the hourly `reserve_hourly` (DEC-154) still gate every attempt, retries included (DEC-157), and they are unchanged.
  - **(c) The gate.** `TRENDS_CONFIRMED_KEYWORDS` is a bool, default off, documented in `.env.example`. Off, `plan()` returns exactly the seed-only plan, uncapped, as before KW-2. The owner turns it on; agents never write an `.env`.
  - **(d) API.**
    - Each `Keyword` gains `tracked` (true or false for a confirmed keyword, null otherwise) and `untracked_reason`: `budget` (past the cap), `disabled` (setting off) or `comma`.
    - `KeywordList` gains `trends: TrendsBudget {enabled, weekly_used, weekly_cap, monthly_limit, as_of}`. `as_of` is when it was counted, for the provenance contract.
    - Both are computed from `weekly_set`, so the page and the Monday job can't disagree.
    - `weekly_used` counts every workspace's keywords as a number only, never the keywords themselves, because the SerpApi budget is shared.
  - **(e) UI.**
    - **Cost line:** under the add field, and it is the accessible description of the field and of every Confirm. It has three states:
      - room: "Each tracked keyword is 1 SerpApi search a week, about 4–5 of the 250 a month. N of 50 weekly slots used.";
      - full: "50 of 50 weekly slots used. A keyword you confirm now is saved but not tracked until a slot frees.";
      - off: "Trends tracking is off. Confirmed keywords are saved, but nothing is fetched for them."
    - **Chip marker:** a filled neutral dot when tracked, a hollow ring when not. The reason shows on hover and is the marker's accessible name. The Confirmed heading explains the dot once ("· ● N tracked").
    - **While a save is in flight:** a keyword shows no marker until the API answers.
    - **Clicks:** confirming is still one click on the tab (two from the product page). Adding is type plus Enter.
- **Alternatives:**
  - A cap of `limit // 4` (62 at 250): a five-Monday month would need 310, and the monthly reservation would cut the last Monday partway through.
  - Budget left this month instead of a fixed cap: the set would change week to week, so a keyword's Tracked flag would flicker and the page couldn't promise anything before the click.
  - Never capping seeds: at limit 0, or with more seeds than the cap, the five-Monday projection breaks.
  - Rotating overflow keywords week to week: one `today 3-m` fetch returns 90 daily points, so fetching every other week would cover more keywords for the same cost. The brief says never queued and never retried, so this is a backlog idea (p3-06-b2).
  - Ordering by the newest confirmation: every new confirmation would push an older tracked keyword out.
  - Folding plurals in the plan: `pulso_sources` can't import the API package, and copying its tokenizer would drift.
- **Why:**
  - `packages/sources/tests/google_trends/test_weekly_set.py` (9 cases, on SQLite and the migrated PostgreSQL schema) covers:
    - setting off gives exactly today's seed plan, uncapped;
    - seeds come first; confirmed keywords are deduplicated across two workspaces and against a seed in another case and spacing; the order is the same on every run; a rejected keyword and a comma keyword are left out;
    - a rejection wins across a merge;
    - the 51st keyword is left out at 250;
    - `weekly × 5 ≤ limit` at 0, 1, 49, 50 and 250.
  - `apps/api/tests/test_product_keywords.py::test_tracked_is_exactly_the_weekly_plan` (PostgreSQL) checks that every confirmed keyword's `tracked` equals its membership in `plan()` for the same rows, with the setting on and off. It also checks the budget block.
  - Eight mutations each fail a test: no merge read, no cap, rejected keywords joining, no workspace count, the setting ignored, seeds after the confirmed keywords, comma keywords kept, and the router always reporting tracked.
  - Playwright (`keywords.spec.ts`, 13) covers the cost line as the Confirm's description, the full set and setting-off copy, and the Tracked marker.
- **Not here:**
  - interest, slope and sparkline columns (p3-02-b3);
  - a `today 5-y` backfill per tracked keyword;
  - the series `search` reason in `product_series.py`, which still says confirmed keywords join the weekly fetch "in a later step" (outside this task's files; report follow-up);
  - turning the setting on, which is the owner's call.

### DEC-293 · Amazon ranks on the Product page (AMZ-1c): `GET /v1/products/{id}/amazon-ranks` gives one row per list the product is ranked in, at its newest ranked day; a section on the Trends tab
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-07)** · Phase 3 task AMZ-1c (p3-07); builds on DEC-286 and DEC-279; DEC-040, DEC-238, DEC-267 (c); AGENTS.md §3, §8; vault Data/Sources/Source - Amazon, Features/Product Page - Trends; `docs/reports/p3-07.md`; no migration, dependency, settings flag or network
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) API.** `GET /v1/products/{id}/amazon-ranks` (operation `list_product_amazon_ranks`) is scoped exactly like `/v1/amazon/ranks`: signed-in, no workspace filter (global market data). It lives in the Amazon router and shares its helpers: `newest_captures()` (each list and day's newest capture), `rows_of()`, `rank_row()` and `is_stale()` (over 14 days). It reads the rows of every day's newest capture that carry GTINs and links them with `link_products()`. Per list (category × rank kind) it keeps the product's newest ranked day, and the best rank when two ASINs share the product's GTIN. Each row is an `AmazonRankRow`. The answer also carries `source`/`source_name` (null without the source row), `imported` (whether any list was imported), the rows' newest `as_of` and `captured_at`, and `stale`. Unknown id 404, merged id 409 (`live_product`, as the other product sub-resources do).
  - **(b) A list the product dropped out of** shows its last ranked day, with that day on the row, and is never presented as current. A superseded capture (a corrected file for the same day) never shows.
  - **(c) No source yet** covers both a missing source row ("Amazon: no source yet") and nothing imported ("No Amazon import yet"), with the copy `/amazon` uses. The source's `enabled` flag isn't read: Amazon is CSV-only, so live collection stays off by design (DEC-279).
  - **(d) UI.** An "Amazon ranks" section after Search keywords on the Trends tab (its own `amazon.tsx`, one mount line). The label reads "Amazon · manual import, as of <day>", with the import time on hover. A compact table has one row per list (List, Rank, Day); every rank's hover names the source, day, import time and ASIN. "Every Amazon list" links to `/amazon`. States: loading skeleton, error with Retry, stale line on refetch failure, a stale banner over the rows, not ranked (links CSV import and names the `gtins` column), no import, no source row.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Only each list's newest day, as `/amazon` defaults: a product that dropped one day out of a list would vanish with no trace; the brief asks for the newest day among the product's own rows.
  - (a) Every linking ASIN per list: two rows for one product in one list read as two products.
  - (a) A separate small router: the helpers live in the Amazon router, and a second module would import them back.
  - (b) Carrying the last rank forward to the list's newest day: that fabricates an observation (AGENTS.md §4).
  - (d) The section between the chart and keywords: most products have no Amazon row, so a not-ranked note there would push the keyword actions down.
- **Why:** API tests (`apps/api/tests/test_amazon_ranks.py`, 9 new, on Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`) cover:
  - a linked product in both of Home's lists;
  - newest ranked day per list, best rank across two ASINs, a dropped-out list dated, a superseded capture hidden;
  - an unlinked product, no import, a missing source row, unknown 404 and merged 409;
  - stale at 15 days and not at 14;
  - a second workspace reading the identical answer, and 401 without a session.

  Five mutations each fail a test. Playwright (`apps/web/e2e/products/amazon.spec.ts`, 9) covers every state, the one-click link, keyboard focus and 375px.

### DEC-295 · `ProfitCalc.in_stock` flags a sold-out costed listing; Profit marks it beside the supplier
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-351)** · Improvement backlog row imp-351 (`docs/reports/imp-289.md` backlog row 1, DEC-291 c); DEC-243, DEC-264 (b), DEC-265; vault Features/Product Page - Profit; `docs/reports/imp-351.md`; additive API field (`pnpm gen:api`), no migration, flag, dependency or money change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) The field.** `ProfitCalc.in_stock` (required boolean) is the costed listing's `stocked()`, the value `calculate()` already reads to put in-stock listings first. `Calc` carries it, and the pool filters on it, so the flag and the pick can't read stock two ways. The default pick and an explicit `supplier_listing_id` alike: false on the default only when no listing is in stock (DEC-291 c); false on a chosen sold-out listing; Suppliers' row `cost` carries it too. With no supplier listing it is true (nothing is known to be out of stock, DEC-243's wording), and the tab shows the No supplier state anyway.
  - **(b) The view.** `workspace_product_economics` gets no column: that would be a migration. It already picks the same listing (DEC-265 parity, checked in the same test), so Discover agrees on which supplier, and the flag stays on the calc answer.
  - **(c) The marker.** On the Profit tab, the Scenario panel's "Costed from the … listing" line shows the shared `Pill` (negative tone, `CircleSlash`, the text "Out of stock") when `in_stock` is false, the same tokens and icon as the Suppliers tab's out-of-stock badge. The default's line adds "; none is in stock", so the pick reads as the fallback it is.
- **Alternatives:** (a) a nullable flag, null with no listing: one more state for a case the tab never renders. Recomputing `stocked()` in the router: a second read of the same snapshot that could drift from the pick. (c) A warning strip above the results: louder than a supplier fact needs, and the brief places it beside the supplier.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py` on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`: in-stock default true, all-out-of-stock default false (= the view's listing), explicit out-of-stock pick false, no listing true; the two-workspace test unchanged. `e2e/products/profit.spec.ts` shows the marker on an all-out-of-stock fixture and not on an in-stock one, and fails with the marker removed.

### DEC-289 · AliExpress on the Suppliers tab (AE-3): the user pastes the link, the answer lives in Redis per workspace product until its own 24 h end, a GET that never calls out and a POST that does, source 9 seeded off
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-05)** · implements DEC-037 and DEC-283; SPEC §4.2, §4.3 #4, §6.4 Suppliers; vault Features/Product Page - Suppliers, Data/Sources/Source - AliExpress; `docs/reports/p3-05.md`; migration 0015 (one source row), no dependency, no setting
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) The lookup key is pasted, never stored.** Pulso keeps no AliExpress product id (DEC-037; storing ids waits for AliExpress's written answer). On the Suppliers tab the user pastes the product's AliExpress link or number. The API accepts `http(s)` links on `aliexpress.com` or `aliexpress.us` (any subdomain) with the path `/item/<digits>.html`, taking the SKU from `skuId=` when present, or a bare number. ASCII digits only, as `lookup()` requires; anything else is a 422 `invalid_link`. The body field is `NoNulStr` (DEC-287), so a NUL is refused as a validation error before parsing.
  - **(b) Where the answer lives.** `lookup()` answers as DEC-283 c says. The router copies an `ok` or `not_found` answer to Redis key `pulso:aliexpress:v1:lookup:<workspace_id>:<product_id>` with an absolute expiry of `as_of` + 24 h (`EXAT`). So the rows show again for 24 h and vanish exactly when `lookup()`'s own entry does, even when the answer came from that cache hours later. The key is per workspace as well as per product, so one workspace never sees another's lookup. Nothing goes to Postgres; failures are not cached.
  - **(c) Two operations, not one with a query parameter.** `GET /v1/products/{id}/aliexpress` (`get_product_aliexpress`) reads only: it never calls AliExpress, so a refetch or tab focus can't spend the 500-a-day cap. `POST` with `{"link": …}` (`lookup_product_aliexpress`) runs `lookup()`. Both answer `AliExpressLookup` `{status, product, shipping, as_of, expires_at, retry_at}`, with `status` one of `empty` (no lookup in the last 24 h), `ok`, `not_found`, `disabled`, `not_configured`, `throttled` (with `retry_at`: `retry_not_before`, or the next 00:00 UTC once the day's cap is used) and `error` (POST only). Both send `Cache-Control: no-store`; the API middleware already sends it on every response, and the tests pin it on both. The response models are renamed copies (`AliExpressProduct`, `AliExpressSku`, `AliExpressShipping`), so the OpenAPI schema never names a bare `Product`. Each repeats the lookup's `as_of` as a required field, so every number keeps the Q-1 provenance contract (DEC-238).
  - **(d) GET follows `lookup()`'s gate order.** Flag off: `disabled`, even over a cached answer. A cached answer: shown until its expiry, even if the source row was turned off since. DEC-037 allows the 24 h cache, and `lookup()` already serves its cache before the row check. Otherwise, in order: keys missing → `not_configured`; row missing or disabled → `disabled`; `retry_not_before` ahead or the cap used → `throttled`; the collector guard fails → `disabled`; else `empty`.
  - **(e) Where live lookups may run: unchanged, behind the guard.** The real transport keeps DEC-283 e's collector-host guard (`COLLECTORS_LIVE=on` outside a linked worktree, plus the contact User-Agent). The API process normally doesn't pass it, so the honest default is that the API answers `disabled` (or `not_configured`) until the owner turns it on, on a host where the guard passes. That takes `ALIEXPRESS_ENABLED=on`, the three keys, the `aliexpress` row enabled, and an API process with `COLLECTORS_LIVE=on` on the collector host. The guard is not weakened, and nothing here changes it.
  - **(f) Source row.** Migration 0015 seeds source 9, `aliexpress`, type `official_api`, phase 3, disabled, for the shared block state. Its downgrade deletes the row; a disable event's foreign key refuses a lossy downgrade. Data Health lists the real row ("Disabled"), and its "No source yet · arrives in Phase 3" placeholder is gone.
  - **(g) UI.** An "AliExpress" section below the supplier table. Every number is a `Metric` whose name and provenance card carry "AliExpress · live · not stored", its `as_of` and "Raw payload: Not stored". Every row (table from 768 px) or card (below 768 px) carries the same visible badge. The rows stay out of landed cost and the picks: there is no listing row for the shared engine to cost, and no history. States: loading, empty, error with Retry, stale (a failed refresh keeps the rows inside their 24 h), off and not configured ("AliExpress is off: needs the owner's app approval", naming the Owner To-Dos item "Start the AliExpress applications" by its text and linking Data Health), paused with the retry time, a wrong link, and a lookup without an answer. At `expires_at` the client clears the rows first and then asks the API, so a failed refetch never leaves expired rows on screen.
  - **(h) §7.10 reading.** No AliExpress or Alibaba mark is in Pulso's name, app name or page URLs: the tab stays `/products/{id}?tab=suppliers`. The API resource path `/v1/products/{id}/aliexpress` (named by DEC-283 and the AE-3 brief) names the source factually, as `sources.key` does. It is not the app's name or a page URL. If the owner reads §7.10 more strictly, renaming it is one router line plus `pnpm gen:api`.
- **Alternatives:**
  - Storing the AliExpress id on the Pulso product: forbidden until AliExpress answers in writing (DEC-037).
  - DS image search (`aliexpress.ds.image.searchV2`) from the product's main image, the vault note's "Find on AliExpress": it needs no paste, but it is a new method to docs-check first. It also returns candidates to choose from, not one product, and spends calls per view. Backlog.
  - One endpoint with `?link=`: a GET that may call AliExpress would spend the daily cap on every refetch.
  - A per-product key without the workspace: it leaks one workspace's lookup to another.
  - `ex` = 24 h from the copy: an answer served from `lookup()`'s cache would outlive its own 24 h.
  - Hiding cached rows as soon as the row is disabled: stricter than DEC-037 and unlike `lookup()`. The flag still hides everything at once.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_product_aliexpress.py` has 21 runs on Postgres `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` with a fake cache and gateway and no network. They cover every status, no-store on GET and POST, link parsing, workspace isolation, expiry (the AE-3 verify), and zero row changes in every migrated table with no S3 client. `apps/web/e2e/products/aliexpress.spec.ts` has 5 Playwright tests on fixtures: the badge on all 11 numbers and every row, the states, keyboard use, and the rows disappearing at expiry. Ten mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/p3-05.md`).
- **Unverified until the first owner-run lookup:** whether `aliexpress.us` item numbers are the DS `product_id`, and whether shared links carry `skuId=`. Without it, the answer has no US shipping.
- **Not here:** image search, the R-17 CSV fallback, the DS token refresh, and hiding Data Health's "Run now" for sources without a collector plan. A manual run there fails safely with "adapter unavailable" and sends nothing.

### DEC-299 · The Overview's landed margin is Discover's best margin: one shared economics read for the caller's workspace
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-280)** · Improvement backlog row imp-280 (p2-27 report backlog row 2); DEC-265, DEC-291, DEC-243, DEC-040; vault Features/Product Page - Overview, Features/Discover; `docs/reports/imp-280.md`; no migration, flag or dependency; API shape: `ProductOverview.landed_margin_minor` goes from `NoSource` to `Margin | Incomplete`
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One read.** `routers/products.py` `best_margins(session, workspace_id, product_ids)` selects the caller's `workspace_product_economics` rows (migration 0014: the default scenario, in-stock suppliers first). Discover's page and the Overview both call it and turn the row into a stat with the same `margin_stat()`. So for the same product and workspace the Overview's `landed_margin_minor` equals Discover's `best_margin_minor` field for field: value, `margin_bps`, `supplier_listing_id`, the oldest input's source, as-of and raw payload, and `stale`; or the same `Incomplete` reason and `missing[]`. No new money math.
  - **(b) Workspace-scoped.** The overview endpoint now takes the principal. Its other numbers stay global market data; only the margin reads the workspace (its Settings: fees, duty). A workspace with no settings row reads "No workspace settings yet." with `missing: []`.
  - **(c) The card.** Complete: the margin in dollars, then "39.6% margin · before ads", then the oldest input's source and age (amber with "stale" when any input is stale). Withheld (R-16): "Duty not set" in amber when duty is one of the gaps, "No source yet" otherwise. The caption names what is missing ("Needs a supplier price and a freight quote."), and the provenance card repeats it. These are the same words as Discover's cell. A loss reads with U+2212.
- **Alternatives:**
  - The best supplier on the Overview chosen by the Profit tab's selected supplier, as the vault table says ("selected or best"): the Overview has no supplier selection, so best is the only answer. A selection would need shared page state, which this task doesn't add.
  - A second query in the overview handler: two copies of the read could drift, and the brief asks for Discover's read.
  - The Profit tab's `calculate()` for one product: it gives the same answer (DEC-265 parity) but does a full Python costing per view, where the view row is one query.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_profit.py::test_the_overviews_landed_margin_is_discovers_best_margin` checks that the Overview equals Discover for a complete margin, a missing freight quote and no supplier pick. `test_the_overviews_landed_margin_reads_only_the_callers_workspace` checks one product under three workspaces: complete, duty not set, and no settings. Both run on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`. Playwright (`overview.spec.ts`) covers the margin, its as-of and raw payload on hover, "Duty not set" in amber, and the no-supplier gaps.
- **Not here:** a next action on a withheld margin (a Settings link for duty, or the Profit tab), which the Overview note lists as still to come for every card (report backlog row).

### DEC-298 · Trends Keywords after KW-2: the search reason names the Monday fetch; no tracked legend while tracking is off; the relevance number explains itself in a tooltip
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-358)** · Improvement backlog rows imp-358, imp-355, imp-343 (p3-06 report b4 and b1, p3-02 report b2); DEC-245, DEC-281, DEC-292; vault Features/Product Page - Trends, Design/Accessibility; `docs/reports/imp-358.md`; copy and render only: no API shape, migration, dependency or flag change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Series reason.** The second sentence of the `search` NoSource reason is now "Tracked keywords are fetched from Google Trends each Monday when tracking is on." The first sentence stays, because no capture is read for the product's keywords yet.
  - **(b) Legend.** The Confirmed heading's "· ● N tracked" shows only when `trends.enabled` is true and at least one keyword is confirmed. With tracking off, the heading reads "Confirmed · N". The chips keep their hollow markers and their names ("Not tracked: tracking is off").
  - **(c) Relevance number.** At every width the number is a `TapTooltip` button instead of a `title`. It opens on hover, tap and keyboard focus, as `Metric` does. The tooltip has two lines: "Relevance 0.50: how many titles use the phrase and its words", then "Listing titles as of …". Below `sm` the helper drops its explanation and reads "Titles as of …" on one line. Each suggested or rejected row gains one tab stop (number, then Confirm, then Reject). After a decision, focus goes to the row's first `data-decide` button, never to the number.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (b1) Keeping the legend at "0 tracked": the off line already says nothing is fetched (styleseed −2 in p3-06).
  - (c1) A tooltip on phones only, by rendering the number twice and hiding one copy per breakpoint: the DOM would be duplicated and every locator would match twice, to save one desktop tab stop per row.
  - (c2) One info button beside the phone helper: the explanation would sit away from the number it explains.
- **Why:** In `keywords.spec.ts`, the off case asserts the exact heading "Confirmed · 2", and the KW-2 case still asserts "Confirmed · 1 · 1 tracked" with tracking on. A new 375 px touch case asserts that the helper is less than 1.5 line-heights tall (it was 28 px, two 14 px lines) and that the number's tooltip opens on a tap, closes on a tap elsewhere, and opens again on keyboard focus (Tab from Add). The keyboard walk case still passes, so a decision focuses the next Confirm. `test_product_series.py::test_series_nothing_backs_say_why` asserts the new reason. Each test failed before the change.
- **Not here:** the confirmed chips' hover and the rejected "Typed in" hover are still `title` text, which isn't tap or keyboard reachable (backlog row in the report).

### DEC-297 · Amazon ranks "Load more" reads the imports page 1 read (the cursor carries the newest import time); an approved comma seed fails the whole Trends seed list closed
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-341)** · Improvement backlog row imp-341 (XR-48, XR-50 from P3-08); DEC-286, DEC-011, DEC-292; `docs/reports/imp-341.md`; API cursor shape only (`pnpm gen:api` adds `AmazonList.stale`), no migration, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) XR-50.** `GET /v1/amazon/ranks`'s cursor gains a fifth item: the newest `captured_at` among the imports page 1 read. Later pages group only rows captured up to it, so a file imported between pages never joins the lists page 1 started, and the page needs no change. A new first page (reload, filter change) reads every import. Cursors of the old four-item shape answer 422 `invalid_cursor`; the web only keeps cursors for one list's pages.
  - **(b) XR-48.** `validate_seed_entries` refuses a keyword holding a comma, so a seed preview or approval with one fails where the owner sees it. A seed list already stored with one now fails validation in `approved_targets`, which fails closed as for any invalid entry: no Trends seeds are planned (before, `plan()` raised). The keyword API also answers `trends.enabled: false` if `weekly_set` ever raises, instead of a 500.
- **Alternatives:** (a) a 409 when page 1's captures changed: smaller in the API, but the page would then need a restart path for Load more (a web change outside this task), and the owner would lose the list being read. Carrying every shown capture (`raw_payload_id`, `parser_version`) in the cursor: one pair per list, so the cursor grows with the lists. (b) Skipping only the comma seed: a partial approved list the owner never approved in that form.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_amazon_ranks.py::test_load_more_keeps_the_imports_page_one_showed` (PostgreSQL) imports a new day between page 1 and page 2 and gets page 1's day on both; it fails on the old router (page 2 read the new day). `infra/scripts/tests/test_seeds.py` refuses `"lamp, shade"`; `apps/api/tests/test_product_keywords.py::test_a_comma_seed_never_fails_the_keyword_api` reads 200 with a stored comma seed and with `weekly_set` raising (500 before); `packages/sources/tests/google_trends/test_trends.py` now expects an empty plan for a stored comma seed.

### DEC-302 · Phase 3 states (Q-2): Store Spy and the Sellers tab read "no source yet" while the store crawler stays off; a CSV-only source reads "No import yet" in Data Health
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p3-10)** · Phase 3 task Q-2 (p3-10); DEC-040, DEC-261, DEC-267 (f), DEC-279, DEC-284; AGENTS.md §8; vault Design/UI States, Phases/Phase 3 - Signals Expansion; `docs/reports/p3-10.md`; web only: no API change, migration, dependency, flag or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Store Spy has no source.** `NavItem.noSource` holds the reason a page has no source. It keeps the route unbuilt whatever the phase: `isBuilt()` is false while it is set, so ending Phase 3 can't turn `/stores` into a 404 or a link to nothing (DEC-284's alternative (a)). Store Spy carries DEC-261's reason, and `/stores/{id}` inherits it. The catch-all page shows "Store Spy: no source yet" (or "Store: no source yet") with that reason and one action, "Import a CSV in Data Health". It shows no phase and no phase bar. The sidebar badge and rail tooltip read "No source yet", as does the palette hint, never "P3" or "arrives in Phase 3". A blocked store and a password store (the Q-2 brief's two store states) can't occur, because nothing ever fetches a store. They read as this state, never as an error or a 0.
  - **(b) Sellers tab with the crawler off.** `crawler.status = "disabled"` is DEC-261's off-for-good, not a pause. With no seller ever seen (`as_of` null), one "Sellers: no source yet" state replaces the paused banner, the summary strip's zeros and the "tracked stores" empty state. Its action is "Import a CSV in Data Health". With imported sellers, the amber "Store crawler paused" banner becomes a neutral line, "Store crawler off … sellers come only from your CSV imports", with the as-of time. `auto_disabled` (a block) keeps its warning.
  - **(c) CSV-only sources in Data Health.** A source of type `csv` (Amazon, Google popular) keeps its row disabled as its flag (DEC-267 f, DEC-279). Its state comes from its imports: "No import yet" (new `no_import` state, muted) until the first import run, then by that run (Healthy, Partial, Failing). It never reads "Disabled · Disabled by the owner. Nothing is collected." `no_import` is settled before the gap check, so a CSV source never makes the attention count a floor.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Mark Store Spy `built: true` and build a page: an empty Store Spy page is a feature, out of Q-2's scope. Keep the coming-soon page: it promises a Phase 3 build that DEC-261 cancelled, and it 404s at the Phase 4 bump. Hide Store Spy from the nav: SPEC §10 lists the route, and the palette and breadcrumbs would lose it.
  - (b) A server-side `crawler.status = "off"`: an API change for words the web app can pick from the existing field.
  - (c) Enable the CSV sources' rows: the row is the flag (DEC-267 f), and enabling one would offer Run now on a source with no collector.
- **Why:** On `origin/main` `fe15473`, `/stores` read "Phase 3, Signals expansion, is under way and builds this page". Every product's Sellers tab showed "Store crawler paused" in amber over "0 active", because the crawler row is disabled for good. Data Health listed Amazon as "Disabled" while `/amazon` says to import a file there. Proof: `apps/web/e2e/p3-10/states.ts` (5 cells at 1440 and 375, plus the sidebar and palette test), Vitest in `src/lib/nav.test.ts` and `src/components/data-health/model.test.ts`, and the §8 browser checks on 11 Phase 3 screens (`e2e/p3-10/phase-end.spec.ts`).

### DEC-303 · Data Health copy: a Load more Retry names its list; the API-down page has one action; the Sources row's retry deadline carries a relative hint
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-112)** · Improvement backlog rows imp-112, imp-118, imp-120 (`docs/reports/imp-075.md`, `imp-090.md`, `imp-094.md`); DEC-166, DEC-184, DEC-220; vault Features/Data Health; `docs/reports/imp-112.md`; web copy and UI only, no API, schema or dependency change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Named footer Retry.** `LoadMore` takes an optional `retryLabel`. The Data Health footers say "Retry loading older gaps", "Retry loading targets" and "Retry loading older runs". Footers outside Data Health (Google popular, product context rail, Sellers, Suppliers) keep the default "Retry" and are left to their owners. The Open gaps heading drops "loaded"; only the footer note says it.
  - **(b) One action while the API is down.** The header Refresh is hidden on the API-down page that replaces the panels, so the card's Retry is the only control that runs the refresh. While that Retry's refetch runs, the page is in its loading state and the header shows "Refreshing…", as on first load. The stale page line ("Couldn't refresh Data Health", data still shown) keeps the header Refresh: there it is the only progress cue for a press of the line's Retry.
  - **(c) Relative deadline hint.** The Sources row and phone card read "Retry after <UTC> (in 1h)", using the dialog's `formatAgo` against the page's data time (`now`, DEC-184), so it holds still until the next refetch. A passed deadline reads "(1h ago)", so it shows as passed.
- **Alternatives:** (a) Renaming every `LoadMore` caller: those footers are outside this task. Deriving the label from `errorTitle`: "Couldn't load more targets" would give "Retry loading more targets", not the backlog's wording. (b) One verb on both controls: two buttons with the same name doing the same thing is still a duplicate for screen readers. Keeping the header button invisible to reserve its space: on a phone that leaves an empty row above the error card. (c) A hint only before the deadline, as the dialog shows: a passed deadline would again need mental arithmetic against "As of".
- **Why:** these are Playwright assertions, each RED before the change. `load-more.spec.ts`: the named retries, the heading without "loaded", and one "loaded" element. `data-health.spec.ts`: on the API-down page, no Refresh button and exactly one Retry. `retry-deadline.spec.ts`: "(in 1h)" painted unclipped at 1440 and 375; unchanged two minutes later with no refetch; "(in 58m)" after Refresh; "(1h ago)" past the deadline.

### DEC-307 · Lifecycle Stage v0 (SC-4): a pure `lifecycle_stage()` over the primary family's rows at t and t−7, ordered rules on ±20% growth, no stage on a gap or a short history, thresholds versioned as `lifecycle_v0-<hash8>`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p4-02)** · Phase 4 task SC-4; SPEC §7; DEC-040, DEC-261, DEC-275; vault Intelligence/Lifecycle Stage, Intelligence/Velocity & Acceleration, Intelligence/Score Definitions §2 and §5; `docs/reports/p4-02.md`; no migration, table, endpoint, UI, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Function.** `pulso_ml.scores.lifecycle.lifecycle_stage()` is pure. It takes the primary demand family's feature rows (DEC-275) at t and t−7: `v7`, `v7_prev`, `g7`, `g7_prev` (acceleration = `g7 − g7_prev`), plus `days_observed` (the family's days up to t, t included; 0 means no family), `has_gap` (either row's flag), `age_days` (since the source's launch date, None when unknown) and `delisted_all`. It returns `Stage(name, reason)`: a stage and the rule that fired, or `None` and why.
  - **(b) No stage, never a guessed one**, checked in this order:
    - `no source`: no demand family, delisted or not (Score Definitions §5).
    - `dead · delisted` comes next: it's an observed fact, not a trend.
    - `insufficient history`: fewer than 15 days (G7(t) reads a covered t−14). Checked before the gap because DEC-275 also sets `has_gap` when V7 is NULL from a short history.
    - `gap`: either row's gap flag, even when every value is present. Nothing is filled in.
    - `insufficient history` again when V7(t), V7(t−7) or G7(t) is NULL without a gap (a stable listing set still too new).
  - **(c) Rules, first match wins.** These are the vault's v0 thresholds, all working guesses to tune once two weeks of data exist:
    - **dead · no sales:** at most 1 unit in each of the two weeks.
    - **declining · two weeks down:** G7 ≤ ln 0.8 (−20%) at both t and t−7.
    - **declining · sharp drop:** G7 ≤ 2 ln 0.8 (−36%) at t.
    - **peaking · braking:** G7 ≥ ln 1.2 (+20%) and acceleration ≤ −0.25.
    - **peaking · plateau:** G7 < ln 1.2.
    - **emerging · new and growing:** +20% or more, launched 28 days ago or less, and at least 3 units.
    - **rising · growing:** +20% or more otherwise.
    - Without G7(t−7), the first 21 days, "two weeks down" and "braking" can't hold. Acceleration stays unknown and is never guessed.
  - **(d) Version.** The thresholds live in a frozen `Config` with `revision = 0`. `VERSION = lifecycle_v{revision}-{sha256(sorted-key JSON)[:8]}` is now `lifecycle_v0-7674a25d`, the format in vault Score Definitions §2. Any threshold change is a new version.
- **Alternatives:**
  - **Staging through a gap that V7 tolerates** (up to 3 days, DEC-275 d). That V7 is exact, not filled in. But a stage is a judgement that feeds stage alerts, the brief asks for none on a gap, and Discover still shows the V7 with its gap marker. Revisit when tuning.
  - **Age from `days_observed`:** for the first 28 days of collection, every growing product would read as emerging.
  - **The vault's rule 3b ("stalled after a run") as its own rule:** it needs the stored stage history (the scores table, p4-01), and it gives the same stage as the plateau rule. Only the label differs.
  - **Requiring G7(t−7)** (22 days of history): that holds every stage back another week. The vault already allows a NULL acceleration.
  - **Taking `Demand` rows:** that ties the function to the writer's row type. Plain values keep the tests one table.
- **Why:** `packages/ml/tests/test_lifecycle.py` has 42 pure cases:
  - every threshold from both sides (exact binary steps, so 0.5 − 0.75 lands on −0.25 exactly);
  - zero velocity (dead; declining after sales);
  - braking at high velocity (V7 120 against 80, previous growth 0.7);
  - a gap with every value present;
  - 14 and 15 days of history;
  - all-missing inputs;
  - a version change for each `Config` field;
  - 16 one-line mutations (each `<`/`≤` flipped, each guard dropped, `and`↔`or`), each of which fails a test (`docs/reports/p4-02.md`).
- **Not here:**
  - Hysteresis (two nights before a change shows) and storing the stage with its explanation: the scores writer (SC-6), on p4-01's table.
  - Coverage in the explanation's confidence: Score Definitions §6.
  - SPEC §7's own threshold text, and `/methodology` (MT-1).

### DEC-300 · Discover `store` and `supplier` filters: a vendor's live listings, named on the page for their chips; an unknown id lists nothing; the palette's vendor rows open them
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-242)** · Improvement backlog row imp-242 (p2-16 report backlog row 1); amends DEC-252's "a store or supplier opens its top product"; DEC-239, DEC-261; vault Features/Discover, Features/Command Palette & Search; `docs/reports/imp-242.md`; no migration, dependency, flag or new endpoint
- **Decision:**
