  - **(a) Filters.** `GET /v1/products` takes `store` and `supplier` (a `stores.id` / `suppliers.id`, 1 to 2^63 − 1, else 422). A product matches when it has a live (not delisted) listing at that store or from that supplier: the same `EXISTS` the `source` filter uses, now one shared shape for all three. They AND with each other and every other filter, and keep the keyset cursor.
  - **(b) Named on the page.** `DiscoverPage` gains `store` and `supplier`, each `{id, name}` (a store's domain, a supplier's name) or null. The chip reads its name from the page it already loads, so there is no lookup endpoint and nothing in the URL but the id.
  - **(c) Unknown id.** An id that names no store or supplier answers 200 with no items and a null echo, as an unknown `category` or `source` already does. The chip then reads "#id, not found" and the filtered empty state offers to remove it.
  - **(d) The palette.** A store or supplier row opens `/discover?store=<id>` or `?supplier=<id>`: Enter, or 1 click or tap once the row shows. The footer reads "Show products". A row whose vendor has no live product (`top_product_id` null) stays disabled with "No products yet", sorted last: opening an empty list would be a dead end.
  - **(e) Chips.** Only the store and supplier filters show as chips, since they have no control. The others keep their visible controls. The chips sit in the toolbar's wrap under the filters at every width, outside the phone's filter sheet, and count in "Filters (n)". "Clear filters" clears them too. A removal moves focus to the other chip, else to the page's h1 (as after a navigation). They leave with Motion pattern 5. Layout reflows only when a chip leaves, not when a name loads, and reduced motion keeps the fade without the scale.
  - **(f) No index.** On p2-16's realistic catalog (10,000 products, 30,000 listings, 2,000 stores, 500 suppliers, VACUUM ANALYZE), the planner drives both filters from the existing `ix_listings_store_id` / `ix_listings_supplier_id` (bitmap scan, a semi-join, then `pk_products`). Feed-query execution is 0.19 ms for a store and 0.56 ms for a supplier, against 158 ms unfiltered.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (b) The name in the URL from the palette: a shared or edited link could show the wrong name. A `GET /v1/stores/{id}`: Store Spy's job (Phase 3), and a second request for one string.
  - (c) A 404 problem: a stale link would show "Couldn't load products" instead of a removable filter. It would also break the category and source precedent.
  - (d) Enable the empty rows too: one click to an empty state the row already predicts.
  - (e) Chips for every active filter: duplicates controls that are already on screen. That is the Screener's pattern (Phase 4).
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_products.py`, three new tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`:
  - match, one product per page so the cursor is exercised, with AND across filters;
  - a delisted listing and a merged product don't match;
  - no match and an unknown id each give an empty page; bad ids answer 422;
  - global market data: two workspaces get identical pages. Margins stay per workspace, as `test_discover_best_margin_reads_only_the_callers_workspace` already checks.

  Playwright `e2e/discover/vendor-filters.spec.ts` (6 tests) covers the palette row reaching filtered Discover in 1 click, the chip removing the filter, keyboard focus, the empty and unknown states, reduced motion and the phone layout.
- **Not here:** Store Spy store pages (Phase 3) would make the palette's store row open the store; a store or supplier picker in the filter bar (Product Screener, Phase 4).

### DEC-306 · Phase 4 scores contract: one append-only `scores` row per (product, day, kind, model_version), a typed explanation in `pulso_core.scores`, the FEATURE_VERSION hash; every other Phase 4 table lands with its own feature task
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p4-01)** · Phase 4 serial-first track "Migration + score/explanation contract"; SPEC §5 `scores`, §7; DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-041, DEC-047, DEC-261, DEC-275, DEC-278; AGENTS.md §3; vault Intelligence/Score Definitions, Intelligence/Trend Score, Intelligence/Lifecycle Stage, Intelligence/Feature Store & Leakage Test; `docs/reports/p4-01.md`; migration 0016, no flag, dependency, endpoint or UI
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Table.** `scores` is global market data (no `workspace_id`): `product_id`, `date` (the UTC day), `kind`, `model_version`, `value numeric(5,1)` (0–100, NULL when every input is missing), `stage` (lifecycle only), `explanation jsonb`, `computed_at`, a bigint `id`. One row per kind, not SPEC §5's wide row: each score has its own config and so its own `model_version`, and a wide row would re-append every score when one score's weights change. SPEC §5's columns map to `kind IN ('trend', 'saturation', 'lifecycle', 'risk', 'refund_risk')`. Opportunity stays a workspace view (DEC-041), and velocity and acceleration stay in `product_daily_features`. Lifecycle is categorical: a CHECK keeps `value` NULL and `stage` one of the five stages on lifecycle rows, and `stage` NULL on every other kind.
  - **(b) Append-only.** UNIQUE `(kind, product_id, date, model_version)`, the brief's key with `kind` first so its backward scan serves (e). The 0005 function `reject_append_only_mutation()` rejects UPDATE and DELETE (row trigger) and TRUNCATE. The downgrade refuses while any row exists: scores are never backfilled, so dropped rows can't come back.
  - **(c) Explanation** (`pulso_core.scores`, Pydantic v2, frozen, extra keys refused). `Explanation`: `model_version`, `config_digest` (the full SHA-256), `components`, `missing` (the missing components' names, in order, validated against them) and `coverage` (present configured weight over all configured weight, DEC-040). `Component`: `name`, `family`, `source`, `weight` (configured), `weight_eff` (renormalised), `raw`, `z`, `contribution`, `as_of`, `missing`, `gap`, `ai_derived`, `prompt_version`. A missing input with `source` NULL reads "no source yet"; missing with a source reads "insufficient signal". `ai_derived` without a `prompt_version` is refused (DEC-047). A score task adds the fields only it needs (Trend's `normalization` and `penalties`, Lifecycle's rule and pending stage, Saturation's `panel_size`) as optional fields in its own PR; confidence (coverage × freshness) waits for MT-1.
  - **(d) Version.** `version(config)` gives `{revision:03d}-{sha256[:12]}` and the full digest of a frozen config dataclass with a `revision` field. That's `FEATURE_VERSION`'s rule (DEC-275 e), so any config change makes a new version and versions sort by revision. Not the vault's `<score>_v<major>-<hash8>`: the kind is its own column. The helper wasn't moved, so `pulso_ml` is untouched and `FEATURE_VERSION` stays `001-0754a5e6a014`.
  - **(e) Helpers.** `explain(config, components)` renormalises (DEC-040): a missing input gets `weight_eff` 0 and no contribution, and the present ones get `weight / Σ present weight`. It returns Σ `weight_eff × z` over the present inputs, or None when none is present. A present input without a `z` raises. `contribution` is `weight_eff × z`, in z units. Trend and Saturation map the sum onto 0–100 as 50 + 50 × sum / 3, so a component's points are 50/3 × its contribution. `write(session, rows)` is `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and returns the new-row count. `latest(kind)` is a `Select` of each product's newest row of that kind: its latest date, then newest `computed_at`, then `model_version`. Callers add filters and joins.
  - **(f) No single Phase 4 migration.** The vault's technical plan put every Phase 4 table in one migration. Instead each table comes with its own feature task, one migration per PR: `saved_screens` with WF-1, `watchlists` and `watchlist_items` with WF-2, `boards` and `board_items` with WF-3, `alert_rules` and `alerts` with WF-4, `tests` with WF-5. Each one rebases onto the head it finds.
  - **(g) FS-1.** `product_daily_features` already exists: migration 0005, `pulso_ml.features` (DEC-275), the `ml.features` job (DEC-278) and the truncation leakage test in the normal pytest run, so CI fails when a demand feature reads a future capture. What FS-1 still needs:
    - the non-demand score inputs through `AsOf`: R-18's `gt_slope` for SC-2 and seller counts and growth over a stable panel for SC-3, with Trends captures and the CSV backfill joining the leakage fixture;
    - per-family `gap_flags` (missed-run dates) that Data Health shows with the same dates;
    - the Beat slot, which waits for imp-284.
    Acceleration needs no column: Acc(t) = G7(t) − G7(t−7) reads two existing rows. SC-1 adds one only if a Screener filter needs it. Part (b) of the leakage test stays in Phase 6.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) SPEC §5's wide row: one `model_version` for five scores.
  - (b) A composite primary key: an alert or a Daily Pulse row couldn't point at one score.
  - (c) Contributions in points: the 0–100 mapping is score maths, which belongs to SC-2 and SC-3.
  - (c) `missing` as a computed field: an explanation read back with extra keys refused would fail.
  - (d) Moving the hash helper into `pulso_core`: two lines, against touching the leakage-guarded package.
  - (f) One Phase 4 migration: parallel tasks would share a migration none of them owns.
- **Why:** `packages/core/tests/test_scores.py` runs 16 tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`:
  - UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE are refused;
  - a re-run night inserts nothing (xmin unchanged), and a weights change appends;
  - a weights change makes a new `model_version`;
  - renormalisation with every input present, with one missing, and with all missing (value None, `missing` lists every input, coverage 0);
  - the explanation round-trips through JSON, and the DEC-047 and `missing` checks hold;
  - `latest` returns each product's newest row per kind, through a backward scan of the unique key;
  - five CHECK cases, and 0015 ↔ 0016 down and up, with the downgrade refused once a row exists.

  Eleven one-line mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/p4-01.md`).

### DEC-309 · A withheld landed margin on the Overview offers one next action, chosen by its gap; Settings opens the duty rate focused on arrival at #duty
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-368)** · Improvement backlog row imp-368; DEC-299, DEC-040, DEC-251, DEC-243; vault Features/Product Page - Overview, Features/Settings; `docs/reports/imp-368.md`; web only: no API, schema, migration, dependency or flag change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) One action per withheld state.** `products/[id]/overview-model.ts` `marginFix(missing)` checks the gaps in this order. `duty_not_set` gives "Set duty", a link to `/settings#duty`; it wins because the card's amber label is "Duty not set" whenever duty is a gap (DEC-299 c). No gaps at all (no settings row yet, DEC-299 b) gives "Open Settings", a link to `/settings`, whose first read creates the row. A missing supplier price or freight quote (`supplier_cost_missing`, `freight_missing`, `freight_variant_mismatch`) gives "Open Suppliers", which opens the Suppliers tab. It beats a missing price because without a supplier the Profit tab shows only "No supplier cost yet". Otherwise the action opens the Profit tab: "Enter a price" when `price_missing` is a gap, "Open Profit" for a USD rate or an out-of-range amount.
  - **(b) The control.** The card's last line uses the same ghost chevron button as "Where it appears", with its text aligned to the card's. A tab action goes through the page's `goTo`, so focus lands on the tab it opened. The card's states, its "never 0" rule and its as-of line are unchanged; `StatCard` gains an optional `action` slot after the reason.
  - **(c) Settings arrival.** On `/settings#duty`, the form's first draft has "Set a rate" chosen, and the empty Default duty rate box takes focus once. This is what the page's own "Set duty" check does. The form then reads "Unsaved changes" until a save or Reset, as it does after that check. A later refetch never reopens the rate, and an already-set duty is just focused.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a1) Following the API's first gap: a product with no supplier would send the owner to a Profit tab that has nothing to cost.
  - (a2) One action per gap: the brief asks for one.
  - (c1) Focusing the "Not set" radio and leaving the rate closed: one more keystroke, and the in-page check already opens it.
  - (c2) Opening the rate from an effect: the React Compiler lint (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`) rejects a setState in an effect. The existing adjust-during-render sync builds the first draft instead.
- **Why:** `overview-model.test.ts` covers the order: duty beside a supplier gap, no gaps, a supplier gap beside a price gap, variant mismatch, price, FX and amount. `overview.spec.ts` clicks each state's action once: Set duty lands on Settings with "Set a rate" checked and the rate box focused; Open Suppliers, by keyboard from the margin's number, has a solid focus outline and focuses the Suppliers tab; Enter a price focuses the Profit tab; Open Settings opens Settings. Each test failed before the change. `settings.spec.ts` still passes.
- **Not here:** focusing the Profit tab's sell price box (not the tab) after "Enter a price", and per-state next actions on the other cards (price, supplier cost, velocity). Both are backlog rows in the report.

### DEC-310 · Velocity & Acceleration (SC-1): a pure `family_velocity()` over one demand family's rows at t and t−7, each NULL with its reason (no source, insufficient history, or a gap naming its window), exact values kept through a tolerated gap, versioned through `pulso_core.scores.version`
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p4-03)** · Phase 4 task SC-1; SPEC §7; DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-261, DEC-275 (d, e), DEC-306 (d, g), DEC-307; vault Intelligence/Velocity & Acceleration, Intelligence/Demand Signals, Intelligence/Score Definitions §3 and §7; `docs/reports/p4-03.md`; no migration, table, endpoint, UI, flag, Beat or dependency change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Function.** `pulso_ml.scores.velocity.family_velocity(now, week_ago, days_observed)` is pure. It takes one product's `product_daily_features` rows in one family for t and t−7 (anything with `velocity_7d`, `growth_7d` and `has_gap`: a `Feature`, the ORM row or a `Demand`; None when there's no row) and `days_observed`, the days since the product's first row in that family with t included (DEC-307's input). It returns `Velocity(v7, g7, acc, has_gap)`, each a `Measure(value, why)`. V7 and G7 are the stored values (DEC-275 d: G7 = ln((V7(t) + 5) / (V7(t−7) + 5)) over the listings covered at t−14 and t). Acc(t) = G7(t) − G7(t−7) is the two rows' stored G7 subtracted, so no column and no new G7 code. Families never mix: the caller passes one family's rows. `shp_bump` has no feature code, so it always reads "no source".
  - **(b) Every NULL says why.** These are checked in order:
    - `no source`: there's no row at t. The family has no series for the product (DEC-275 e, DEC-040).
    - `insufficient history`: fewer days than the window needs. V7 needs 8 (t−7..t), G7 needs 15 (t−14..t) and Acc needs 22 (G7(t−7) reads t−21).
    - `gap <window>`: the history is long enough but the value is NULL. The window is `t-7..t` (V7), `t-14..t` (G7), or for Acc the NULL G7's: `t-14..t`, or `t-21..t-7` when G7(t−7) is NULL or its row is missing.
    Only the rows at t and t−7 are read, so nothing is interpolated or carried forward from a neighbouring day.
  - **(c) The gap rule is the stored one.** The rows already apply the vault's tolerance over their whole windows (DEC-275 d). A target-coverage gap over 3 days, or an uncovered window end, stores NULL. A shorter gap keeps the exact value, because inventory differencing spans it. `has_gap` is set when a value is NULL from a gap, or when either row's flag marks a tolerated gap behind a present V7. DEC-275 also flags a V7 that is NULL only from a short history, and that flag is not read as a gap, so a new product reads `insufficient history` with `has_gap` False.
    - **Ceiling:** the stable panel's size isn't on the row. A product whose every listing was replaced within 14 days has an empty G7 panel, and that reads as `gap t-14..t`, where a stored `panel_size` would read `insufficient history`. A test pins this. Storing the size needs the feature writer and a column or the `features` JSON (FS-1), so it's a backlog row.
  - **(d) Version.** A frozen `Config(revision=0, feature_version=FEATURE_VERSION)`. `VERSION, _ = pulso_core.scores.version(CONFIG)` (DEC-306 d) is now `000-b80f4a7f4a93`. A new G7 maths (a new `FEATURE_VERSION`) or a rule change (bump `revision`) is a new version, and `feature_version` names the rows the rules read.
- **Alternatives:**
  - **NULL on any gap flag, even when the stored value is present** (DEC-307's choice for a stage). A one-day outage would then blank every product's G7 for two weeks and its Acc for three. With a NULL G7 there's no Trend Score row (it's the required component). Strict rules on Acc would also need the t−14 row's flag. Velocity is data, not a judgement, and Score Definitions §3 already carries `gap` beside a present input.
  - **"NULL without a gap flag is insufficient history"** (DEC-307's order). A 4-day gap in t−21..t−15 leaves both rows unflagged with G7(t−7) NULL, so it would read as short history. So would the second of two listings missing t−7 exactly: both rows are unflagged, G7(t) is NULL, and V7(t) drops that listing.
  - **Recomputing from the pure demand inputs** (`product_demand` at t and t−7): that gives the panel size, but scores read the stored rows (Feature Store "Phase 4 scores read only from it"), and those inputs need an `AsOf` read per score.
  - **A `panel_size` helper lifted from `product_demand`'s stable-set closure:** nothing could call it until the writer stores the size, and `demand.py` is under p4-04's parallel FS-1 change.
- **Why:** `packages/ml/tests/test_velocity.py` has 25 cases. Gappy fixtures run through the real `product_demand` at t and t−7:
  - the 3 → 30 unit jump (G7 = ln(35/8) = 1.476, Acc = ln(35/8) − ln(8/6));
  - tolerated 3-day gaps in t−7..t and t−14..t−8 (exact values, flagged);
  - 4-day gaps in each of the three weeks, and uncovered ends at t, t−7, t−14 and t−21 (NULL with the window named);
  - no history (1 day and 10 days);
  - no source;
  - no row a week ago (with 21 and 22 days);
  - the 7/8, 14/15 and 21/22-day boundaries;
  - the vault lamp's Acc of 0.208;
  - a listing joining mid-window, which moves neither G7 nor Acc;
  - a second listing missing t−7 exactly, with both rows unflagged;
  - the emptied panel;
  - the version.
  21 one-line mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/p4-03.md`).
- **Not here:** storing `panel_size` (FS-1 backlog row), an acceleration column or Screener filter (WF-1), SC-2's components (`<fam>_g7`, `<fam>_acc`) and SC-6's nightly read, R-25 (Untrackable store reason: Shopify is off for good, DEC-261; backlog row).

### DEC-312 · Testing Board backend (WF-5a): `product_tests` with computed CPA/ROAS, a start snapshot that is the Profit calc taken once on entering Testing, and kill-reason counts over the workspace
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p4-05)** · Phase 4 task WF-5 part a, R-21, R-24; SPEC §5 `tests`, §6.11; DEC-040, DEC-246, DEC-265, DEC-287; vault Features/Testing Board, Features/Product Page - Profit; `docs/reports/p4-05.md`; migration 0017; no UI, flag or dependency
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Table `product_tests`** (SPEC §5's `tests`; the class is `ProductTest` so pytest never collects it, and the router is `routers/testing.py`). Workspace-owned, `BIGINT` identity id. Columns: `product_id`, `stage` (`idea` / `researching` / `testing` / `winner` / `killed`, default `idea`), `started_on` / `ended_on` (UTC dates), `currency` (`USD`), `ad_spend_minor`, `revenue_minor` (BIGINT, ≥ 0, default 0), `orders` (≥ 0), `notes`, `kill_reason`, `start_snapshot` (jsonb), `created_at` / `updated_at`. CPA and ROAS are not columns (SPEC §5 lists them; the vault and the brief compute them).
  - **(b) Kill reasons** are the vault's nine (`no_sales_over_stop_loss` … `other`), checked in SQL and as an API literal held equal by a test. A CHECK makes `kill_reason` non-null exactly when `stage = 'killed'`: the API answers 422 `kill_reason` for Killed without one or a reason on another stage, and leaving Killed clears it. No separate `kill_note`: free text goes in `notes`.
  - **(c) One open test per product** in a workspace (Idea, Researching or Testing): a partial unique index; a second one is the global 409 `conflict`.
  - **(d) Start snapshot (R-21).** When a test first enters Testing (POST or PATCH), the server runs the Profit calc (`calc_profit`, the same `landed_cost()` and workspace settings) and stores its whole answer plus `captured_at`. That covers the supplier listing and variant, quantity, method, DDP, the freight quote row (`freight_quote_id`, new on `ProfitCalc`), every line, landed cost, `margin_minor` (= break-even CPA), the duty, fee, returns and CPA rates, `estimates`, `sources`, `complete` and `missing`. An optional `scenario` (the Profit request without `product_id`) picks the supplier, variant, price or CPA; omitted, it's the default scenario. An incomplete calc is stored as is (`complete: false`, `missing` named) rather than refused. A `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger refuses any change to a set snapshot, PATCH has no snapshot field (422), and `scenario` after the snapshot is 422 `snapshot_locked`. Leaving and re-entering Testing keeps it.
  - **(e) Computed on read.** `cpa_minor = ad_spend_minor / orders`, half-even to the cent (DEC-246's rounding), null with 0 orders. `roas = revenue_minor / ad_spend_minor` as a float, null with 0 spend.
  - **(f) Stage moves.** Entering Testing sets `started_on` to today (UTC) when empty; entering Winner or Killed sets `ended_on` when empty; reopening from either clears `ended_on` unless the same PATCH sets it. `ended_on < started_on` is 422 `date_order` (also a CHECK). Any move is allowed, including Killed straight from Idea.
  - **(g) List.** `GET /v1/tests?stage=&cursor=&limit=` (≤ 100, default 50), newest id first, an integer keyset cursor. `kill_reasons` counts every killed test in the workspace by reason, whatever the filter or page (R-24).
- **Alternatives:**
  - **Table `tests`, class `Test`:** collides with pytest collection and with grep for the test folders.
  - **Snapshotting only price, landed cost and break-even CPA** (the vault's first list): R-21 asks for the variant, quote row and rates too, and the whole calc is what "changed since start" will compare against the current one.
  - **Refusing Testing while the economics are incomplete:** blocks the owner's real test for want of a duty rate. Storing the incomplete calc keeps the record honest; the re-snapshot endpoint (vault) is the way to replace it, with the UI task.
  - **Enforcing immutability only in the API:** the trigger also stops a script or a future endpoint from rewriting it by accident, like DEC-281's keyword trigger.
  - **A typed `start_snapshot` (ProfitCalc) in the response:** a later ProfitCalc field would make older snapshots fail validation; a JSON object stays readable.
- **Why:** `apps/api/tests/test_testing.py` (20 tests) and `packages/core/tests/test_product_tests_schema.py` on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie`: CPA and ROAS against hand calculations ($200.00 / 3 = $66.67, $48.65 / 2 = $24.32 half-even, ROAS 1.29995), null on 0 orders and 0 spend; Killed without a reason 422; the snapshot holds the chosen variant, the quote row id and the 20% duty, 2.9% + $0.30 fees and $18.00 CPA, with a $22.53 break-even CPA, and survives a fee change, a new supplier price, metrics and a stage round trip, while PATCH and SQL can't change it; kill-reason counts equal the killed tests across pages and filters; workspace B sees none of A's tests. 14 one-line mutations each fail a test (`docs/reports/p4-05.md`).
- **Not here (the board UI task):** the stop-loss and winner-candidate chips with k and N in Settings, "changed since start" in the drawer, `POST /v1/tests/{id}/snapshot-economics`, card ordering (`position`), product title and image on the list, CSV export, and Phase 7's auto-filled metrics.

### DEC-305 · Skeleton shimmer is offset by `left`, never a static `translate`; Data Health skeletons have a row per seeded source; panels hold a first-load error until both core calls settle
**2026-10-25 · proposed (imp-372)** · CI runs 40199989854 (`fe15473`), 40202530141 (`d9b73ef`), 40204846727 (`b3d082a`); DEC-203 (p1-53), DEC-289 (p3-05), DEC-303 (b); vault Features/Data Health; `docs/reports/imp-372.md`; web only: no API change, migration, dependency, flag or CI change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Shimmer offset.** The `Skeleton`'s sweep box (three bars wide, middle third lit, p1-53) starts two bars left with `left: -200%` instead of `left: -100%` plus a static `translate: -33.333%`. The sweep, the `transform`-only keyframe and the reduced-motion end state are unchanged.
  - **(b) Skeleton rows.** The Sources table, the Sources phone cards and the Coverage skeleton each show five rows, one per seeded source (Phase 1's four plus AliExpress, p3-05). A skeleton table row is as tall as a loaded row without a status note (47 px: the name bar is `h-8.5`). A skeleton card's name bar is 38 px (`h-9.5`), because the card's name is body size, outside `zone-data`. Below `sm`, the Open gaps skeleton heading is two lines (`max-sm:h-9`). A status note (blocked, overdue) still adds its line when the data lands.
  - **(c) Settling hold.** `useCoreSettling()` is true while neither core call (sources, host) has data and either is still in flight, so the page may yet become "Can't reach the API". While it holds, the host, backups, sources, coverage, Open gaps and import panels keep their loading skeletons instead of showing their own load error. When both core calls fail, the page shows the API-down card as before. When either core call has data, every panel loads and fails on its own as before. A paused (offline) fetch is not in flight, so offline behaviour is unchanged.
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Turning off the shimmer on the Coverage skeleton only: other skeletons below a panel that grows would still score. Animating `background-position`: AGENTS §8 animates `transform` and `opacity` only. Raising the CLS bound or retrying the test: ruled out by the task.
  - (b) Reserving every row at its tallest (57 px): loaded rows without a note would carry an empty line. Deriving the count before the data lands: nothing gives it before the sources call answers.
  - (c) Turning the page into "Can't reach the API" as soon as one core call fails without an answer: a single failed call would replace the whole page. Changing only the test: the panel errors really did flash, for about 3 s on CI's runner.
- **Why:** (a) Chrome's layout-shift attribution reported the shimmer `::after` of a moved Coverage skeleton bar as moving from `(0,0,0×0)` to its bar, so a real move of 18–28 px scored 0.137 per frame. With the same geometry offset by `left`, the same frames score 0.007. Turning the shimmer off also gives 0.0095, while keeping the `translate` on a bar-sized box still gives 0.099. (b) p3-05 removed the AliExpress "Planned" row. That lifted the Coverage skeleton 32 px into the 1440×900 window, where the move first counted. With the row put back, the worst landing order scored 0.007. (c) With the hold off, the new `retry-focus.spec.ts` case (the host call answers 1.5 s late after the press) records the Backups card's Retry while the page is still refreshing, and fails. The other 13 retry-focus cases are unchanged.

### DEC-311 · Feature store FS-1: `gt_interest` and `shp_sellers` rows with their inputs in `features`, every row's missed runs in `gap_flags`, migration 0018, feature version 002
**2026-10-25 · proposed (p4-04)** · Phase 4 task FS-1; R-18; review M15; DEC-031, DEC-040, DEC-060 (D4), DEC-247 (g, h), DEC-261, DEC-275, DEC-278, DEC-281, DEC-292, DEC-306 (g); AGENTS.md §3; vault Intelligence/Feature Store & Leakage Test, Intelligence/Trend Score, Intelligence/Saturation Score, Features/Data Health; `docs/reports/p4-04.md`; migration 0018 (on p4-05's 0017); no flag, dependency, endpoint, UI, Beat or nightly-chain change
- **Decision:**
  - **(a) Layout.** Migration 0018 adds nullable `features` and `gap_flags` jsonb columns to `product_daily_features`: the vault's hybrid layout, typed columns for filters and jsonb for the rest. The two new inputs come as two new families, one row each per (product, day, version): `gt_interest` and `shp_sellers`. Their `velocity_7d` and `growth_7d` are NULL, and their inputs sit in `features` under source-prefixed keys. Demand rows keep their columns and carry `features` NULL. Discover's `best_feature` and the Trends tab already filter by family, so they don't see the new rows. `Config.revision` goes to 2, so `FEATURE_VERSION` becomes `002-<hash>`. Rows from version 001 stay, with NULL in both new columns. The downgrade refuses while any row holds gap flags.
  - **(b) `gt_slope` (R-18).**
    - **Keywords:** the product's `product_keywords` rows with status `confirmed` and `decided_at` before the cutoff, from any workspace. The weekly Trends set reads them the same way (DEC-292).
    - **Captures:** US `search_trends` rows with `captured_at` before the cutoff, grouped by raw payload. A capture is either SerpApi's 90-day answer or a manual CSV import. Keywords match casefolded with spacing collapsed, and the newest parse of a capture wins.
    - **Inside one capture:** partial points are dropped. L is the last complete day. The last 4 complete weeks are L−27..L and the 8 before are L−83..L−28: 7-day blocks counted back from L, not calendar weeks. Every one of the 84 days must be there, or the slope is None. The slope is ln((m4 + 1) / (m8 + 1)), where m4 and m8 are those blocks' means.
    - **Per keyword:** the newest capture known by the cutoff that holds 12 complete weeks. A newer capture that doesn't is skipped, never stitched onto an older one: an empty answer, or a short or weekly-spaced CSV backfill.
    - **Per product:** `gt_slope` is the median of its keywords' slopes. `gt_keywords` counts the keywords that have one, and `gt_as_of` is the oldest used capture's `captured_at`.
    - **Rows:** a product gets a row when it has a member listing at the cutoff and one of its keywords has a capture. `coverage` is the keywords with a slope over the keywords with a capture, and `has_gap` means no slope.
    - **Ceilings:** a later decision rewrites a keyword's row (DEC-281), so a re-decided keyword drops out of earlier days: missing, never leaked. A merged-away product's keywords aren't carried to the survivor; only the survivor's own confirmations count.
  - **(c) Sellers (Saturation).**
    - **Listings:** store listings (`store_id` set) placed on the product by membership at cutoff(t). A listing is live at a cutoff c when `first_seen_at < c` and it wasn't delisted before c. That is the Sellers tab series rule (DEC-247 h).
    - **`shp_sellers`:** distinct stores with a live member listing at cutoff(t), counting every tracked store.
    - **Panel:** stores with `first_seen_at` before cutoff(t−28), which is DEC-247 (g) at day grain.
    - **`shp_seller_g28`:** ln((p_t + 1) / (p_t−28 + 1)), where p counts the panel stores with a live member listing. Both ends use membership at cutoff(t), as G7's stable sets do, so a merge can't read as growth. It is None while the panel is empty.
    - **Stored with it:** `panel_size` is the panel's store count, `coverage` is panel stores over tracked stores, and `has_gap` means no growth.
    - **Rows:** a product gets a row when it has a member store listing first seen by the cutoff. With the crawler off (DEC-261), only CSV imports and old history feed it.
    - **Ceiling:** a live crawl that relisted a listing reset its `delisted_at`, and that history isn't rebuilt (DEC-247 h). No live crawl runs any more.
  - **(d) `gap_flags`.** Every new-version row carries `{"missed_runs": [ISO dates]}`.
    - **Which slots:** the UTC dates touched by `collection_gaps` slots of the family's source, with `detected_at` before the cutoff, within t−28..t. `cj_stock` reads `cj`; `shp_inv` and `shp_sellers` read `shopify_stores`; `gt_interest` reads `google_trends`. Days expand as `source_daily_coverage` expands them.
    - **Fills:** a fill doesn't clear a missed run (DEC-060 D4). Data Health's heatmap shows a healed day as `filled` and its Open gaps list drops it; the feature keeps the date.
    - **Late detection:** the audit records a missed slot when the host wakes. A gap detected after day t's cutoff appears from the next day's row on. Day t's own demand row already carries `has_gap` from target coverage.
    - **Existing columns:** `has_gap` and `coverage` keep their DEC-275 meaning for demand rows.
  - **(e) Leakage fixture.** `build()` is unchanged, so the Discover momentum end-to-end test keeps its fixture. A new `build_inputs()` adds:
    - confirmed keywords: P1 on day 0, P6 on day 25, P3 on day 44;
    - SerpApi captures on Mondays 13, 20 and 41, each ending in a partial point. Day 18's spike rescales the captures from day 20 against day 13's. Mondays 27 and 34 were missed;
    - a day-45 CSV: 30 days for P1's keyword and 86 for P3's;
    - four stores (two old, one first seen on day 10, one from the day-45 CSV import) and five store listings, one delisted on day 38;
    - gaps for `cj` day 30, `shopify_stores` days 30–34 and `google_trends` days 27 and 34, each detected at 00:05 the next day.

    The truncated copy also deletes Trends rows captured at or after the cutoff, gaps detected at or after it, and keyword rows decided at or after it, and it clears later delistings. The comparison now runs on 11 dates (30 and 44 added).
- **Alternatives:**
  - (a) Typed columns per input: a migration for every new input. One product-level row instead of one per family: it changes 0005's key and every reader.
  - (b) The newest capture by `captured_at` even when it holds no slope (the vault's literal "latest capture"): a one-off backfill import would blank `gt_slope` until the next Monday. SerpApi captures only, as `series_as_of` reads them: the CSV fallback (AGENTS.md §3) would never feed Trend. Calendar weeks: a 90-day answer minus its partial day doesn't always hold 12 whole ISO weeks. The mean over keywords: one noisy keyword moves it more.
  - (c) Liveness from snapshot dates: a second rule beside the Sellers tab's. Membership at each end's own cutoff: a merge would read as seller growth. A workspace's tracked-store list as the panel: workspace-owned, and scores are global market data.
  - (d) Honouring fills: a fill would rewrite what a past feature knew (D4). Gaps by slot time instead of `detected_at`: reads evidence written after the cutoff.
- **Why:** pure tests (`packages/ml/tests/test_market.py`) and Postgres tests on `pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18-trixie` (`test_leakage.py`, `packages/core/tests/test_phase2_schema.py`):
  - R-18 maths: a partial point excluded, a missing day, fewer than 84 days;
  - the day-13, day-20 and day-41 captures each read alone, and day 20's slope differs from a stitched series;
  - the 30-day CSV doesn't replace day 41's capture, and P3's first capture is the CSV;
  - a keyword decided later, and a merged-away product;
  - seller level, growth and panel on four dates; adding 10 newly tracked stores leaves P1's growth and panel size and P2's whole row unchanged;
  - every day-45 row's `missed_runs` equals `source_daily_coverage`'s gap days, and day 30's row can't see day 30's late-detected gap;
  - the 0018 downgrade refused once a row holds flags.

  Twenty one-line mutations each fail a test, and so does removing the downgrade guard (`docs/reports/p4-04.md`). Six of them read past the cutoff. The truncation test fails for five: future captures, captures cut by `observed_for`, later keyword decisions, later-detected gaps and later delistings. The seller test catches the sixth, stores first seen after the cutoff, which the truncated copy keeps because their listings still reference them.
- **Not here:** SC-1, SC-2 and SC-3's maths, the explanation fields, the Beat slot (imp-284), CJ listing counts as a saturation input (open in the vault), and `ads_*` (no source yet).
